# Personal postal tea: agricultural import audit

**Updated 30 September 2026 — 203 enabled destinations; review is incomplete.**

[Australia](#au) allows this plain finished green tea. The full destination list cannot yet be described as cleared: some routes need permits or certificates, and other countries’ published rules leave a specific question unresolved. Each destination is assessed below.

Sealed 100 g retail pouches of plain Japanese steamed/dried Camellia sinensis sencha, sent from Japan by post to individuals for their own consumption. No live plants, fresh leaves, seeds, herbal mixtures, dairy, extracts, resale, traveller-baggage or gift-only assumptions.

**Store constraint:** The volunteer store will not obtain permits, phytosanitary/health/origin certificates, or special regulatory approvals. Routine postal/customs declarations and recipient arrival declarations are distinguished from obtaining certification. Mandatory separate agency registration or advance notice is stated explicitly rather than called a certificate.

The agricultural finding and the no-special-documents operating decision are separate. An agricultural exemption does not waive another mandatory food-import document. An unresolved finding is not a legal prohibition and is not counted as a completed clearance.

| No-special-documents review | Destinations |
|---|---:|
| No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions | 113 |
| Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint | 69 |
| No-special-documents route unresolved | 21 |
| No-special-documents review still in progress | 0 |

| Finding | Destinations |
|---|---:|
| Allowed under the stated conditions | 76 |
| Conditional personal route | 42 |
| Permit / documented clearance route | 31 |
| Phytosanitary certificate route | 10 |
| Unresolved — no verified import route | 43 |
| Domestic shipment | 1 |

The findings are based on agricultural import rules for the actual finished product and postal channel. Passenger allowances and live-plant rules are used only when the source also establishes their applicability here. The earlier same-date audit is superseded.

## Current checkout: quantity mismatches

These orders exceed the verified personal-use or certificate-exemption route. Larger routes requiring permits or certificates are unavailable under the store constraint. No live shipping settings were changed.

| Destination | Current maximum | Reviewed threshold | Required action |
|---|---:|---|---|
| [Micronesia (FM)](#fm) | 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea | 2 kg | Reduce cap to the supported no-special-documents route and enforce its other conditions. |
| [Iraq (IQ)](#iq) | 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea | 10 kg; weight basis unresolved (82 pouches if packaging is counted) | Exclude under the store constraint; reducing quantity alone does not remove the required regulatory step. |
| [Mauritania (MR)](#mr) | 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea | 10 kg; weight basis unresolved (82 pouches if packaging is counted) | Reduce cap to the supported no-special-documents route and enforce its other conditions. |
| [Panama (PA)](#pa) | 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea | 5 kg | Exclude under the store constraint; reducing quantity alone does not remove the required regulatory step. |
| [Solomon Islands (SB)](#sb) | 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea | 1 kg | Reduce cap to the supported no-special-documents route and enforce its other conditions. |

The country entries also show thresholds that checkout already respects. The calculations check weight and explicit pouch-count limits; monetary and annual/frequency conditions are not validated by a weight cap. Singapore’s S$100 total-value condition, for example, still requires an order-value check.

## How to use the decisions

- **Allowed under the stated conditions:** Official evidence supports this finished product and import channel without a plant permit or phytosanitary certificate. Packaging, declaration, food-quantity/value limits and inspection powers still apply where stated.
- **Conditional personal route:** A supported personal import route has specific quantity, packaging, inspection, notification or geographic conditions. Resolve the listed conditions before dispatch.
- **Permit / documented clearance route:** Published evidence establishes a permit, approval or documented clearance route. This is not permission already obtained. A possible processing exemption must be confirmed before omitting documents.
- **Phytosanitary certificate route:** Published evidence requires a phytosanitary certificate for the applicable route. Any claimed processing exemption needs the specific confirmation identified in the record.
- **Unresolved — no verified import route:** The research identified a material gap in the finished-tea classification, postal applicability, current rule or required documents. Not a finding that tea is prohibited; do not treat this destination as cleared.
- **Domestic shipment:** Japan-origin shipment within Japan; no international agricultural import question.

A missing numeric limit is not an unlimited personal allowance. Use truthful contents, origin, net/gross weights and sale value, and retain manufacturer packaging. The report does not establish compliance with every separate food-labelling, organic-claim, tax or carrier requirement.

## Shared rules

### EU-TEA

The common EU plant-health regime exempts finished dried tea from phytosanitary certification. Swedish guidance expressly covers private orders; Finnish guidance confirms processed tea is outside plant import controls. This applies to the 27 EU destinations and Åland below. Fresh leaves, live plants and herbal mixtures need separate assessment. Overseas territories are assessed separately.

[jordbruksverket.se 1](<https://jordbruksverket.se/languages/english/swedish-board-of-agriculture/plants/bringing-in-or-ordering-plants-and-plant-products-to-sweden-as-a-private-individual>) · [ruokavirasto.fi 2](<https://www.ruokavirasto.fi/en/themes/import-and-export/import/plants-and-plant-products/>)

### EU-PRIVATE-ORGANIC

Austrian Customs guidance VB-0240 section 2.4(b), explaining EU organic import rules, expressly excludes personal/private imports sent by post. A private recipient consuming the tea does not need a commercial organic certificate of inspection. Routine customs declarations remain necessary.

[findok.bmf.gv.at 1](<https://findok.bmf.gv.at/findok/iwg/80/80664/80664.3.pdf>)

### MAFF-POSTAL

Japan MAFF’s 21 April 2026 postal matrix has a specific manufactured-green-tea column. Q means phytosanitary certificate; P means destination import permit; ◎ means no phytosanitary certificate. Footnote 11 notes that some cases fall outside plant quarantine and then need neither document; it does not identify which cases qualify. Confirm applicability with the destination. This reference guidance does not waive other laws.

[maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

Selected postal matrix cells: AE Q*11, BH PQ*11, KW PQ*11, OM PQ*11, QA PQ*11, SA PQ*11, BN PQ*11, LK PQ*11, PK PQ*11, TH Q, ID Q, IN Q, VN Q, PH ◎, CL ◎, MX ◎.

## Country findings

<a id="ad"></a>
### Andorra (AD)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Article 29 of the consolidated food-activities regulation requires an origin health certificate for non-animal foods imported from a non-EU country. Its definitions include manufactured foods and its scope covers imported foods generally; no small personal-post exemption is stated. This requirement alone conflicts with the store’s no-certificates policy.

**Document-rule sources:** [portaljuridicandorra.ad 1](<https://www.portaljuridicandorra.ad/R20000712E>)

Andorra food-activities regulation Article 29 requires an origin health certificate for non-animal foods arriving from outside the EU. Articles 2 and 5 include imported and processed foods. This documentary requirement applies to the Japanese finished-tea product independently of the unresolved plant-health classification.

**Personal post:** The food import provision is general and contains no personal, small-quantity or postal exception; commercial sale to a consumer is not exempted.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No certificate-free personal-post exception was established; do not use this destination under the store’s no-special-documents policy.

**Sources:** [portaljuridicandorra.ad 1](<https://www.portaljuridicandorra.ad/R20130612A_1>) · [portaljuridicandorra.ad 2](<https://www.portaljuridicandorra.ad/R20210203A>) · [portaljuridicandorra.ad 3](<https://www.portaljuridicandorra.ad/R20000712E>)

**Research trail:** Read the plant-product definitions and certificate annex in the official phytosanitary regulation. Searched Andorran agriculture and customs guidance for tea and private postal imports; did not establish a finished-tea exception. Read the full consolidated food-activities regulation, including scope, processed-food definition, Article 29 health-certificate requirement and the complete text for personal-use exemptions.

<a id="ae"></a>
### United Arab Emirates (AE)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Not supported under the affirmative Article 1 dead-plant-part / agricultural-consignment categories and Article 5(2) certificate duty, applied conservatively to the actual physical sencha leaves. ISPM 32 processing evidence and the risk-qualified manufactured-product definition support a contrary contextual construction, but do not expressly displace that independent category. The decision is not based merely on failure to find an exemption.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 2](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/3995>) · [uaecabinet.ae 3](<https://uaecabinet.ae/ar/news/minister-of-environment-issues-ministerial-resolution-on-control-of-imported-food-for-non-commercial-purposes>) · [moccae.gov.ae 4](<https://www.moccae.gov.ae/ar/services/release-of-agricultural-products-consignment>) · [jetro.go.jp 5](<https://www.jetro.go.jp/world/middle_east/ae/foods/exportguide/greentea.html>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 6](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/1160/download>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=3294&menuid=28&reporeid=110>) · [dm.gov.ae 8](<https://www.dm.gov.ae/documents/federal-law-no-7-regarding-agricultural-quarantine/>) · [dmpmedia.dm.gov.ae 9](<https://dmpmedia.dm.gov.ae/uploads/2026/07/قانون-اتحادي-رقم-7-لسنة-2025-بشأن-الحجر-الزراعي.pdf>) · [portal.sharjahcustoms.gov.ae 10](<https://portal.sharjahcustoms.gov.ae/en/tools/tariff-search>) · [icp.gov.ae 11](<https://icp.gov.ae/en/central-customs-tariff-system/>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 12](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2710/regulations>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 13](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2710/download>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **Q*11**. Conservative no-document exclusion on an explicit reading of Federal Law No. (7) of 2025. Article 1 includes live or dead plants and their parts; agricultural consignment separately includes plants. These manufactured sencha leaves remain physical nonliving plant parts under that broad limb, and Article 5(2) requires an exporting-country-authenticated phytosanitary certificate for every imported agricultural consignment. No further commodity designation is needed for this certificate duty. The separate manufactured-product limb is pest-risk qualified, so a specific-over-general contextual interpretation could exclude adequately manufactured tea; this material counterargument is preserved in definitionReview. This is a moderate legal-scope inference, not a tea-specific UAE ruling or an assertion of proven pest risk.

**Personal post:** Article 5(2) applies to every imported agricultural consignment without a commercial-sale, business-registration, baggage or quantity limitation. Article 1 importer includes natural persons and consignees. Japan MAFF's 21 April 2026 postal matrix expressly includes manufactured green tea and lists UAE Q*11. The separate personal-food guidance includes unaccompanied shipments and personal-food registration is exempt; no independent food-registration barrier is asserted.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 2](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/3995>) · [uaecabinet.ae 3](<https://uaecabinet.ae/ar/news/minister-of-environment-issues-ministerial-resolution-on-control-of-imported-food-for-non-commercial-purposes>) · [moccae.gov.ae 4](<https://www.moccae.gov.ae/ar/services/release-of-agricultural-products-consignment>) · [jetro.go.jp 5](<https://www.jetro.go.jp/world/middle_east/ae/foods/exportguide/greentea.html>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 6](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/1160/download>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=3294&menuid=28&reporeid=110>) · [dm.gov.ae 8](<https://www.dm.gov.ae/documents/federal-law-no-7-regarding-agricultural-quarantine/>) · [dmpmedia.dm.gov.ae 9](<https://dmpmedia.dm.gov.ae/uploads/2026/07/قانون-اتحادي-رقم-7-لسنة-2025-بشأن-الحجر-الزراعي.pdf>) · [portal.sharjahcustoms.gov.ae 10](<https://portal.sharjahcustoms.gov.ae/en/tools/tariff-search>) · [icp.gov.ae 11](<https://icp.gov.ae/en/central-customs-tariff-system/>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 12](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2710/regulations>) · [uaelegislation.gov.ae 13](<https://uaelegislation.gov.ae/en/legislations/2710/download>) · [dubaicustoms.gov.ae 14](<https://www.dubaicustoms.gov.ae/en/eServices/Pages/HScodesearch.aspx>) · [mirsal2new.dubaitrade.ae 15](<https://mirsal2new.dubaitrade.ae/eMirsal/hsCodeSetup.do>) · [adcustoms.gov.ae 16](<https://www.adcustoms.gov.ae/en/Policies-and-Regulations/Restricted-Banned-Commodities>) · [tamm.abudhabi 17](<https://www.tamm.abudhabi/wb/adc/smart-classification-system?lang=en>) · [dubaicustoms.gov.ae 18](<https://www.dubaicustoms.gov.ae/en/PoliciesAndNotices/Documents/IntegratedCustomsTariffv4.pdf>)

<a id="af"></a>
### Afghanistan (AF)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The recovered full operative 2025 law supplies an independent food-permission barrier. Article 22(1) prohibits importing food without licence or official permission;22(2) and 23(1) expressly reach companies and persons, with 23 covering official and unofficial routes. Article 2(13) defines official permission as a Health/Agriculture registered proforma/product-registration certificate, distinct from ordinary Customs declarations. Applying the unqualified 22–23 rule to a 100 g personal parcel is a stated conservative scope inference. Article 21(1) separately limits its dual trading-licence and Health-permit clause to commercial purposes; no express personal-food waiver from 22–23 was found. Agricultural processed-product classification remains unverified.

**Document-rule sources:** [mail.gov.af 1](<https://www.mail.gov.af/en/law-documents>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/as/afghanistan/index.html>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/afg183835.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 4](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/af3-2019-2516pqreg_de.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 5](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/einfuhrbestimmungen-pflanzengesundheit-afghanistan-1-187.html>) · [nvwa.nl 6](<https://www.nvwa.nl/site/binaries/site-content/collections/documents/fytosanitair/landeneisen/diverse-producten/afghanistan-diverse-producten-exporteisen/afghanistan-diverse-producten-exporteisen-1-3.pdf>) · [moj.gov.af 7](<https://www.moj.gov.af/dr/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%AC%D9%84%D9%88%DA%AF%DB%8C%D8%B1%DB%8C-%D8%A7%D8%B2-%D9%82%D8%A7%DA%86%D8%A7%D9%82-%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%AF-%D8%BA%D8%B0%D8%A7%DB%8C%DB%8C%D8%8C-%D8%A7%D8%AF%D9%88%DB%8C%D9%87-%D9%88-%D9%85%D8%AD%D8%B5%D9%88%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%B5%D8%AD%DB%8C-%D8%AA%D9%88%D8%B4%DB%8C%D8%AD-%D9%88-%D8%AF%D8%B1-%D8%AC%D8%B1%DB%8C%D8%AF%D9%87-%D8%B1%D8%B3%D9%85%DB%8C-%D9%86%D8%B4%D8%B1-%DA%AF%D8%B1%D8%AF%DB%8C%D8%AF>) · [afda.gov.af 8](<https://www.afda.gov.af/dr/node/1732>) · [laws.moj.gov.af 9](<http://laws.moj.gov.af/uploadedfiles/OG_01472.pdf>) · [laws.moj.gov.af 10](<http://laws.moj.gov.af/ShowLawPersian.aspx>) · [moj.gov.af 11](<https://moj.gov.af/dr/node/4263>)

Agricultural classification of finished sencha remains unresolved. Independently, the enacted 2025 Food/Medicine Anti-Smuggling Law, Articles 22–23, prohibits food import without licence or official permission and expressly covers companies and persons. The no-special-document route is excluded by a conservative inference from that unqualified rule; this is not a blanket tea prohibition.

**Personal post:** Articles 22–23 contain no business, resale, minimum-quantity or mode-of-carriage limit; Article 4 includes unlicensed importers and Article 23 names official and unofficial routes. Applying those provisions to a personal purchased postal parcel is an explicit conservative legal-scope inference. Article 21(1)’s separate trade-licence plus Health-permit duty is expressly commercial and is not treated as universal.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No unresolved question prevents the conservative no-special-document exclusion under the independent unqualified 2025 food-permission provisions. A published personal-food exemption or authoritative narrower application of Articles 22–23 could change it. Agricultural classification of finished sencha and current applicability of older agricultural provisions remain unresolved; no agricultural approval/prohibition is inferred from the food law.

**Sources:** [mail.gov.af 1](<https://www.mail.gov.af/en/law-documents>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/as/afghanistan/index.html>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/afg183835.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 4](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/af3-2019-2516pqreg_de.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 5](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/einfuhrbestimmungen-pflanzengesundheit-afghanistan-1-187.html>) · [nvwa.nl 6](<https://www.nvwa.nl/site/binaries/site-content/collections/documents/fytosanitair/landeneisen/diverse-producten/afghanistan-diverse-producten-exporteisen/afghanistan-diverse-producten-exporteisen-1-3.pdf>) · [moj.gov.af 7](<https://www.moj.gov.af/dr/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%AC%D9%84%D9%88%DA%AF%DB%8C%D8%B1%DB%8C-%D8%A7%D8%B2-%D9%82%D8%A7%DA%86%D8%A7%D9%82-%D9%85%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%AF-%D8%BA%D8%B0%D8%A7%DB%8C%DB%8C%D8%8C-%D8%A7%D8%AF%D9%88%DB%8C%D9%87-%D9%88-%D9%85%D8%AD%D8%B5%D9%88%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%B5%D8%AD%DB%8C-%D8%AA%D9%88%D8%B4%DB%8C%D8%AD-%D9%88-%D8%AF%D8%B1-%D8%AC%D8%B1%DB%8C%D8%AF%D9%87-%D8%B1%D8%B3%D9%85%DB%8C-%D9%86%D8%B4%D8%B1-%DA%AF%D8%B1%D8%AF%DB%8C%D8%AF>) · [afda.gov.af 8](<https://www.afda.gov.af/dr/node/1732>) · [laws.moj.gov.af 9](<http://laws.moj.gov.af/uploadedfiles/OG_01472.pdf>) · [laws.moj.gov.af 10](<http://laws.moj.gov.af/ShowLawPersian.aspx>) · [moj.gov.af 11](<https://moj.gov.af/dr/node/4263>)

**Research trail:** Checked the MAFF Afghanistan NPPO directory and followed the agriculture authority. Read the MAIL law directory; plant quarantine law and regulation entries have no usable linked texts. Searched official sources for tea, postal/personal use and plant quarantine in English and Dari; no current tea-specific rule established. Obtained the enacted 21 January 2017 Gazette 1243 law from FAOLEX; visually checked Article 3(7), PDF page 10, because embedded-text encoding is damaged. Its manufactured-product definition depends on pest risk. Read the German NPPO-hosted translation of the 2019 regulation, Articles 6, 9 and 22. The named up-to-2-kg personal permit exemption does not include tea. Checked the Dutch NPPO miscellaneous-product sheet revised February 2026; it has no tea classification and expressly does not establish unspecified products’ certification status. Did not convert a general regulated-product rule into a tea-specific prohibition. 30 September independent primary-law check: visually read 2017 Act Article 11 on PDF p.32 (printed p.30). It permits the authority to exempt certain importers for pest-risk analysis/personal use; it does not itself exempt every personal import. The 2019 regulation expressly covers trade or other purposes and consumption, but incorporates the Act’s pest-risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. Retained unresolved because that risk classification remains genuinely undecided. 30 September bounded independent-food follow-up: Read the Justice Ministry official indexed publication notice for the 2025 anti-smuggling law, Gazette 1472 (34 articles), and AFDA January 2026 implementation news. The notice expressly qualifies trade/import licences by importing for trade; it does not settle the scope of its separate document-processing clause for a private food parcel. Direct official article retrieval timed out and the public Gazette PDF was not recovered from the available links/search results. Retained unresolved instead of assuming either a commercial licence for every personal parcel or an unverified personal exemption. 30 September fresh official online recovery: HTTPS legal-database access failed, but the exact HTTP link published by Justice worked. Submitted only the public food-law text search and public download control; no login or contact. Retrieved Gazette 1472 (86 pages,1,447,130 bytes), verified direct official PDF hash identical, extracted all text and visually checked key Articles 4,21–22. Reviewed definition 2(13), scope 4, Customs 8, transport 18, commercial-specific 21, general 22–23 and commencement/repeal 34. Saved fullPDF, text, searchHTML, provenance, key-page renders and careful scope comparison in official-online-recovery-2026-09-30/asia/. Proposed no-doc exclusion on the separately unqualified 22–23, explicitly retaining 21’s commercial caveat; agricultural status remains not_verified.

<a id="ag"></a>
### Antigua and Barbuda (AG)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

No special documents supported for the stated manufactured tea on a combined-source inference: the local commodity schedule regulates Categories 2–4, current/signed PPU guidance limits documents to regulated goods, and the current-linked ISPM 32 illustration places tea in Category 1. Plant Protection Act 2012 section 2 is risk-qualified for manufactured products, consistent with this reading. Appendix 2 is illustrative/non-prescriptive and is not used alone to override local law. The 2014 broad leaflet is disclosed but weighed against the newer 2021/current PPU scope.

**Document-rule sources:** [agriculture.gov.ag 1](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/index.php/plant-protection/>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 2](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Import-Status-of-Plant-Commodities-and-Regulated-Items-PLANT-PROTECTION-ANTIGUA-AND-BARBUDA-ver.-27082020.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 3](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/cap-353.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 4](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/cap-211.pdf>) · [health.gov.ag 5](<https://health.gov.ag/guidelines/>) · [ippc.int 6](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 7](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 8](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2021/09/Plant-Protection-Import-Guidelines-ver-17062021.pdf>) · [ippc.int 9](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/antigua-and-barbuda/reportingobligation/2014/05/import-export-guide/>) · [assets.ippc.int 10](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2014/05/14/import_export_information_guide.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 11](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/import-export-information-guide.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 12](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/a2012-18.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 13](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ant126404.pdf>) · [health.gov.ag 14](<https://health.gov.ag/laws-regulations/>)

Supported by a combined domestic-category and commodity-classification inference for the stated manufactured plain tea. The PPU’s 27 August 2020 all-country commodity table applies permit/phytosanitary requirements to Categories 2–4. Its signed 17 June 2021 guidance and current website require documents for regulated plant commodities and describe processed items that still carry pests; the PPU expressly operates within IPPC standards. Current-linked ISPM 32 section 2 and Appendix 2 identify tea as a Category 1 example. This is a reasoned application of the domestic scheme, not a freestanding international exemption. A broad older leaflet requiring certificates for non-regulated items was recovered in an IPPC report dated 14 May 2014; the ministry copy uploaded in 2022 changes only its contact email. Newer signed/current guidance supplies the operative narrower scope. Separate enacted food laws reviewed do not impose a blanket private-tea origin certificate.

**Personal post:** Product-scope inference applies to a clean sealed 100 g manufactured Camellia tea packet bought for personal use and sent by post; it is not a traveler or gift allowance. Ordinary customs declaration and arrival inspection remain.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [agriculture.gov.ag 1](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/index.php/plant-protection/>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 2](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Import-Status-of-Plant-Commodities-and-Regulated-Items-PLANT-PROTECTION-ANTIGUA-AND-BARBUDA-ver.-27082020.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 3](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/cap-353.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 4](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/cap-211.pdf>) · [health.gov.ag 5](<https://health.gov.ag/guidelines/>) · [ippc.int 6](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 7](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 8](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2021/09/Plant-Protection-Import-Guidelines-ver-17062021.pdf>) · [ippc.int 9](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/antigua-and-barbuda/reportingobligation/2014/05/import-export-guide/>) · [assets.ippc.int 10](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2014/05/14/import_export_information_guide.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ag 11](<https://agriculture.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/import-export-information-guide.pdf>) · [laws.gov.ag 12](<https://laws.gov.ag/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/a2012-18.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 13](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ant126404.pdf>) · [health.gov.ag 14](<https://health.gov.ag/laws-regulations/>)

<a id="al"></a>
### Albania (AL)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The current implementing certificate schedule controls fresh tea leaves only; sealed steamed/dried sencha does not fit that regulated commodity. This rule applies to import consignments generally, including private postal orders. Ordinary customs declarations remain required.

**Document-rule sources:** [aku.gov.al 1](<https://aku.gov.al/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/FZ-2025-97.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/eu/albania/index.html>) · [dogana.gov.al 3](<https://www.dogana.gov.al/c/226/blerjet-online>)

Legal classification inference: Order 461 of 12 May 2025 replaced the certificate commodity annex under Order 113/2022. Annex IX Part B lists ex 090210/ex 090220 only as fresh, whole, uncut, unfermented green tea. Manufactured steamed/dried retail sencha is outside that description.

**Personal post:** The commodity-based certificate schedule governs imports generally, so this conclusion applies to personal postal tea and does not rely on a baggage allowance.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Sources:** [aku.gov.al 1](<https://aku.gov.al/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/FZ-2025-97.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/eu/albania/index.html>) · [dogana.gov.al 3](<https://www.dogana.gov.al/c/226/blerjet-online>)

<a id="ar"></a>
### Argentina (AR)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for factory-steamed and artificially dehydrated retail sencha: category 1 processed plant parts require no phytosanitary measures/certificate; personal-food authorization abolished by 3280/2025. This does not cover raw leaves or naturally air-dried unmanufactured plants.

**Document-rule sources:** [argentina.gob.ar 1](<https://www.argentina.gob.ar/normativa/nacional/resoluci%C3%B3n-12-2021-346384/actualizacion>) · [argentina.gob.ar 2](<https://www.argentina.gob.ar/node/465653>) · [boletinoficial.gob.ar 3](<https://www.boletinoficial.gob.ar/detalleAviso/primera/345309/20260803>)

Resolution 12/2021 AnnexVI makes industrially dehydrated plants/plant parts category 1, without phytosanitary measures or certification. It expressly distinguishes artificial/industrial dehydration from natural drying (category 2). Factory-steamed and artificially dried sencha fits the category 1 processing definition. ANMAT 3280/2025 removes the personal-food import authorization; the August 2026 transfer of food supervision to SENASA does not reinstate it.

**Personal post:** Processing-based agricultural exemption applies to imports from third countries, including Japan, without restricting transport to baggage. Personal-food authorization repeal is for direct users and personal use. Normal postal/customs import procedures remain.

**Checkout:** 3 pouches / 0.3 kg tea; highest carrier band 0.44 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [argentina.gob.ar 1](<https://www.argentina.gob.ar/normativa/nacional/resoluci%C3%B3n-12-2021-346384/actualizacion>) · [argentina.gob.ar 2](<https://www.argentina.gob.ar/node/465653>) · [boletinoficial.gob.ar 3](<https://www.boletinoficial.gob.ar/detalleAviso/primera/345309/20260803>)

<a id="as"></a>
### American Samoa (AS)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Do not ship under the volunteer-store policy: the published route requires a prior permit/authorization or special regulatory documents. American Samoa plant quarantine rules require a prior permit for plant material, with origin certification and arrival inspection; definitions include plant products and no manufactured-tea exception was identified. Obtain the tea-specific permit before dispatch.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ams68618.pdf>) · [asbar.org 2](<https://asbar.org/regulation/24-0328-permit-for-plant-material-importation/>)

American Samoa plant quarantine rules require a prior permit for plant material, with origin certification and arrival inspection; definitions include plant products and no manufactured-tea exception was identified. Obtain the tea-specific permit before dispatch.

**Personal post:** Import definition covers any person and shipment. No small personal postal waiver found.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Agriculture must confirm permit conditions and the required origin certificate for sealed Japanese sencha.

<a id="aw"></a>
### Aruba (AW)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Personal consignments of tea within Afl.400 CIF are expressly exempt from the economic licence; official ordinary-food guidance states no import certification documents are required and reserves sanitary certification for perishable foods. This conclusion concerns manufactured packaged food, not nursery plants or herbal medicines.

**Document-rule sources:** [gobierno.aw 1](<https://www.gobierno.aw/nl/1012-prijsvorming>) · [cuatro.sim-cdn.nl 2](<https://cuatro.sim-cdn.nl/arubaoverheid2858bd/uploads/1012gt91.018.pdf>) · [gobierno.aw 3](<https://www.gobierno.aw/nl/ab2022no168>) · [deaci.aw 4](<https://www.deaci.aw/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMPORT-2022.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 5](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/aw_2022_imp-req_en.pdf>)

Tea is an economic-licence commodity, but article 1(2) of the current Landsbesluit invoervergunning goederen expressly exempts consignments with CIF value at most Afl.400, destined for natural persons and not imported for trade. DEACI food guidance requires sanitary certificates for perishables, and says no import certification documents are required for ordinary food; manufactured dry tea is a nonperishable food.

**Personal post:** The legal exemption is per consignment to a natural person for noncommercial use, with no traveler-only or gift-only wording. DEACI also addresses parcel-post/mail-order shipments. Apply only to sealed manufactured plain tea for the recipient’s own consumption.

**Quantity conditions:** CIF value per noncommercial consignment to a natural person, including freight and insurance; economic licence exemption, not a weight limit. Value threshold: 400 AWG.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** None for the specified nonperishable plain tea within Afl.400 CIF. Maintain normal retail food labeling and customs declaration; medicinal/supplement products and perishable foods use different rules.

**Sources:** [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 1](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/aw_2022_imp-req_en.pdf>) · [gobierno.aw 2](<https://www.gobierno.aw/nl/1012-prijsvorming>) · [cuatro.sim-cdn.nl 3](<https://cuatro.sim-cdn.nl/arubaoverheid2858bd/uploads/1012gt91.018.pdf>) · [gobierno.aw 4](<https://www.gobierno.aw/nl/ab2022no168>) · [deaci.aw 5](<https://www.deaci.aw/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMPORT-2022.pdf>)

### Bosnia and Herzegovina (BA)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

Finished food tea is outside the complete certificate-controlled commodity list; the personal-post tea entry also expressly waives sanitary/market certificates. <a id="bb"></a>
### Barbados (BB)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Combined-source scope inference supports no special documents for sealed plain steam-manufactured sencha: the operative 2023 Act expressly aligns interpretation with ISPM 5 and its measures with international standards; the current-linked ISPM 32 example classifies tea in Category 1, consistent with the domestic risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. This resolves the prior missing commodity evidence without requiring a bespoke ruling. It does not rely on IPPC membership alone, treat Appendix 2 as an enacted domestic exemption list, or extend to raw leaves, herbal mixtures, planting material or contaminated goods.

**Document-rule sources:** [governmentprintery.gov.bb 1](<https://governmentprintery.gov.bb/gazette-february-20-2023-part-a-clviii-no-19/>) · [governmentprintery.gov.bb 2](<https://governmentprintery.gov.bb/gazette-may-29-2024-part-a-vol-clix-no-56-with-s-is-nos-1-2/>) · [ippc.int 3](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 4](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [oag.gov.bb 5](<https://oag.gov.bb/attachments/Health%20Services%20%28Food%20Hygiene%29%20Regulations%2C%201969%20Cap44%27M.PDF>)

Plant Protection Act 2023–2, assented 16 February 2023 and commenced 29 May 2024 by SI 2024/23, replaces the previously cited Cap.268 (2007). Section 2 limits manufactured plant products to products whose nature/processing may create pest risk and section 2(2) expressly requires interpretation in accordance with ISPM 5. Sections 14 and 18(4) require internationally harmonised, scientific/risk-based measures. The current-linked ISPM 32 Appendix 2 illustrates tea as Category 1; normative Category 1 guidance says processing has made the commodity incapable of carrying quarantine pests and no phytosanitary measures should be required for pests eliminated by that processing. Combining the domestic scope and mandatory standards interpretation with that actual tea example supports treating the stated factory-manufactured steamed/rolled/dried pure sencha as outside the Act’s plant-product/regulated-article document route. This is an explicit legal-scope inference, not a Barbados tea-specific ruling; Appendix 2 itself is reference-only/non-prescriptive. Sections 18–20 permit, certificate and advance-notice duties attach to covered articles. Independent Food Hygiene Regulations 26 A allows arrival inspection and discretionary requests for an origin inspection certificate but does not impose a universal advance foreign certificate. A domestic wholesome-food certificate issued following inspection is not a certificate the volunteer exporter must procure.

**Personal post:** Section 27 expressly covers postal and courier interception of regulated articles. The inference concerns statutory manufactured-product scope and is therefore not limited to travellers or gifts: it covers the stated paid personal postal sencha. Ordinary declaration, inspection, uncontaminated packaging and compliance with any actual pest finding remain necessary.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [governmentprintery.gov.bb 1](<https://governmentprintery.gov.bb/gazette-february-20-2023-part-a-clviii-no-19/>) · [governmentprintery.gov.bb 2](<https://governmentprintery.gov.bb/gazette-may-29-2024-part-a-vol-clix-no-56-with-s-is-nos-1-2/>) · [ippc.int 3](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 4](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.bb 5](<https://agriculture.gov.bb/Apply/ImportationOfPlants>) · [agriculture.gov.bb 6](<https://agriculture.gov.bb/attachments/Plant%20Protection%20Act%20CAP268_1.pdf>) · [oag.gov.bb 7](<https://oag.gov.bb/attachments/Health%20Services%20%28Food%20Hygiene%29%20Regulations%2C%201969%20Cap44%27M.PDF>) · [alpha.gov.bb 8](<https://alpha.gov.bb/business-trade/apply-for-food-business-licence>) · [health.gov.bb 9](<https://www.health.gov.bb/Legislation>)

<a id="bd"></a>
### Bangladesh (BD)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [hub.bangladeshcustoms.gov.bd 1](<https://hub.bangladeshcustoms.gov.bd/operative-tariff/details/09021000/import>)

Bangladesh Customs tea-specific HS 09021000 requirements identify DAE plant quarantine import permit,7 days prior notification, origin-country phytosanitary certificate and quarantine release. A Tea Board import licence is also listed.

**Personal post:** Commodity-specific import requirements cover retail green tea; no exemption for a personal postal 100 g consignment was located. Do not substitute traveller duty relief for quarantine permission.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Obtain DAE confirmation/permit and Japanese phytosanitary certificate before dispatch, and confirm whether the personal consignment also needs the listed Tea Board licence.

**Sources:** [hub.bangladeshcustoms.gov.bd 1](<https://hub.bangladeshcustoms.gov.bd/operative-tariff/details/09021000/import>)

<a id="bh"></a>
### Bahrain (BH)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Explicit legal-scope and manufacturing inference: the operative national law limits manufactured plant products by pest risk, and the actual ISPM 32 tea example supports the finding that factory-made, sealed steamed/rolled/dried sencha is outside that class. A missing Customs agency lookup does not require a bespoke ruling where this general definition suffices. Use the separate official personal-food route, supply Arabic or English product labelling, and have the recipient complete the routine personal-use undertaking if requested. This is not a claim of a tea-specific Bahrain ruling or an exemption for raw dried herbs, seeds, blends or contaminated goods.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [ippc.int 2](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/bahrain/eventreporting/2017/10/import-phytosanitary-requirements-2/>) · [mun.gov.bh 3](<https://www.mun.gov.bh/newportal/newportal/en/agriculture-marine-resources-affairs/services/examination-and-application-agricultural-quarantine>) · [assets.ippc.int 4](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/eventreporting/2017/10/23/plant_quarantine_law_-_English.pdf>) · [ofoq.gov.bh 5](<https://www.ofoq.gov.bh/pdf/FoodImpotersGuide_2024_EN.pdf>) · [mun.gov.bh 6](<https://www.mun.gov.bh/newportal/newportal/en/agriculture-marine-resources-affairs/rules-and-regulations>) · [lloc.gov.bh 7](<https://lloc.gov.bh/OG/3800A.pdf>) · [tfadatabase.org 8](<https://tfadatabase.org/en/members/bahrain-kingdom-of/publication-of-information-for-governments-and-traders>) · [bahraincustoms.gov.bh 9](<https://www.bahraincustoms.gov.bh/ar/tariff-finder>) · [customs.gov.bh 10](<https://www.customs.gov.bh/en/tariff-finder>) · [ofoq.gov.bh 11](<https://www.ofoq.gov.bh/pdf/FoodImportersGuide_2024_EN.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 12](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bah72236.pdf>) · [gov.si 13](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [bahrain.bh 14](<https://www.bahrain.bh/wps/portal/ar/BNP/ServicesCatalogue/GSX-UI-PServiceDetails?psID=1560>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. Supported for the described factory-steamed, rolled and dried plain tea by an explicit scope inference. Bahrain's enacted GCC Plant Quarantine Law Article 2 covers living plants and manufactured plant products that can introduce or spread pests; Articles 15(1) and 15(3) address covered consignments and risk-based waivers. ISPM 32 section 2 and Appendix 2 identify manufactured tea as Category 1. Applied as factual evidence to the domestic definition, the sealed finished sencha falls outside the pest-risk-qualified manufactured-product limb; it does not need an individual waiver to be outside that limb. Article 31(1) requires measures to conform with IPPC/WTO-SPS standards, but is not treated as automatically adopting every ISPM. The current 2024 food guide separately allows regular food without prior registration/authorization and provides a personal-use route with recipient undertaking, suitable personal quantity, Arabic/English labels and possible inspection.

**Personal post:** The conclusion uses the generally applicable domestic product definition and the food guide's non-commercial-import section, not a traveller-only concession. Japan MAFF's postal PQ*11 entry preserves a processing exception; its default is not treated as proof that manufactured Category 1 tea is covered. Ordinary postal Customs declarations and recipient food-clearance declarations remain required.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

<a id="bj"></a>
### Benin (BJ)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The operative annex expressly admits suitably processed dried plant products without a phytosanitary certificate and exempts processed tea from the tea-plant prohibition. No permit is prescribed for that category. Use only sealed, properly processed, pest-free pure sencha and make the ordinary arrival/postal declaration; fresh tea, propagative material and contaminated goods are outside this conclusion.

**Document-rule sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/08/13/Arrêté_Inter_Année_1995-N128_Ctrl_phyto.pdf>) · [sgg.gouv.bj 2](<https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-91-004/download>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ben2350.pdf>) · [douanes.gouv.bj 4](<https://douanes.gouv.bj/storage/2024/11/Mesures-de-la-protection-phytosanitaire-au-Benin.pdf>)

Order 95-128 of 7 March 1995, Annex II, expressly excludes processed Camellia sinensis products from the plant-import prohibition. Annex III section 2.5 authorizes manufactured dried, roasted, candied and dehydrated plant products without a phytosanitary certificate when prepared so they present no quarantine-pest contamination risk and are not otherwise prohibited. They must be declared on entry. Section 2.1 imposes a permit on fresh/refrigerated fruit and vegetables, while section 2.5 imposes no permit on the manufactured category. Applying that category to clean, sealed, steamed/dried sencha supports the scoped shipment; this is a reasoned application of an express processing exemption.

**Personal post:** Article 8 of Order 95-128 applies its requirements to private postal imports. The manufactured-product exemption in Annex III section 2.5 is commodity-based and is not restricted to passenger baggage.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/08/13/Arrêté_Inter_Année_1995-N128_Ctrl_phyto.pdf>) · [sgg.gouv.bj 2](<https://sgg.gouv.bj/doc/loi-91-004/download>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ben2350.pdf>) · [douanes.gouv.bj 4](<https://douanes.gouv.bj/storage/2024/11/Mesures-de-la-protection-phytosanitaire-au-Benin.pdf>)

<a id="bl"></a>
### Saint Barthélemy (BL)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

The expressly adopted risk-qualified manufactured-product definition and ISPM 32 tea Category 1 provide favorable processing evidence. However, current DGAL/SDSPV/2026-165 retains pre-2019 overseas implementation despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop; the actual local-order scope/compatibility remains unresolved. L 272-11-1 supplies the statutory bridge. Organic Law 2007-223 Article 18 IX preserves non-contrary pre-existing measures with territorial-reference substitution; this can preserve both favorable national lists and an applicable Guadeloupe 1996 order. The 2026 environmental code living-specimen restrictions do not cover finished nonliving tea. Remaining issue is actual inherited local-order scope and the published old/new implementation conflict, not absence of a new tea-specific schedule.

Remaining issue is actual inherited local-order scope and the published old/new implementation conflict, not absence of a new tea-specific schedule. Current statute and ministry implementation conflict; a general published processing/implementation clarification suffices without a bespoke tea letter.

Second review searched 2025/2026 local tea exclusions. The 2025 ministry overseas instruction explicitly excludes Saint Barthélemy/Saint Martin regulations from its analysis; it does not extend a mainland exemption. Retrieved the official 199-page Environment Code via the territorial website’s March 2026 consolidation link. Read Articles 32-1 to 32-28 and the Article 32-17 plant annex: the invasive-species prohibition concerns living specimens. Separately read current Code rural L 272-1, L 272-3 and L 272-11-1. Environmental scope is now clear but does not establish the applicable national agricultural import schedule; no inferred blanket tea ban or clearance. 2026-09-30 bounded independent French-territory peer review: Organic Law 2007-223 Article 18 IX expressly retains non-contrary pre-existing legislative and regulatory provisions and substitutes territorial references. This supplies a continuity chain for the national 1990/2006 rules after detachment from Guadeloupe, reinforced by current Code rural L 272-11-1. The complete applicable national certificate schedules do not list finished tea; the 2026 Environment Code living-specimen rule does not cover non-living finished tea. However, the same continuity mechanism can preserve an applicable Guadeloupe local 1996 restriction. Its original territorial and channel scope has not been recovered. 30 September fresh domestic-definition review: read currentL 271-7 and 2019-1110 original/currentArticle 6, ratification by 2023-171 Art 39, and official indexed 2026-165/2026-278 implementation statements. Verified 2025-102 abrogated, with 2026-165 current. New actualTeaCategory 1 evidence supports a general processing inference, but present statute/agency implementation and concrete local-rule scope conflict persists; not merely membership or a missing bespoke tea ruling. Source/territory findings in french-definition-review/peer-conclusions.json.

<a id="bn"></a>
### Brunei (BN)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [bdfa.gov.bn 1](<https://bdfa.gov.bn/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/BDFA-Guidelines-for-Personal-Importation-of-Processed-Food-Products-Jul-2023.pdf>) · [customs.gov.bn 2](<https://www.customs.gov.bn/ref-rced-prohibited-restricted-controlled-items/>) · [maff.go.jp 3](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. Footnote 11 states that some cases are outside plant quarantine and need neither document, without identifying this product as exempt. The BDFA personal processed-food route is separate.

**Personal post:** The official guide expressly covers individuals, courier services and forwarding companies importing for personal consumption.

**Quantity conditions:** Tea-item cap: 5 × 100 g pouches (0.5 kg tea). Up to 5 kg product weight. All food in one personal import. Total personal food allowance: 5 kg and BND 100; also at most 5 retail pieces of each item. For these 100 g pouches that means 500 g of this tea item. Exceeding a condition requires personal food-import approval, separate from agricultural clearance. Five pieces per item; each 100 g retail pouch is counted as one piece for this reconciliation.

**Checkout:** 5 pouches / 0.5 kg tea; highest carrier band 0.68 kg gross. Current maximum is within this numeric threshold; this does not settle permits, value limits, frequency, packaging or genuine personal use.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Confirm whether this sealed manufactured sencha is outside plant quarantine under footnote 11; otherwise obtain plant import permission and Japanese phytosanitary certification. Food personal-import approval is separately needed when small-consignment conditions are exceeded.

**Sources:** [bdfa.gov.bn 1](<https://bdfa.gov.bn/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/BDFA-Guidelines-for-Personal-Importation-of-Processed-Food-Products-Jul-2023.pdf>) · [customs.gov.bn 2](<https://www.customs.gov.bn/ref-rced-prohibited-restricted-controlled-items/>) · [maff.go.jp 3](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

<a id="bq"></a>
### Bonaire, Saba and Sint Eustatius (BQ)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The official current BES Invoeringswet, article 18.2.4 a(c)–(d), limits plants to living plants/parts and plant products to unprocessed products or products subjected only to simple processing. Article 18.2.4 b authorises import conditions within that scope; it does not itself impose a certificate on every plant-derived food. The described steamed, repeatedly rolled/pressed, hot-air dried sencha is a finished manufactured food, with ISPM 32 Category 1 tea providing technical corroboration. The better general-category inference places it beyond simple processing and therefore outside that plant-product control class. This applies the BES statute itself, which covers Bonaire, Sint Eustatius and Saba, rather than importing European Netherlands rules or extending Bonaire’s Venezuela-only notice. The statutory used-packaging category does not create a duty for clean new factory and postal packaging. No contrary applicable tea or broad manufactured-food condition was recovered in the bounded island/national rule check. The actual product is pure Camellia sinensis sencha manufactured by steaming to arrest enzyme activity, repeated rolling/pressing with hot air, and drying, then supplied sealed for infusion. MAFF describes this manufacture. ISPM 32 section 2 and Appendix 2 identify manufactured tea as Category 1, supporting the factual conclusion that this differs from merely dried raw botanical material. The international standard is used as technical evidence when applying the local definition, not as an automatically binding local exemption. Ordinary inspection, food safety and pest freedom remain conditions; this is not a route for raw/sun-dried leaves, seeds, blends or contaminated goods.

**Document-rule sources:** [bonairegov.com 1](<https://bonairegov.com/en/nieuwsoverzicht/artikel/import-uit-venezuela-versoepeld>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 2](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028063/2024-01-01/>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 3](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028619/>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/net214082.pdf>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 5](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028063/2025-01-01>) · [gov.si 6](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastlin/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 7](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

The official current BES Invoeringswet, article 18.2.4 a(c)–(d), limits plants to living plants/parts and plant products to unprocessed products or products subjected only to simple processing. Article 18.2.4 b authorises import conditions within that scope; it does not itself impose a certificate on every plant-derived food. The described steamed, repeatedly rolled/pressed, hot-air dried sencha is a finished manufactured food, with ISPM 32 Category 1 tea providing technical corroboration. The better general-category inference places it beyond simple processing and therefore outside that plant-product control class. This applies the BES statute itself, which covers Bonaire, Sint Eustatius and Saba, rather than importing European Netherlands rules or extending Bonaire’s Venezuela-only notice. The statutory used-packaging category does not create a duty for clean new factory and postal packaging. No contrary applicable tea or broad manufactured-food condition was recovered in the bounded island/national rule check.

**Personal post:** This conclusion follows commodity scope in the applicable local law, not a baggage, gift or noncommercial-seller exception. It applies to the stated private direct postal purchase and clean new packaging.

**Checkout:** 207 pouches / 20.7 kg tea; highest carrier band 25 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [bonairegov.com 1](<https://bonairegov.com/en/nieuwsoverzicht/artikel/import-uit-venezuela-versoepeld>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 2](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028063/2024-01-01/>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 3](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028619/>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/net214082.pdf>) · [zoek.officielebekendmakingen.nl 5](<https://zoek.officielebekendmakingen.nl/kst-31957-7.html>) · [wetten.overheid.nl 6](<https://wetten.overheid.nl/BWBR0028063/2025-01-01>) · [gov.si 7](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastlin/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 8](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

### Bahamas (BS)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

No-document shipping is not supported on an explicit legal-scope inference: regulation 4(1) independently requires a written licence for imported food, and Interpretation Act section 33 continues compatible subsidiary rules when their parent law is replaced. Current official legal indexing places this regulation under Customs Management Act 2011. The stated product is ordinary sealed manufactured tea; BAHFSA’s published exemption for aseptically packaged and hermetically or vacuum-sealed food is not established by ordinary retail sealing alone. Neither tea’s omission from the plant-permit HS list nor the private recipient’s absence of a food business waives this separate licence duty.

**Document-rule sources:** [bahfsabahamas.com 1](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/plants/faqs>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 2](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/international/guidelines-for-the-importation-of-plants-and-plant-products>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 3](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/downloads/documents/legislation/Food-Import-Conditions-Appendix.pdf>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 4](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/services/epermitting-for-imports>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 5](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/uploads/General-Import-Stepsv2_2024-10-04-140708_iqzg.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 6](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bha158350.pdf>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 7](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/international/food-import-conditions>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 8](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/SUBORDINATE/1972/1972-0050/1972-0050.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bha112362.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 10](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1976/1976-0020/1976-0020_1.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 11](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/2011/2011-0030/2011-0030.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 12](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2013/2013-0023/2013-0023.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 13](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/index/by-category.html>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 14](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1985/1985-0001/1985-0001_1.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 15](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1955/1955-0001/1955-0001_R.pdf>)

The 2024 plant-permit HS list omits 0902 tea, leaving the phytosanitary classification unverified. A separate recovered instrument, Import Control (Foods, Plants and Animals) Regulations 1993 (SI 50/1972 as amended SI 35/1993), regulation 4(1), requires a written agriculture-authority licence for any food import, without a business-only or personal-post limitation. Legal-scope inference: the subsidiary regulation remains operative after replacement of its parent Act under Interpretation and General Clauses Act section 33(1), consistent with its current official indexing under Customs Management Act 2011 and that Act’s import-restriction powers. No automatic exemption applicable to the stated ordinary sealed sencha packet was established. This conclusion relies on the separate food-import licence, not a phytosanitary certificate or section 35 of the business-scoped 2016 Food Act.

**Personal post:** Inference from an unqualified any-person/any-food import licence duty, independent of the 2016 Food Act business scope. Customs Management Act section 344 also applies customs law to postal articles. No passenger or gift exception is transferred to this paid private postal purchase.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Agricultural classification remains unverified. The operational exclusion is based on the separate food-import licence and an explicit continuation/scope inference, not a tea-specific ruling. Reconsider if an operative general licence, repeal or personal-post exemption under this instrument is published, or if an applicable BAHFSA exemption is established for the actual product; none was recovered.

**Sources:** [bahfsabahamas.com 1](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/plants/faqs>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 2](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/international/guidelines-for-the-importation-of-plants-and-plant-products>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 3](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/downloads/documents/legislation/Food-Import-Conditions-Appendix.pdf>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 4](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/uploads/General-Import-Stepsv2_2024-10-04-140708_iqzg.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bha158350.pdf>) · [bahfsabahamas.com 6](<https://bahfsabahamas.com/sectors/international/food-import-conditions>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 7](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/SUBORDINATE/1972/1972-0050/1972-0050.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/bha112362.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 9](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1976/1976-0020/1976-0020_1.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 10](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/2011/2011-0030/2011-0030.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 11](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/AMENDING/2013/2013-0023/2013-0023.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 12](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/legislation/index/by-category.html>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 13](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1985/1985-0001/1985-0001_1.pdf>) · [laws.bahamas.gov.bs 14](<https://laws.bahamas.gov.bs/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1955/1955-0001/1955-0001_R.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read BAHFSA tea declaration FAQ, all-channel plant-import rules and food-conditions appendix. Confirmed mail/courier scope but could not classify manufactured sencha under processed-product exceptions. 2026-09-30: Reviewed current BAHFSA e-permitting and FAQ material. September 2024 Click 2 Clear plant-permit instructions and a referenced HS schedule are current leads, but schedule content could not be retrieved (HTTP 403). Traveler declarations of teas and all-plant permit language do not settle processed retail sencha classification. 2026-09-30: Read indexed full September 2024 BAHFSA Click 2 Clear HS permit schedule, described as all commodities requiring permits. It omits HS 0902 while listing selected raw spices; this supports exclusion from the listed plant permit scope, but not independently food-document exemption. Retrieved Food Safety and Quality Act 2016: section 35(1) applies import-permit/certificate-of-origin rules to food generally; section 35(2) permits Director discretion. Published food-conditions appendix does not clarify a personal retail-tea waiver, so the schedule and food-document framework remain unreconciled. 2026-09-30: Re-read Act sections 1, 2, 4 and 35 in full. Corrected the earlier suggestion that section 35 alone applies to all private food imports: section 4(1) limits the Act to food businesses, defined to include nonprofit operations; importer is separately broadly defined. Current BAHFSA Food Import Conditions webpage provides the previously inaccessible appendix text: points 5/8/9 distinguish aseptically sealed permit exemption from health/origin certificates, without private-post scope. Current law index lists the Act but no commencement notice was found. Retain unresolved, not an invented waiver or automatic exclusion. Fresh official online recovery 30 September 2026: obtained the two-page SI 50/1972 as amended SI 35/1993 from the official national legal repository and a complete FAO mirror. Regulation 2 adopts Food Act’s food definition; regulation 4(1) applies to any person importing any food and regulation 4(2) allows licence terms. No food-business scope or automatic small postal exception occurs in this instrument. Food Act 1985 section 2 includes articles used for drink and ingredients used to prepare human food. Checked repeal before relying on the old regulation. Official parent 1955 Act PDF is marked repealed by Act 30/2011. Interpretation and General Clauses Act section 33(1) continues subsidiary legislation when an Act is repealed and substituted, insofar as consistent. Customs Management Act 2011 section 354(1)(q) and section 208 provide replacement import-restriction powers; the current official legislation-by-category index explicitly places the food regulation and the separate 1957 general import rules under this Act. Section 344 preserves application to postal articles. This is a stated continuation/scope inference, not a claim that the 1955 parent remains in force. Read Customs Management Amendment Act 23/2013 section 31: only changes section 358(a) repeal wording from other existing Customs legislation to Customs Regulations 1976. The Court of Appeal 2022 Jones judgment confirms the 2011 Act commenced 1 July 2013 and old Customs Act was repealed; it does not decide this food regulation. Current consolidated section 358 is marked Repealed (spent repeal clause), so did not infer parent revival. Read separate 1957 Import Control Regulations regulations 6–7. Their general/class-licence power is not an actual published waiver. Their personal-baggage, bona fide gift, UK-manufacture and limited low-value noncommercial exceptions are expressly exceptions to these Regulations, not a universal exemption from the separate 1972/1993 food instrument. No such exception was expanded to the stated paid Japanese postal tea. Rechecked current BAHFSA Food Import Conditions points 1–5,8–9 and 2024 HS schedule. Aseptically packaged and hermetically/vacuum-sealed foods have a stated permit exemption; ordinary retail sealing does not establish aseptic packaging. The food guidance still includes certificates and risk assessment, but its business/private scope is not used to decide this exclusion. Tea omission from the plant-permit schedule is preserved as narrower evidence, not a food-licence waiver.

### Central Africa (CF)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The published law requires a phytosanitary certificate for imported plant parts without a living-material or risk-classification qualification. Dried tea leaves are treated as plant parts for this conservative operational decision; that application is explicitly an inference. This is exclusion under the store constraint, not a finding that tea is prohibited in all circumstances.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/caf7180.pdf>) · [cbd.int 2](<https://www.cbd.int/doc/world/cf/cf-nr-04-fr.pdf>) · [documents1.worldbank.org 3](<https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/460361594294635417/pdf/Cadre-de-Gestion-Environnementale-et-Sociale.pdf>)

Law 62/350 of 4 January 1963, article 2, requires a phytosanitary certificate for imports of plants and plant parts. The text does not limit plant parts to living or unprocessed material. Application to the identifiable dried leaves in sencha is an inference from this broad wording, rather than a tea-specific official ruling.

**Personal post:** Article 2 is not limited to commercial importers or bulk quantities, and the text contains no automatic personal-post exemption. No later applicable processed-tea exemption was located.
