<a id="cg"></a>
### Congo (CG)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Independent import-authorization requirement: enacted Law 3-2007 Article 7(2) expressly applies the special import-authorization regime to Article 9 imports, and Article 9 expressly includes households importing for their own needs without commercial-importer status. Articles 1–2 cover goods acquired abroad for consumption, without a quantity or postal exclusion. Applying this wording to a paid private tea parcel is an inference from the general statute, not a finding that tea is phytosanitarily prohibited. Article 11 removes personal-use preshipment inspection only; the separately documented PCEC e-commerce-post exemption does not remove the statutory special import authorization. No later applicable personal-post waiver was recovered.

**Document-rule sources:** [ippc.int 1](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/congo/reportingobligation/4>) · [wto.org 2](<https://www.wto.org/french/tratop_f/tpr_f/s445-03_f.pdf>) · [cotecna.com 3](<https://www.cotecna.com/media/1tii4ifg/voc-congo-exporter-guide-french-translation.pdf>) · [cotecna.com 4](<https://www.cotecna.com/media/pbegdzqc/voc-republic-of-congo-exporter-guide.pdf>) · [sgg.cg 5](<https://www.sgg.cg/JO/2007/congo-jo-2007-04.pdf>) · [sgg.cg 6](<https://www.sgg.cg/JO/2025/congo-jo-2025-19.pdf>)

The WTO 2023 Congo review, using information supplied by the authorities, reports prior authorizations and sanitary/phytosanitary certificates for animal and plant products (paragraph 3.92). It identifies the applicable institutional framework but does not define the treatment of finished tea or small personal postal imports. Historical NPPO filings do not resolve that classification.

**Personal post:** Law 3-2007 Articles 7(2) and 9 explicitly cover households importing for their own needs. Articles 1–2 contain no minimum amount or postal carrier exception. Applicability to this paid private postal purchase is an expressly stated general-rule inference.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** The actual ACONOQ-authorized Cotecna Congo exporter guide expressly excludes e-commerce postal parcels from PCEC/CoC certification and separately excludes complete imports below FOB 1,000,000 CFA. This is an affirmative exemption for the shipment route, not an extension of used personal effects or valueless gift exemptions. The guide says Cotecna acts on ACONOQ’s mandate. It does not resolve independent agricultural authorization/certification.

**Evidence access:** Complete French authorized-programme guide downloaded and read, especially PDF pages 2–3. It is programme-operator guidance, not a retrieved national Gazette instrument. Its postal exemption is supporting operational evidence only; no national plant-health waiver is inferred.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Finished-tea phytosanitary classification remains unresolved, but it does not remove the independently established household special import-authorization requirement. No-document feasibility is excluded on that separate basis.

**Sources:** [ippc.int 1](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/congo/reportingobligation/4>) · [wto.org 2](<https://www.wto.org/french/tratop_f/tpr_f/s445-03_f.pdf>) · [cotecna.com 3](<https://www.cotecna.com/media/1tii4ifg/voc-congo-exporter-guide-french-translation.pdf>) · [cotecna.com 4](<https://www.cotecna.com/media/pbegdzqc/voc-republic-of-congo-exporter-guide.pdf>) · [sgg.cg 5](<https://www.sgg.cg/JO/2007/congo-jo-2007-04.pdf>) · [sgg.cg 6](<https://www.sgg.cg/JO/2025/congo-jo-2025-19.pdf>) · [sgg.cg 7](<https://www.sgg.cg/fr/recherche.html?page=4942&row=123383>) · [guot.cg 8](<https://guot.cg/plateformes/>)

**Research trail:** Checked NPPO legislation filings; excluded unrelated pesticide rules and an ambiguous colonial-era gazette as proof of current tea admissibility. Read WTO 2023 Congo country report paragraphs 3.86–3.95, especially plant-product authorizations in 3.92 and implementing references. Separately searched current customs/single-window and government gazette material for tea, processed products and personal post. Did not infer agricultural permission from customs tax relief or a commercial conformity program. 30 September independent food/conformity pass: The actual ACONOQ-authorized Cotecna Congo exporter guide expressly excludes e-commerce postal parcels from PCEC/CoC certification and separately excludes complete imports below FOB 1,000,000 CFA. This is an affirmative exemption for the shipment route, not an extension of used personal effects or valueless gift exemptions. The guide says Cotecna acts on ACONOQ’s mandate. It does not resolve independent agricultural authorization/certification. Complete French authorized-programme guide downloaded and read, especially PDF pages 2–3. It is programme-operator guidance, not a retrieved national Gazette instrument. Its postal exemption is supporting operational evidence only; no national plant-health waiver is inferred. 30 September fresh official-online recovery: obtained the complete enacted Law 3-2007 directly from SGG, visually verified printed page 256 including Articles 7(2), 9 and 11, and checked the complete Article 27 context. Recovered 2025 official Gazette corroboration. Targeted later household, postal, small-consignment and ASI-exemption searches on SGG/GUOT found no applicable legislative waiver. Did not mistake the separate PCEC postal waiver for an exemption from the general import statute. Agricultural status remains not_verified.

<a id="ch"></a>
### Switzerland (CH)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Processed plant products, explicitly including dried tea, need no phytosanitary certificate or phytosanitary inspection. Switzerland and Liechtenstein share the plant-health import system. This is a personal-consumption route; comply with the stated packaging and quantity conditions and ordinary postal/customs declarations.

**Document-rule sources:** [blw.admin.ch 1](<https://www.blw.admin.ch/en/importing-plants>)

Processed plant products, explicitly including dried tea, need no phytosanitary certificate or phytosanitary inspection. Switzerland and Liechtenstein share the plant-health import system.

**Personal post:** FOAG gives a general processed-products exemption on its import page, which separately addresses postal/courier consignments; it is not only a passenger allowance.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Sources:** [blw.admin.ch 1](<https://www.blw.admin.ch/en/importing-plants>)

<a id="ci"></a>
### Ivory Coast (CI)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The current official portal relies on Decree 63-457. Articles 1(b), 2 and 5 together impose prior permission on dried plants irrespective of shipment size. Application to dried sencha leaves is an inference from that express scope; the required prior permit conflicts with the store’s no-permits policy. This is not a claim that tea is prohibited.

**Document-rule sources:** [gucecotedivoire.ci 1](<https://www.gucecotedivoire.ci/pwic/vegetaux-et-produits-d-origine-vegetale/>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/Ivc178121.pdf>)

Decree 63-457, still identified by the official trade portal as the governing instrument, requires an import permit before ordering covered goods (article 2). Article 1(b) expressly covers any dried plant; article 5 applies articles 1 and 2 to every import whatever its size. The current portal also requires a sanitary or analysis certificate for processed plant-origin products. Applying the broad dried-plant provision to identifiable dried sencha leaves is an interpretation of the statutory wording, not a tea-specific written ruling.

**Personal post:** Article 5 expressly extends the permit provisions to all imports regardless of size, with baggage mentioned as an additional example rather than an exclusive route. A 100 g personal parcel therefore does not obtain a small-quantity exemption under this text.

**Quantity conditions:** No small-quantity exemption: article 5 applies regardless of import size.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [gucecotedivoire.ci 1](<https://www.gucecotedivoire.ci/pwic/vegetaux-et-produits-d-origine-vegetale/>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/Ivc178121.pdf>)

<a id="ck"></a>
### Cook Islands (CK)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Camellia sencha is plainly a manufactured plant food and ISPM 32 Category 1 tea supports low pest risk. It does not establish that the local Herbs (dried) heading uses a broad infusion-leaf meaning rather than the narrower culinary/botanical category. The Act supplies a concrete reason why low risk alone does not settle entry: specification selection remains legally relevant for manufactured plant products. No supported or excluded decision should be forced from tea being unnamed.

**Document-rule sources:** [agriculture.gov.ck 1](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/Update-Import-Specification-6-January-2021..pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 2](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 3](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2020/04/Update-Import-Specification-27-April-2020.pdf>) · [mpi.govt.nz 4](<https://www.mpi.govt.nz/dmsdocument/632/direct/>) · [cook-islands.tradeportal.org 5](<https://cook-islands.tradeportal.org/media/Food-Regulations-2014.pdf>) · [health.gov.ck 6](<https://www.health.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/Food-Regulations-2014.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 7](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/register-forms/>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 8](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/importing/>) · [health.gov.ck 9](<https://www.health.gov.ck/food-regulations-2014/>) · [gov.si 10](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 11](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 12](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/bio-security-act/>) · [assets.ippc.int 13](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2017/08/11/Cook_Islands_Biosecurity_Act_2008.pdf>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/cook-islands/reportingobligation/2017/08/biosecurity-act-2008/>)

The January 2021 biosecurity import specification currently linked by the official ministry Biosecurity page distinguishes PP 29 dried herbs (all origins, no import permit or phytosanitary certificate, inspection) from PP 23 dried plant material (all origins, import permit and fumigation/heat treatment). Other dried foods have their own separate entries; there is no blanket exemption for all dried retail foods. Tea is not named. Separately, Food Regulations 2014 regulation 18 provides a personal/family food-control exemption up to 5 kg per person for other foods; that food rule does not establish the applicable plant-biosecurity classification.

**Personal post:** Both are commodity rules, but the appropriate classification for a personal mailed sencha pouch remains unconfirmed.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** An applicable current specification or a general published meaning/category allocation that distinguishes PP 29 dried herbs from PP 23 dried plant material for manufactured infusion tea; alternatively an exhaustive register showing this type has no specification. No bespoke individual ruling is intrinsically necessary.

**Sources:** [agriculture.gov.ck 1](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/Update-Import-Specification-6-January-2021..pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 2](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 3](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2020/04/Update-Import-Specification-27-April-2020.pdf>) · [mpi.govt.nz 4](<https://www.mpi.govt.nz/dmsdocument/632/direct/>) · [cook-islands.tradeportal.org 5](<https://cook-islands.tradeportal.org/media/Food-Regulations-2014.pdf>) · [health.gov.ck 6](<https://www.health.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/Food-Regulations-2014.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 7](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/register-forms/>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 8](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/importing/>) · [health.gov.ck 9](<https://www.health.gov.ck/food-regulations-2014/>) · [gov.si 10](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 11](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 12](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/biosecurity/bio-security-act/>) · [assets.ippc.int 13](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2017/08/11/Cook_Islands_Biosecurity_Act_2008.pdf>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/cook-islands/reportingobligation/2017/08/biosecurity-act-2008/>) · [agriculture.gov.ck 15](<https://agriculture.gov.ck/wp-content/uploads/2019/11/Biosecurity-Act-2008.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read the official full import specification and compared PP 23 with PP 29. Searched official agriculture guidance for tea/processed-food or personal-mail exemption; no tea-specific answer found. Reviewed current MPI guidance dated 25 July 2025 and its section 1.2 New Zealand-origin scope; did not extend it to a Japan shipment. Searched the agriculture authority and government trade portal for Camellia, manufactured tea and personal-post specifications; the PP 23/PP 29 classification remains missing. The trade-portal Food Regulations download returned HTTP 403 and is not relied on for a food exemption. 30 September independent scope reread: checked the complete 2020 biosecurity schedule, including PP 23, PP 26–29, PP 31, PP 33 and PP 47–49. Specific dried fruit/herb/mushroom/vegetable concessions preclude inferring that every dried plant food automatically needs PP 23 permission, but none names tea. Current ministry importing and electronic-register pages supply no additional tea or general processed-food waiver. Retrieved the previously inaccessible Food Regulations 2014 from the official Ministry of Health host and read regulation 18, PDF pp.10–11. Other personal/family foods are limited to 5 kg, and larger quantities need Director approval. This is a food-control concession under the Ministry of Health framework; it is not a plant-biosecurity specification and does not expressly mention post. Source PDF/text saved as asia-sources/ck-food 2014.*. 30 September independent food-document review: reread Food Regulations 2014 regulations 16, 18–19 and 23–25. Regulation 16 advance notification concerns listed high-risk/regulatory-interest food imported for sale; regulation 18 provides the personal/family concession, and regulation 19 expressly makes general requirements subject to it. Regulations 23–25 allow product-identity checks and assessment of accompanying health/analysis certificates, but do not independently mandate a foreign certificate for every small private tea parcel. A reprocessing permit under regulation 19(4) was not applied to a finished ready-for-retail pouch. These provisions do not remove the unresolved postal scope/plant-category questions. 30 September final Pacific commodity pass: retrieved the ministry Biosecurity landing page and its January 2021 import specification, a newer current-linked document than the previously reviewed April 2020 PDF. Read the complete table and visually checked PDF page 4: PP 23 dried plant material from any origin requires an import permit, no phytosanitary certificate, inspection, and pre-export fumigation/heat treatment; PP 29 dried herbs from any origin requires neither permit nor phytosanitary certificate, but inspection and the prohibited-species condition apply. The document does not name tea or Camellia and has no blanket manufactured/dried-food waiver. Its processed-food entries therefore preserve the same unresolved tea-category question. PDF, extracted text and table image saved in pacific-final-source-review/ck-import-specification 2021.*. The separate Ports Authority page contains only general cargo/quarantine guidance, not a tea waiver. 30 September independent legal-scope peer review: reread saved current-linked January 2021 specification PP 23/PP 29 and Food Regulations 2014 regulations 5, 18 and 19. PP 29 is an affirmative permit-and-certificate exemption, which must not be overridden merely by calling tea dried plant material. The schedule supplies no definition placing Camellia tea within dried herbs. Regulation 18 uses personal imports and consumption by importer/family, without an express baggage-only restriction; narrowed the remaining question to the separate biosecurity category rather than demanding that the food concession explicitly mention post. No decision change. 30 September finished-tea definition review: Camellia sencha is plainly a manufactured plant food and ISPM 32 Category 1 tea supports low pest risk. It does not establish that the local Herbs (dried) heading uses a broad infusion-leaf meaning rather than the narrower culinary/botanical category. The Act supplies a concrete reason why low risk alone does not settle entry: specification selection remains legally relevant for manufactured plant products. No supported or excluded decision should be forced from tea being unnamed. See pacific-finished-tea-definition-review/review-notes.json. Act section 2 has a processing exception within plant material: manufacture must render the item nonviable, pest/disease-free and incapable of carrying them. But plant product separately and expressly includes any product manufactured wholly or partly from plants; regulated article includes all plant products. Consequently the former definition alone does not remove finished tea from the Act or specification system. 30 September additional primary review: The Act covers manufactured plant products; the narrower plant-material definition is not an exemption from the separate plant-product limb. Sections 25–26 require permits/certificates only where the applicable specification requires them. Section 27 is an affirmative advance written-application duty only for a type not already the subject of a specification. Current ministry-published PP 29 (dried herbs) is no-permit/no-PC; PP 23 (dried plant material) requires a permit. Those are genuinely competing general categories for finished infusion leaves, not merely absent literal tea naming. Recovered the complete 73-page Act directly from the current ministry Act-page link. Its PDF text layer uses faulty font encoding, while previously saved official IPPC text confirms the relevant provisions. Public WordPress media searches for import and tea returned 403; no alternate specification was retrieved and no access bypass attempted.

<a id="cl"></a>
### Chile (CL)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Although Japan’s NPPO confirms no phytosanitary certificate for manufactured green tea sent by post to Chile, the separate food-import authorization prevents a normal-customs-only route. CorreosChile expressly lists food of any kind among postal goods requiring special authorization, and ChileAtiende requires a sanitary customs-destination certificate and authorization for use/consumption. MINSAL’s section 10.2 specifically covers personal-consumption packaged food; personal use is a special approval procedure, not a blanket waiver.

**Document-rule sources:** [correos.cl 1](<https://www.correos.cl/lo-que-debes-saber?inheritRedirect=true>) · [chileatiende.gob.cl 2](<https://www.chileatiende.gob.cl/fichas/184-autorizacion-de-uso-y-disposicion-de-alimentos-importados>) · [minsal.cl 3](<https://www.minsal.cl/sites/default/files/files/Manual%20para%20la%20Importaci%C3%B3n%20de%20Alimentos%20destinados%20al%20consumo%20humano%20MINSAL%202015%281%29.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 4](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **◎**. Japan’s current NPPO postal table explicitly marks manufactured green tea to Chile ◎: no Japanese phytosanitary export certificate required. SAG category-1 guidance supports entry of sufficiently processed plant foods; retain the sealed finished-tea packaging and manufacturing description for any inspection. This conclusion is for manufactured sencha, not raw merely dried leaf material.

**Personal post:** The 21 April 2026 MAFF table expressly covers postal shipments. No agricultural personal tea weight cap was identified.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Agricultural manufactured-tea exemption is supported, but the separate personal food-import authorization makes this route incompatible with the no-special-paperwork policy.

**Sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/sa/chile/index.html>) · [sag.gob.cl 3](<https://www.sag.gob.cl/sites/default/files/res._1.284_de_2021_con_anexo.pdf>)

<a id="cm"></a>
### Cameroon (CM)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude under the no-certificate constraint by applying the express tea-leaf inclusion in the 2019 phytosanitary schedule together with Law 2003/003 article 9(1), which requires certification of imported plant products. The fee schedule alone is not the legal basis for the certificate duty. Applying its tea-leaf item to steamed/dried retail sencha is a stated inference, not a published product ruling. This finding does not assert that an import permit is always required or that Japan-origin tea is designated high risk.

**Document-rule sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/03/30/1137602168132_Law_Relating_To_Phytosanitary_Protection_2013062711-18En.doc>) · [assets.ippc.int 2](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/03/30/1137678445097_Decret_sur_la_quarantain_vegetale_2014042319-21Fr.doc>) · [minader.cm 3](<https://www.minader.cm/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/final_RECUEIL-ANGLAIS-SIGNE-11-10-23.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/cmr126426.pdf>) · [minader.cm 5](<https://www.minader.cm/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/Final_RECUEIL-FRANCAIS-SIGNE-2-09-10-23-1.pdf>)

Law 2003/003 article 9(1) requires a phytosanitary certificate for imports of plants and plant products; article 9(2) separately regulates when an import permit is needed. Decree 2005/0771 article 3 limits prior import permits to designated high-risk products; its article 8 specifically regulates origin certification for those high-risk goods but does not expressly waive the statutory certificate duty for other plant products. The later Joint Order of 4 March 2019, Annex I, expressly lists tea leaves and, separately, manufactured products within phytosanitary import/export inspection categories, includes parcel-carrier/courier platforms, and defines a shipment by coverage under one phytosanitary certificate. Treat the scoped identifiable dried sencha leaves as the listed tea-leaf commodity under article 9. This is an explicit scope inference: the tea-leaf entry does not itself say dried or steamed, and no Japan/sencha-specific ruling was found.

**Personal post:** The 2019 implementing inspection schedule expressly includes parcel carriers and couriers. No personal-use waiver of the article 9 certificate duty was established. The professional-importer registration requirements in the separate commercial e-Force permit workflow are not imposed here on the private recipient.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/03/30/1137602168132_Law_Relating_To_Phytosanitary_Protection_2013062711-18En.doc>) · [assets.ippc.int 2](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/03/30/1137678445097_Decret_sur_la_quarantain_vegetale_2014042319-21Fr.doc>) · [minader.cm 3](<https://www.minader.cm/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/final_RECUEIL-ANGLAIS-SIGNE-11-10-23.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/cmr126426.pdf>) · [minader.cm 5](<https://www.minader.cm/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/Final_RECUEIL-FRANCAIS-SIGNE-2-09-10-23-1.pdf>)

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Current Curaçao operative plant/plant-product import definition or conditions for processed food; if the older free-sale certificate claim remains applicable, its enacted current private-post scope is also missing.

**Sources:** [douane.cw 1](<https://douane.cw/particulier/reizigersbagage/verboden-in-en-uit-te-voeren/>) · [douane.cw 2](<https://douane.cw/zakelijk/inklaring-en-invoer/>) · [lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl 3](<https://lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl/CVDR143880/1>) · [lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl 4](<https://lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl/CVDR143880/1?show-wti=true>) · [nvwa.nl 5](<https://www.nvwa.nl/documenten/fytosanitair/landeneisen/diverse-producten/cura-ao-diverse-producten-exporteisen>) · [nvwa.nl 6](<https://www.nvwa.nl/site/binaries/site-content/collections/documents/fytosanitair/landeneisen/diverse-producten/cura-ao-diverse-producten-exporteisen/Curacao_Diverse_producten_exporteisen_1.1.pdf>) · [ftac.cw 7](<https://ftac.cw/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Rapport-levensmiddelen-FTAC.pdf>) · [apps.fas.usda.gov 8](<https://apps.fas.usda.gov/newgainapi/api/report/downloadreportbyfilename?filename=Curacao+-+FAIRS+Country+Report_Miami+ATO_Netherlands+Antilles_12-23-2013.pdf>) · [gobiernu.cw 9](<https://gobiernu.cw/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Klapper-A.B.-Hoofdstuk-X-Economische-Aangelegenheden-Update-tot-en-met-P.B.-2025-no.-143.pdf>) · [gobiernu.cw 10](<https://gobiernu.cw/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Klapper-P.B.-Hoofdstuk.-X-ECONOMISCHE-AANGELEGENHEDEN-Updated-tot-en-met-P.B.-2025-no.-149GT.pdf>) · [gobiernu.cw 11](<https://gobiernu.cw/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Klapper-P.B.-Gebruiksaanwijzingen.-Alfabetische-Trefwoorden-Updated-tm-2025-no.-128.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed Curaçao customs forbidden-goods and general clearance procedures. Plant/flower permit entries do not classify tea, and personal customs treatment does not establish a plant-health exemption. 2026-09-30: Current Warenlandsverordening article 2(1) likewise excludes manufacture within a private household, not import of purchased retail food. Article 3(1)(j)–(k) authorizes import/export and registration rules, not a self-executing universal origin certificate; regulation-making powers alone do not exclude personal tea. The publicly indexed permit-list decree concerns specific bakery/meat/produce categories, not a new tea ruling. No material personal-postal exemption/barrier established; LVV finished-tea conditions remain missing. 2026-09-30 fresh official online recovery: Recovered the current-linked Dutch NPPO diverse-product Curaçao sheet, effective 22 May 2013, and the Curaçao Fair Trade Authority 2024 food-market report. The NPPO sheet has no populated certificate commodity list and states no specific requirements are known; it says a requested certificate for an unnamed product requires official destination information and assessment. Its missing-information statement is not a positive Curaçao permit/certificate exemption for Japanese tea. The local authority report maps food import laws but supplies no all-private-food certificate duty or manufactured-tea waiver. Saved full PDFs/texts cw-nvwa-diverse 2013 and cw-ftac 2024. 30 September renewed official-source/general-definition review: Recovered USDA’s official 23 December 2013 Curaçao FAIRS report (CB 1317). It distinguishes processed products monitored at retail from plant products controlled at entry; it also says most processed products require a free-sale certificate and product samples sent by parcel post are subject only to customs duty. Neither statement settles the current private paid 100 g order: the report is old, written for US commercial exporters, supplies no exact enacted certificate provision or personal-use scope, and a purchase is not a trade sample. Native-language official legal searches returned pesticide/CITES/tariff provisions rather than a new operative manufactured-tea plant rule. No conclusion was drawn from unrelated EU plant rules. 30 September operative-source recovery: Queried official public WordPress media index and retrieved latest returned country(P.B.)and island(A.B.)economic-law indexes, uploaded 26 November 2025. Read countryX.16 land/tuinbouw and complete 21 pageislandindex: no recovered operative general plant-import definition. Countryindex positively marks 1987 no 133 tea-bag import regulation expired 25 December 1992; this is not a current tea exemption.2010 Dutchtransition parliamentary text says BESthenhad no phytosanitary rules, but does not establish CW/SXscope and was not transferred. CurrentCustoms repeats plants/flowers permit category. Index omissions/expired economic controls cannot negate any independent phytosanitary condition; old 2013 commercialfree-salescertificate assertion remains unverified for private post.

<a id="dm"></a>
### Dominica (DM)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported conditionally for a 100 g sealed plain industrially steamed/rolled/dried sencha parcel for personal drinking: domestic risk-qualified product scope plus domestic recognition of processed pest-free products and actual Category 1 tea evidence. Excludes raw/naturally dried herbs, viable material, visible infestation/contamination, and plant-based untreated packing. No independently established enacted private-food certificate, registration or advance-approval duty.

**Document-rule sources:** [establishment.gov.dm 1](<https://establishment.gov.dm/services/application-import-permit>) · [divisionofagriculture.gov.dm 2](<https://divisionofagriculture.gov.dm/images/documents/forms/sample_import_permit_and_submission_form.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi4119.pdf>) · [bwcimplementation.org 4](<https://bwcimplementation.org/sites/default/files/resource/chap58-40.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi111807.pdf>) · [customs.gov.dm 6](<https://customs.gov.dm/trade/prohibited-and-restricted-items>) · [agriculture.gov.dm 7](<https://agriculture.gov.dm/images/documents/forms/application_for_import_permit.pdf>) · [printery.dominica.gov.dm 8](<https://printery.dominica.gov.dm/ProductDocuments/DownloadPreview/7908>) · [printery.dominica.gov.dm 9](<https://printery.dominica.gov.dm/ProductDocuments/DownloadPreview/7913>) · [dominica.gov.dm 10](<https://www.dominica.gov.dm/images/docs/bills/food_safety_bill_2026.pdf>) · [dominica.gov.dm 11](<https://dominica.gov.dm/laws/1997/act08-1997.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 12](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi87508.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [gov.si 14](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [pressroomopm.gov.dm 16](<https://pressroomopm.gov.dm/parliament-passes-food-safety-bill-to-strengthen-public-health-and-protect-consumers/>)

Conditional support by applying the domestic pest-capability definition to factory-steamed, rolled and dried plain sencha. Act 10/1986 section 2 restricts plant products only when capable of harbouring or transporting plant pests; its other botanical categories concern propagative material and raw/unprocessed fruits or vegetables. Saved Importations Regulations 8(g) and their printed Explanation recognize cooked/parboiled/otherwise processed pest-free products as unlikely to introduce injurious organisms. Current-linked ISPM 32 Appendix 2 identifies tea as Category 1, providing manufacturing evidence rather than an automatically incorporated foreign exemption. Taken together, these support treating uncontaminated industrial retail sencha in clean new packaging as outside restricted-article scope. Regulation 8 itself waives regulation 4 entry/inspection only, not Act 3–4 permits/certificates. The no-document conclusion is an explicit combined-source scope inference, not a tea-specific authority ruling or a claim that every dried leaf is exempt.

**Personal post:** Act 10 explicitly covers restricted articles in the mail; this conclusion instead applies the general restricted-article scope to the actual manufactured product, irrespective of postal purchase. It does not borrow a passenger allowance. Ordinary customs declarations and arrival enforcement remain.

**Checkout:** 182 pouches / 18.2 kg tea; highest carrier band 22 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [establishment.gov.dm 1](<https://establishment.gov.dm/services/application-import-permit>) · [divisionofagriculture.gov.dm 2](<https://divisionofagriculture.gov.dm/images/documents/forms/sample_import_permit_and_submission_form.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi4119.pdf>) · [bwcimplementation.org 4](<https://bwcimplementation.org/sites/default/files/resource/chap58-40.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi111807.pdf>) · [customs.gov.dm 6](<https://customs.gov.dm/trade/prohibited-and-restricted-items>) · [agriculture.gov.dm 7](<https://agriculture.gov.dm/images/documents/forms/application_for_import_permit.pdf>) · [printery.dominica.gov.dm 8](<https://printery.dominica.gov.dm/ProductDocuments/DownloadPreview/7908>) · [printery.dominica.gov.dm 9](<https://printery.dominica.gov.dm/ProductDocuments/DownloadPreview/7913>) · [dominica.gov.dm 10](<https://www.dominica.gov.dm/images/docs/bills/food_safety_bill_2026.pdf>) · [dominica.gov.dm 11](<https://dominica.gov.dm/laws/1997/act08-1997.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 12](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/dmi87508.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [gov.si 14](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [pressroomopm.gov.dm 16](<https://pressroomopm.gov.dm/parliament-passes-food-safety-bill-to-strengthen-public-health-and-protect-consumers/>)

### Dominican Republic (DO)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude under the no-special-paperwork constraint: Law 42-01 arts 127/129 impose food-import authorization/certification, and current DIGEMAPS explicitly lists retail green tea for prior no-objection. Applying that default absent a verified private-post exception is an inference; commercial wording in the implementing procedure is recorded, not concealed.

**Document-rule sources:** [agricultura.gob.do 1](<https://agricultura.gob.do/servicios/informacion-sobre-procedimientos-para-la-importacion-de-productos-y-sub-productos-de-origen-vegetal/>) · [vucerd.gob.do 2](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2219/exclusi%C3%B3n-de-subpartidas-de-sanidad-vegetal.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 3](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2234/listado-salida-de-inspecci%C3%B3n-sanidad-vegetal.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 4](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2438/listado-de-subpartidas-reguladas-a-trav%C3%A9s-del-formulario-de-no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-productos-alimenticios-industrializados-preenvasados-previo-2026.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 5](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2432/servicio-de-no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-alimentos-industrializados-preenvasados-previo.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 6](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2264/importaci%C3%B3n-de-productos-de-consumo-personal.pdf>) · [sisalril.gob.do 7](<https://www.sisalril.gob.do/transparencia/phocadownload/BaseLegal/leyes/ley_no_42-01.pdf>) · [proconsumidor.gob.do 8](<https://proconsumidor.gob.do/files/Decreto_No__528-01_de_fecha_14_de_mayo_del_ao_2001_Reglamento_General_para_Control_de_Riesgos_en_Alimentos_y_Bebidas_en_la_Repblica_Dominicana.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 9](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2218/no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-alimentos-industrializados-preenvasados-previo.pdf>) · [aduanas.gob.do 10](<https://www.aduanas.gob.do/media/l40czkdv/decreto-755-22-reglamento-de-aplicacion-de-la-ley-num-168-21-de-aduanas.pdf>)

Law 42-01 articles 127/129 subject food importation to health control and require authorization and origin-health certification without a personal-use qualifier. The 7-Sep-2026 DIGEMAPS schedule expressly places retail green tea HS 09021000 under prior food no-objection. On those enacted/default and exact-product rules, a declaration-only route is not supported. This is a legal-scope inference: Decree 528-01 and the current procedure also contain commercial-importer wording; no applicable private-post waiver was verified. Agricultural treatment remains separately unresolved.

**Personal post:** The conclusion uses Law 42-01 food-import wording and the current exact-tea schedule, not traveler allowances. Personal-consumption procedure ICP-PD-01 covers specified supplements/alcohol, not plain tea. Customs low-value courier relief preserves restricted-goods authorizations; it is not proof of a postal food waiver.

**Checkout:** 207 pouches / 20.7 kg tea; highest carrier band 25 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No declaration-only route supported under the published food-import default and exact-tea schedule. A specific operative personal-post exemption could alter this result, but none was verified. Separate agricultural classification need not be resolved to exclude shipping under the no-special-paperwork constraint.

**Sources:** [agricultura.gob.do 1](<https://agricultura.gob.do/servicios/informacion-sobre-procedimientos-para-la-importacion-de-productos-y-sub-productos-de-origen-vegetal/>) · [vucerd.gob.do 2](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2219/exclusi%C3%B3n-de-subpartidas-de-sanidad-vegetal.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 3](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2234/listado-salida-de-inspecci%C3%B3n-sanidad-vegetal.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 4](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2438/listado-de-subpartidas-reguladas-a-trav%C3%A9s-del-formulario-de-no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-productos-alimenticios-industrializados-preenvasados-previo-2026.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 5](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2432/servicio-de-no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-alimentos-industrializados-preenvasados-previo.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 6](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2264/importaci%C3%B3n-de-productos-de-consumo-personal.pdf>) · [sisalril.gob.do 7](<https://www.sisalril.gob.do/transparencia/phocadownload/BaseLegal/leyes/ley_no_42-01.pdf>) · [proconsumidor.gob.do 8](<https://proconsumidor.gob.do/files/Decreto_No__528-01_de_fecha_14_de_mayo_del_ao_2001_Reglamento_General_para_Control_de_Riesgos_en_Alimentos_y_Bebidas_en_la_Repblica_Dominicana.pdf>) · [vucerd.gob.do 9](<https://vucerd.gob.do/media/2218/no-objeci%C3%B3n-de-alimentos-industrializados-preenvasados-previo.pdf>) · [aduanas.gob.do 10](<https://www.aduanas.gob.do/media/l40czkdv/decreto-755-22-reglamento-de-aplicacion-de-la-ley-num-168-21-de-aduanas.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed ministry import procedure and 2025 VUCE phytosanitary exclusion/inspection lists; attempted the SIREVUCE commodity lookup. No matching personal postal sencha exemption verified. 2026-09-30: Retrieved 7-Sep-2026 DIGEMAPS prior no-objection HS schedule through the same official VUCE public Azure host. Its first item is 09021000 retail green tea <=3 kg. August notice moves packaged-food no-objection exclusively to prior procedure. Read all 13 pages of July 2025 personal-consumption procedure ICP-PD-01: food section 5.2 covers supplements and alcoholic drinks specifically, not plain tea. It cannot alone establish either an exemption or required personal-food permit for ordinary sencha. 2026-09-30: Read official Law 42-01 arts 109 and 125–131, particularly unqualified food-import authorization/certification in art 129. Read Decree 528-01 arts 2 and 5–10: commercial scope prevents using that decree alone as proof of private-post requirements. Retrieved and OCR-read current AIP-PO-01 revision 04-Feb-2025: general packaged-food scope but checks an importing company and sanitary registration. Cross-checked exact 090210 Sep 2026 schedule and Decree 755-22 customs restricted-goods exclusions from low-value courier treatment. Classified not_supported as an explicit inference from the higher-level enacted food default and exact current product schedule; no personal waiver assumed and no agricultural permit requirement invented. 2026-09-30: Normalized the separate agricultural status to not_verified: the operational exclusion rests on food-import authorization/certification, not a demonstrated plant-health permit for manufactured tea.

<a id="ec"></a>
### Ecuador (EC)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Do not ship under the volunteer-store policy: the published route requires a prior permit/authorization or special regulatory documents. Ecuador’s national COMEX 009-2022 row 616 explicitly requires an Agrocalidad import permit and DDA for retail green tea (090210). The quarantine manual requires operator registration, prior permit, origin certification and arrival inspection for regulated postal consignments. A processed-product exemption requires an Agrocalidad categorization decision; it cannot be assumed from the general Andean category alone.

**Document-rule sources:** [agrocalidad.gob.ec 1](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/2RESOL1.pdf>) · [agrocalidad.gob.ec 2](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Resol_133_Manual-general-de-cuarentena-vegetal.pdf>) · [agrocalidad.gob.ec 3](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/direccion-de-control-fitosanitario/>)

Ecuador’s national COMEX 009-2022 row 616 explicitly requires an Agrocalidad import permit and DDA for retail green tea (090210). The quarantine manual requires operator registration, prior permit, origin certification and arrival inspection for regulated postal consignments. A processed-product exemption requires an Agrocalidad categorization decision; it cannot be assumed from the general Andean category alone.

**Personal post:** Quarantine manual section 9 expressly covers natural persons using postal/courier services. The separate 1 kg traveler allowance is not applied to mail.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Request Agrocalidad categorization for this manufactured sencha if seeking exemption; absent that decision follow the retail-green-tea permit entry.

**Sources:** [agrocalidad.gob.ec 1](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/2RESOL1.pdf>) · [agrocalidad.gob.ec 2](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Resol_133_Manual-general-de-cuarentena-vegetal.pdf>) · [agrocalidad.gob.ec 3](<https://www.agrocalidad.gob.ec/direccion-de-control-fitosanitario/>)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

<a id="eg"></a>
### Egypt (EG)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The personal-consignment exemption waives both permit and phytosanitary certificate up to 50 kg for admitted non-propagating products. The current CAPQ database now supplies the missing admissibility evidence for Japanese green tea. A sealed 100 g sencha packet for private consumption fits the scope; normal postal/customs declarations and possible official inspection remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/09/24/eg3-2019-562pqreg_en.pdf>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 2](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/Index>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 3](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/GetItemsByInitiator?ImInitiatorID=83>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 4](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/GetConstraints?ImInitiatorID=83&ItemID=955>)

Plant Quarantine Regulation 562/2019 Annex 10 item 11 exempts personal-use consignments of admissible items weighing no more than 50 kg from both import permits and phytosanitary certificates, excluding propagation material. CAPQ’s current commodity/origin database lists Japan (83), Tea Tree/Green Tea (955) with entry requirements, confirming this product/origin is admitted.

**Personal post:** Regulation 562 explicitly covers imported postal parcels, and Annex 10 item 11 covers personal-use consignments rather than only passenger baggage. The general database’s certificate/permit requirements are displaced by this specific personal-use exemption. Pests must still be absent and arrival inspection remains possible.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 50 kg product weight. Personal-use consignment exemption from permit and phytosanitary certificate; applies only to items already allowed to be imported.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. Current maximum is within this numeric threshold; this does not settle permits, value limits, frequency, packaging or genuine personal use.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/09/24/eg3-2019-562pqreg_en.pdf>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 2](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/Index>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 3](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/GetItemsByInitiator?ImInitiatorID=83>) · [site.capq.gov.eg 4](<https://site.capq.gov.eg/ImportingProcedure/GetConstraints?ImInitiatorID=83&ItemID=955>)

<a id="er"></a>
### Eritrea (ER)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported conditionally for the stated 100 g sealed industrially steamed, rolled and dried plain Japanese Camellia tea by the domestic risk-qualified definition and operative PRA/international-standard provisions together with ISPM 32 tea Category 1. Storage/transport surveillance in Articles 4(2)(c) and 7 and suspicion powers in Article 8 do not themselves require permits for every manufactured object; Article 3(27) still requires pest-carrying capability. Require clean sealed packaging without soil, seeds or raw plant additives. Separate L.N.78/2003 Article 3 commercial import permits and its L.N.100/2005 banking/amended permission provisions are read in the commercial-dealer context of parent Proclamation 125/2002 Article 3(b–c), rather than extended solely because the private order was paid. Tea price-control listing is not a separate personal import certificate. Ordinary food safety/arrival inspection remain. Article 3(15) defines packaging material by use to pack/contain/keep plant material or regulated articles, and 3(5) defines containers by covered goods they carry or have carried. Clean new packaging enclosing nonregulated manufactured tea is therefore not automatically that defined packaging category. This domestic definition chain, rather than an ISPM 32 waiver of post-processing contamination, limits the route. Reused agricultural containers, wood/soil contamination or actual pest-capable material can change the conclusion.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eri68047.pdf>) · [shabait.com 2](<https://shabait.com/2026/05/13/international-day-of-plant-health-observed-at-national-and-international-levels/>) · [shabait.com 3](<https://shabait.com/2025/05/29/encouraging-results-in-ensuring-health-of-agricultural-production/>) · [ippc.int 4](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/eritrea/reportingobligation/4>) · [shabait.com 5](<https://shabait.com/2026/07/12/workshop-on-developing-national-food-control-policy/>) · [shabait.com 6](<https://shabait.com/2010/03/26/maintaining-a-standard-of-products-to-secure-national-interests/>) · [tile.loc.gov 7](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-78-2003/eritrean-notice-78-2003.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eri42427E.pdf>) · [mojeritrea.net 9](<https://mojeritrea.net/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/Thematic-Categorization-of-Domestic-Legislations-of-the-State-of-Eritrea.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 10](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-76-2003/eritrean-notice-76-2003.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 11](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-proc-125-2002/eritrean-proc-125-2002.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 12](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-100-2005/eritrean-notice-100-2005.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 14](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Explicit combined-source legal interpretation: Proclamation 156/2006 Articles 3(20–22,27) covers living plants, risk-capable manufactured plant products and pest-capable regulated articles. Articles 4(2)(g–h) require restricted lists and phytosanitary measures to be based/justified on PRA or applicable international standards; Article 17 repeats that standard for restrictions. Applied with current-linked ISPM 32 Category 1 tea evidence and the actual industrial steaming/rolling/drying, clean sealed retail sencha is inferred outside the product class triggering Articles 11,14–15 permits/certificates. The law is confirmed current by the Ministry of Information in May 2026. This is a national-law/processing interpretation, not an individual Eritrean tea ruling.

**Personal post:** The product-scope conclusion applies to private paid post, not just travelers. Article 11(4) expressly covers the post office when goods are regulated. Ordinary customs declarations, lawful arrival inspection and action against actual/suspected contamination remain. The private recipient is an end consumer, not a commercial dealer/importer under the separately reviewed trade law.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Read full Proclamation 125/2002 English text from the official Library of Congress scan: Article 3(b) dealer means a person engaged in commercial activity, and 3(c) importer means a dealer importing goods. L.N.78/2003 Article 3(1) requires permits for commercial goods; Article 5 designates tea for price control. L.N.100/2005 changes Article 4(2) bank checks and 4(3) case-by-case permission for development/basic-consumer/other important goods, but does not remove Article 3 commercial scope; Article 4 remains mode-of-using-import-permit. In context these provisions do not impose a dealer licence or commercial import permission on the private end consumer solely because the shipment is purchased. The July 2026 national food-policy workshop is not enacted certificate legislation; the official standards description concerns arrival inspection/issued quality certificates, not a separately imposed private pre-shipment document.

**Evidence access:** Official Ministry of Information indexed reports read. The 2026 policy-drafting report is current but not a law; the 2010 interview is historical operational context only.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eri68047.pdf>) · [shabait.com 2](<https://shabait.com/2026/05/13/international-day-of-plant-health-observed-at-national-and-international-levels/>) · [shabait.com 3](<https://shabait.com/2025/05/29/encouraging-results-in-ensuring-health-of-agricultural-production/>) · [ippc.int 4](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/eritrea/reportingobligation/4>) · [shabait.com 5](<https://shabait.com/2026/07/12/workshop-on-developing-national-food-control-policy/>) · [shabait.com 6](<https://shabait.com/2010/03/26/maintaining-a-standard-of-products-to-secure-national-interests/>) · [tile.loc.gov 7](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-78-2003/eritrean-notice-78-2003.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eri42427E.pdf>) · [mojeritrea.net 9](<https://mojeritrea.net/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/Thematic-Categorization-of-Domestic-Legislations-of-the-State-of-Eritrea.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 10](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-76-2003/eritrean-notice-76-2003.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 11](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-proc-125-2002/eritrean-proc-125-2002.pdf>) · [tile.loc.gov 12](<https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/lleritrea/eritrean-notice-100-2005/eritrean-notice-100-2005.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 14](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

<a id="et"></a>
### Ethiopia (ET)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Conservative exclusion for the stated paid public-store sale under current Food and Medicine Administration Proclamation 1112/2019 Articles 3 and 10(1). Article 3 covers food intended for market placement or otherwise offered for public use; a retail product publicly sold by the store and supplied on purchase to an Ethiopian consumer is reasonably treated as market supply even though the consumer will not resell it. Article 10(1) then requires the executive organ's import permit, which this store will not obtain. This is a disclosed legal-scope inference of moderate confidence, not an express personal-postal or tea-specific prohibition. The contrary reading confines market placement to future domestic resale and the older directive to Ethiopian food-trade companies. Directive 22/2014 Article 8(1) waives personal-consumption competence licensing only; it does not establish a blanket food-import waiver. Its Article 20 origin/health certificates are not independently treated as an unequivocal private duty. The agricultural manufactured-product interpretation is favorable and kept separate.

**Document-rule sources:** [justice.gov.et 1](<https://justice.gov.et/en/law/plant-protection-and-quarantine-proclamation-no-1400-2026/>) · [justice.gov.et 2](<https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/አዋጅ-ቁጥር-1400-2018.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth208821.pdf>) · [justice.gov.et 4](<https://justice.gov.et/en/law/food-and-medicine-administration-proclamation/>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth216290.pdf>) · [eris.efda.gov.et 6](<https://eris.efda.gov.et/>) · [apps.fas.usda.gov 7](<https://apps.fas.usda.gov/newgainapi/api/Report/DownloadReportByFileName?fileName=Ethiopia%27s+Food+Importers-Exporters+and+Wholesalers+Directive_Addis+Ababa_Ethiopia_12-1-2015.pdf>) · [apps.fas.usda.gov 8](<https://apps.fas.usda.gov/newgainapi/api/Report/DownloadReportByFileName?fileName=FAIRS+Country+Report+Annual_Addis+Ababa_Ethiopia_ET2025-0021.pdf>) · [justice.gov.et 9](<https://justice.gov.et/en/entity/ethiopian-coffee-and-tea-authority/>) · [justice.gov.et 10](<https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/አዋጅ-ቁጥር-1400-2018-1.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 11](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth4524.pdf>) · [eaa.gov.et 12](<https://eaa.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/plant-protection-decree-561971.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 14](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Explicit combined-source interpretation: Proclamation 1400/2026 Articles 2(1–2,7),3 and 4 confine plant import documents to living plants, pest-risk plant products and pest-capable articles deemed to require measures. Actual clean steam-manufactured sencha is inferred outside that class, using current-linked ISPM 32 tea Category 1 as technical evidence. Article 25(2) disapplies inconsistent older breadth; Article 26 preserves Regulation 4/1992. Its Article 3 likewise uses infestation liability and ScheduleI item 11 names Camellia sinensis without specifically regulating manufactured tea. The former 1971 unqualified natural/dried/manufactured definition cannot automatically override the 2026 risk qualification. This is a legal/processing inference, with the botanical-list counterargument preserved in the peer review, not an Ethiopian commodity ruling.

**Personal post:** The plant scope inference applies regardless of private postal transport; no passenger/gift exemption is being borrowed. Ordinary customs and arrival inspection remain. No raw additives, pests, soil, straw or wood packaging. Independent private-food scope is being reviewed separately.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Current Proclamation 1112/2019 Article 3 and Article 10(1) together support conservative exclusion for this paid retail supply: the statutory product market/public-use scope does not expressly require recipient resale. The own-consumption recipient/company-only reading is a substantial contrary interpretation and is preserved in foodScopePeerReview; no tea-specific or expressly private-postal ruling is claimed. Regulation 531/2023 defines agency functions and does not itself add an unqualified private duty. Directive 22/2014 Articles 3–4 concern food-trade companies; Article 8(1) expressly exempts personal-consumption imports from competence certification, while Article 20(4) separately lists health/origin certificates. The latter are cautionary contrary evidence to a blanket private exemption, not the independent conclusive reason for excluding this parcel. Article 70 of the 2019 law disapplies inconsistent prior instruments; Article 73 is an ethics-body continuity clause, not a personal-food exemption.

**Evidence access:** Complete FAO-hosted Gazette law and 2023 agency regulation downloaded; article 10 visually verified because PDF OCR is poor. Ministry of Justice labels 1112/2019 in force. Personal medical/supplement exceptions and customs tax allowances were not extended to ordinary tea.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [justice.gov.et 1](<https://justice.gov.et/en/law/plant-protection-and-quarantine-proclamation-no-1400-2026/>) · [justice.gov.et 2](<https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/አዋጅ-ቁጥር-1400-2018.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth208821.pdf>) · [justice.gov.et 4](<https://justice.gov.et/en/law/food-and-medicine-administration-proclamation/>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth216290.pdf>) · [eris.efda.gov.et 6](<https://eris.efda.gov.et/>) · [apps.fas.usda.gov 7](<https://apps.fas.usda.gov/newgainapi/api/Report/DownloadReportByFileName?fileName=Ethiopia%27s+Food+Importers-Exporters+and+Wholesalers+Directive_Addis+Ababa_Ethiopia_12-1-2015.pdf>) · [apps.fas.usda.gov 8](<https://apps.fas.usda.gov/newgainapi/api/Report/DownloadReportByFileName?fileName=FAIRS+Country+Report+Annual_Addis+Ababa_Ethiopia_ET2025-0021.pdf>) · [justice.gov.et 9](<https://justice.gov.et/en/entity/ethiopian-coffee-and-tea-authority/>) · [justice.gov.et 10](<https://justice.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/አዋጅ-ቁጥር-1400-2018-1.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 11](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/eth4524.pdf>) · [eaa.gov.et 12](<https://eaa.gov.et/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/plant-protection-decree-561971.pdf>) · [ippc.int 13](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 14](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

<a id="fm"></a>
### Micronesia (FM)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for personal-consumption tea within the 2 kg total other-food allowance, in fully manufactured hermetically sealed shelf-stable packaging. The plant schedule imposes inspection but no certificate for stored dried food; the personal-food exemption removes separate food-document requirements. Ordinary declaration and postal/customs quarantine clearance remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [fsmlaw.org 1](<https://www.fsmlaw.org/fsm/regulations/pi_iii.htm>) · [fsmlaw.org 2](<https://www.fsmlaw.org/fsm/regulations/piv_x.htm>) · [fsmlaw.org 3](<https://fsmlaw.org/fsm/code/code2014/pdf/FSMCode2014Tit22Chap04.pdf>) · [hsa.gov.fm 4](<https://hsa.gov.fm/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/DHS-Regulation-7-Food-Safety-Standards_compressed.pdf>)

Plant and Animal Quarantine Regulation 20(g)(i) exempts fully manufactured, hermetically sealed shelf-stable foods from import permits. Part IV, QP 8 subjects stored dried foods to inspection/treatment if contaminated without imposing a phytosanitary certificate. DHS Food Safety Standards 2022, 8.4.1 and 8.4.5, exempt personal-consumption food from food-legislation requirements up to 2 kg total per person for other foods. The actual tea pouch must meet the hermetic-seal condition.

**Personal post:** The exemption applies to importation generally; it is not limited to passenger baggage.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 2 kg product weight. Total other foods per person. DHS Regulation No. 7 Food Safety Standards 2022, 8.4.5: other food for personal/family consumption, total per person; includes any other foods imported together.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. Current checkout exceeds the cited personal/exemption route. Reduce the cap; a larger route requiring permits or certificates is unavailable to this store.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions. The current maximum order is outside the reviewed threshold.

**To resolve:** Confirm the actual manufacturer packaging is hermetically sealed; a loosely closed or resealable-only pouch is insufficient evidence.

**Sources:** [fsmlaw.org 1](<https://www.fsmlaw.org/fsm/regulations/pi_iii.htm>) · [fsmlaw.org 2](<https://www.fsmlaw.org/fsm/regulations/piv_x.htm>) · [fsmlaw.org 3](<https://fsmlaw.org/fsm/code/code2014/pdf/FSMCode2014Tit22Chap04.pdf>) · [hsa.gov.fm 4](<https://hsa.gov.fm/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/DHS-Regulation-7-Food-Safety-Standards_compressed.pdf>)

<a id="ga"></a>
### Gabon (GA)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Food-import requirements provide an independent document obstacle: Order 426/2005 Article 2 covers all food-import operations; Articles 21–24 require a prior signed food-import declaration and an import-release authorization with original origin and health documents. AGASA continues to publish food-certification requirements and explicitly includes private individuals in the registration process. No personal postal exception was verified. This is a food-document exclusion; the separate plant-health tea category remains unresolved.

**Document-rule sources:** [sda.agasa.tech 1](<https://www.sda.agasa.tech/assets/juridique/Arrete%20426.pdf>) · [agasa.site 2](<https://www.agasa.site/wp-content/uploads/2021/01/Procedure-de-certification-1.pdf>) · [agasa.site 3](<https://www.agasa.site/wp-content/uploads/2020/07/avis-aux-op%C3%A9rateurs-02.pdf>)

Law 7/77 article 5 separates prohibited, permit-controlled and certificate-controlled products, with a packaging-mark alternative in one category. The current commodity tables needed to place dried tea were not located.

**Personal post:** Fresh-plant traveller guidance does not determine a manufactured-tea postal parcel.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** The store cannot use the documented food-import approval/certificate route. The phytosanitary commodity category remains unclassified, but clearing it alone would not remove the separate food-document requirements.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2020/02/24/Loi_n_7-77.pdf>) · [douanes.ga 2](<https://douanes.ga/individuals/traveler-s-guide>)

**Research trail:** OCR-read the 1977 law and searched current category tables, tea and personal-post conditions. Read AGASA-hosted Order 426 of 21 March 2005 Articles 2 and 21–24. Found current-published certification guidance including private individuals; its detailed leaflet is maritime-specific and is corroboration of agency procedure, not the sole basis for postal applicability. The Order itself covers all food-import operations without a personal quantity exception.

Processed packaged plant products may enter Great Britain without a phytosanitary certificate; plain steamed/dried sencha meets that description. Northern Ireland applies the EU dried-tea exemption. This is a personal-consumption route; comply with the stated packaging and quantity conditions and ordinary postal/customs declarations.

Processed packaged plant products may enter Great Britain without a phytosanitary certificate; plain steamed/dried sencha meets that description. Northern Ireland applies the EU dried-tea exemption.

**Personal post:** The UK plant-import overview explicitly covers personal goods ordered by post. The processed-product exemption is commodity-based, not a weight allowance for baggage.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

<a id="gd"></a>
### Grenada (GD)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude under the no-certificate constraint by explicit inference from Food and Drugs Act Cap.110 section 33(2): an origin-law/lawful-sale document is required for imported food. The 2015 Food Safety Act exempts private consumption from its own scope, but does not expressly repeal the separate Cap.110 duty. This is a legal-scope conclusion with statutory-overlap and 2015-commencement qualifications, not a tea-specific or phytosanitary ruling.

**Document-rule sources:** [wto.org 1](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s299-02_e.pdf>) · [ippc.int 2](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/grenada/>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 3](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=3535&menuid=28&reporeid=197>) · [cahfsa.org 4](<https://cahfsa.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Grenada_Plant-protection-act_1986.pdf>) · [grenadaparliament.gd 5](<https://grenadaparliament.gd/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/Cap110-FOOD-AND-DRUGS-ACT.pdf>) · [grenadaparliament.gd 6](<https://grenadaparliament.gd/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/Act-No.-19-of-2015-Food-Safety-Act.pdf>) · [laws.gov.gd 7](<https://laws.gov.gd/index.php/map-of-folders>)

Food and Drugs Act Cap.110 (Act 14/1986), section 33(2), requires imported food to conform to origin-country law and be accompanied by an authentic document certifying that conformity and lawful sale. Section 2 defines food and importer broadly. Applying this unqualified duty to a private sencha parcel is an explicit legal-scope inference, not a tea-specific ruling. The newer Food Safety Act 19/2015 section 4(2)(a) excludes food imported for private consumption, so its own section 46 notice/certificate route is not applied here. It contains no express repeal of Cap.110, and section 46(4) preserves duties under other relevant enactments. The 2015 Act’s commencement notice and authoritative treatment of the overlapping statutes were not independently verified. Separately, the manufactured-tea plant-product classification remains unresolved; regulation 8(i) only exempts processed pest-free products from specified entry/inspection rules.

**Personal post:** The Cap.110 certificate conclusion is inferred from an unqualified imported-food duty and broad importer definition, with no published automatic personal-post exception established. The express private-consumption exclusion in the separate 2015 Act is preserved and prevents applying that Act’s own import-notice/certificate duty to this parcel.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No ordinary-declaration-only route supported under the independently published Cap.110 food-certificate default. An operative exemption or authoritative reconciliation displacing that duty for private imports could change the result; none was established. Plant-health tea classification and the 2015 Act’s commencement remain unverified but are not presented as the basis for exclusion.

**Sources:** [wto.org 1](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s299-02_e.pdf>) · [ippc.int 2](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/grenada/>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 3](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=3535&menuid=28&reporeid=197>) · [cahfsa.org 4](<https://cahfsa.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/Grenada_Plant-protection-act_1986.pdf>) · [grenadaparliament.gd 5](<https://grenadaparliament.gd/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/Cap110-FOOD-AND-DRUGS-ACT.pdf>) · [grenadaparliament.gd 6](<https://grenadaparliament.gd/wp-content/uploads/2021/08/Act-No.-19-of-2015-Food-Safety-Act.pdf>) · [laws.gov.gd 7](<https://laws.gov.gd/index.php/map-of-folders>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed official NPPO profile and government-notified WTO description of Grenada plant import controls; searched manufactured-tea and personal postal exceptions. No commodity-specific conclusion established. Retrieved/read surviving Plant Protection Regulations. Regulation 8(i) exempts cooked/parboiled/otherwise processed pest-free plant products from regulation 4 (entry/declaration/inspection), and 8(h) specifies roasted coffee. This is not an express exemption from the primary Act permit/certificate provisions; no finished-tea classification was established. Original Act 19/1986 on CAHFSA defines plant products by pest-carrying capability and explicitly covers mail; current consolidated Cap.242 unavailable from government site (406). 2026-09-30: Read full official Parliament texts of Cap.110 (including broad section 2 definitions, section 33(2), and its statement that there is no subsidiary legislation) and Food Safety Act 19/2015. Explicitly preserved 2015 section 4(2)(a) private-consumption exclusion; section 46(4) makes its duties additional to other enactments and does not relieve those duties. Found no express repeal of Cap.110 in the 2015 Act; the government law index continues to list Chapter 110, although direct current-map retrieval returned an access error. No 2015 commencement notice or published personal-post exemption under Cap.110 established. Classified only the independent food duty as a no-document exclusion by legal-scope inference; left agricultural status unverified. Parent independently reviewed both primary texts and the overlap qualification.
