<a id="gf"></a>
### French Guiana (GF)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

The expressly adopted risk-qualified manufactured-product definition and ISPM 32 tea Category 1 provide favorable processing evidence. However, current DGAL/SDSPV/2026-165 retains pre-2019 overseas implementation despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop; the actual local-order scope/compatibility remains unresolved. Order R 03-2021-04-26-00003 Article 1, read through the prior legal-text reproduction cross-checked with its official index, applies personal introductions/imports in any quantity and origin using 1990 Article 1 definitions. Article 2 requires a studied derogation. This is a concrete broad local measure, not a missing tea line in a certificate schedule. Newer risk-qualified statutory scope is a material counterargument, but the 2026 implementation position expressly retains old rules and the manufactured/simple-preparation compatibility remains unresolved. Official PDF retrieval remains unavailable.

**Document-rule sources:** [guyane.gouv.fr 1](<https://www.guyane.gouv.fr/content/download/18708/131303/file/recueil-r03-2021-109-recueil-des-actes-administratifs.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 2](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000000343648/>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 3](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102/telechargement>) · [politique.pappers.fr 4](<https://politique.pappers.fr/commune/document/0987289975315402799780b56d718d4c>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000039297041/2026-07-22>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 8](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/2031/oj?locale=fr>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 9](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/FR/TXT/?uri=CELEX%3A02016R2031-20250105>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 11](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/supima/8b94ce04-a4a9-4f01-a12f-3fba8de8f85a>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 12](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 13](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-278/telechargement>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Guyane Order R 03-2021-04-26-00003 Article 1 prohibits personal introduction/importation from all origins in any quantity using the 1990 Article 1 definitions. It is not limited to goods in the national certificate schedules. Article 2 permits derogation only following prior study. Whether steaming/manufacturing removes retail sencha from the unprocessed/simply-prepared product definition remains unresolved. Current Code rural expressly adopts a risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. Its statutory 2020 commencement conflicts with current Ministry 2026-165 guidance retaining the older framework; neither claim is omitted. This does not establish a tea prohibition.

**Personal post:** The order covers personal introduction/importation broadly, not merely a passenger allowance; no postal exemption established.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Order R 03-2021-04-26-00003 Article 1, read through the prior legal-text reproduction cross-checked with its official index, applies personal introductions/imports in any quantity and origin using 1990 Article 1 definitions. Article 2 requires a studied derogation. This is a concrete broad local measure, not a missing tea line in a certificate schedule. Newer risk-qualified statutory scope is a material counterargument, but the 2026 implementation position expressly retains old rules and the manufactured/simple-preparation compatibility remains unresolved. Official PDF retrieval remains unavailable. Current statute and ministry implementation conflict; a general published processing/implementation clarification suffices without a bespoke tea letter.

**Sources:** [guyane.gouv.fr 1](<https://www.guyane.gouv.fr/content/download/18708/131303/file/recueil-r03-2021-109-recueil-des-actes-administratifs.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 2](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000000343648/>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 3](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102/telechargement>) · [politique.pappers.fr 4](<https://politique.pappers.fr/commune/document/0987289975315402799780b56d718d4c>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000039297041/2026-07-22>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 8](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/2031/oj?locale=fr>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 9](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/FR/TXT/?uri=CELEX%3A02016R2031-20250105>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 11](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/supima/8b94ce04-a4a9-4f01-a12f-3fba8de8f85a>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 12](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 13](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-278/telechargement>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read the 1990 overseas rule and 2025 ministry instruction retaining pre-2019 law pending an overseas decree. Located the official 2021 personal-import order; the official PDF was inaccessible, so checked its reproduced legal text (articles 1–2) against the official indexed title and number. No 2025/2026 processed-tea exemption found. 2026-09-30 bounded independent French-territory peer review: Rechecked the reproduced operative Article 1 of Order R 03-2021-04-26-00003 against its official title/index. It applies the 1990 Article 1 definitions to personal imports from all origins in any quantity; it does not limit itself to Annex IV certificate-listed goods. Article 2 requires prior study for a derogation. Consequently the national exhaustive schedule cannot itself clear this personal import. Direct official PDF retrieval again closed the connection; the operative wording remains a legal-text reproduction, not an independently downloaded official copy. 30 September fresh domestic-definition review: read currentL 271-7 and 2019-1110 original/currentArticle 6, ratification by 2023-171 Art 39, and official indexed 2026-165/2026-278 implementation statements. Verified 2025-102 abrogated, with 2026-165 current. New actualTeaCategory 1 evidence supports a general processing inference, but present statute/agency implementation and concrete local-rule scope conflict persists; not merely membership or a missing bespoke tea ruling. Source/territory findings in french-definition-review/peer-conclusions.json.

<a id="gi"></a>
### Gibraltar (GI)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

EU plant-health clearance now applies under Gibraltar’s enacted customs-union legislation. Manufactured dried tea ordered by a private individual is outside the EU phytosanitary-certificate requirement. Private postal organic imports for consumption are also exempt from the commercial organic-import certificate. Ordinary customs/postal declarations and taxes remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [gibraltarlaws.gov.gi 1](<https://gibraltarlaws.gov.gi/uploads/legislations/treaty-on-gibraltar/2026-11/2026-11(15-07-26).pdf>) · [gibraltarlaws.gov.gi 2](<https://www.gibraltarlaws.gov.gi/legislations/notice-of-commencement-treaty-on-gibraltar-and-the-european-union-act-2026-8492>) · [gibraltar.gov.gi 3](<https://www.gibraltar.gov.gi/press-releases/another-historic-day-for-gibraltar-as-checks-removed-from-frontier-5612026-12204>) · [jordbruksverket.se 4](<https://jordbruksverket.se/languages/english/swedish-board-of-agriculture/plants/bringing-in-or-ordering-plants-and-plant-products-to-sweden-as-a-private-individual>) · [post.gi 5](<https://post.gi/customs-requirements/>) · [findok.bmf.gv.at 6](<https://findok.bmf.gv.at/findok/iwg/80/80664/80664.3.pdf>)

Current-law classification: Gibraltar implemented its EU customs-union arrangements on 15 July 2026. Sections 81 and 90 of the Treaty Act apply Union sanitary/phytosanitary entry formalities to third-country goods. Under the corresponding EU commodity rule, manufactured dried tea requires no phytosanitary certificate; the Swedish authority expressly confirms this for private orders.

**Personal post:** This applies to third-country goods generally, including postal purchases; it is not the passenger-baggage exception. Gibraltar Post requires the ordinary postal customs declaration. The personal-use organic-import exception also avoids an organic certificate of inspection for a recipient consuming the tea privately.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for sealed plain Japanese manufactured tea for the recipient’s own consumption. Current Greenland-specific guidance expressly admits non-animal food sent to individuals. The surviving vegetation permit provision concerns foreign hay fodder, heather and wild vegetation; cultivated processed sencha is outside it. No special plant-health permit/certificate or private-food registration is identified for this route; ordinary customs declarations and charges remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 1](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/indgange/groenland/groenlandske-foedevarer/mad-med-paa-rejsen>) · [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 2](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/lovstof/groenland-lovstof>) · [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 3](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/Media/638139536842116037/Regulativaf1948.pdf>) · [ft.dk 4](<https://www.ft.dk/samling/20222/almdel/GRU/bilag/76/2744240.pdf>)

Greenland-specific current food guidance allows non-animal foods imported by or sent to private individuals, generally without a quantity limit. The presently listed 1949 Greenland import regulation restricts foreign hay fodder, heather and other wild vegetation, not manufactured cultivated tea; government evidence to the Danish Parliament also confirms Greenland lacks an official phytosanitary import-control system. Sealed cultivated Japanese sencha therefore has a supported route without a plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate. General food-safety and customs requirements remain.

**Personal post:** The Greenland guidance expressly covers food sent to private individuals, not only baggage. Its non-animal-food section says imported by or to private persons. Do not resell or add animal ingredients.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Sources:** [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 1](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/indgange/groenland/groenlandske-foedevarer/mad-med-paa-rejsen>) · [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 2](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/lovstof/groenland-lovstof>) · [foedevarestyrelsen.dk 3](<https://foedevarestyrelsen.dk/Media/638139536842116037/Regulativaf1948.pdf>) · [ft.dk 4](<https://www.ft.dk/samling/20222/almdel/GRU/bilag/76/2744240.pdf>)

<a id="gn"></a>
### Guinea (GN)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Guinea’s authentic 2021 food law requires industrial-food quality control before AMC and penalizes unauthorized imports, but the special-document versus routine arrival-release nature of that authorization is not established for this private postal order. The signed 2024 BNIQ order separately has unresolved transport and private non-sale exemption scope; it requires foreign documents if covered. Do not infer clearance or sender-certificate duties from the unavailable private implementation, or universal personal registration from the company manual.

**Document-rule sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/11/29/Loi_Phytosanitaire_République_de_Guinée.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.gn 2](<https://agriculture.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/PROCEDURE-DELIVRANCE-PERMIS-D-IMPORTATION.pdf>) · [magel.gov.gn 3](<https://magel.gov.gn/file/2023/01/PROCEDURE-DELIVRANCE-PERMIS-D-IMPORTATION.pdf>) · [service-public.gov.gn 4](<https://service-public.gov.gn/ova_sev/delivrance-dun-permis-dimportation/>) · [oncq-guinee.com 5](<https://oncq-guinee.com/service/controle-des-denrees-alimentaires-a-limportation/>) · [service-public.gov.gn 6](<https://service-public.gov.gn/ova_sev/demande-dautorisation-dimportation-de-bien-ou-marchandise/>) · [guineelex.com 7](<https://guineelex.com/lois/loi-l-2021-033-an>) · [guineelex.com 8](<https://guineelex.com/decrets-arretes/decret-d-2021-237-prg-sgg>) · [bniq.gov.gn 9](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Arrete-A2024920MCIPMECABSGG-%E2%80%94-Inspection-et-quarantaine-des-produits-entrants-et-sortants.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 10](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Inspection-et-Quarantaine-aux-entrees-et-sorties-en-Guinee-Plate-forme-de-Declaration-Manuel-dutilisation.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 11](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/documents/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 12](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/20260212-2/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 13](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Arrete-A20241102PMCABSGG-%E2%80%94-Creation-et-organisation-du-BNIQ.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 14](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Arrete-conjoint-AC20251274MCMBMEFSGG-%E2%80%94-Tarifs-des-prestations-des-activites-dinspection-et-de-mise-en-quarantaine-.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 15](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/service-inspection-entree/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 16](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/category/politiques/>) · [journal-officiel.sgg.gov.gn 17](<https://journal-officiel.sgg.gov.gn/JO/2021/guinee-jo-2021-07-30-sp.pdf>)

The NPPO’s published PR 01-23 import-permit procedure requires an application, risk analysis and origin phytosanitary or disinsection certification for goods within its plant/plant-product scope. It expressly cites ISPM/NIMP 5 and 32. Neither it nor Law L/92/027 assigns manufactured steamed tea to a risk category; the law limits manufactured-product coverage by pest risk.

**Personal post:** No tea-specific personal postal allowance located.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** The complete authenticated Law L/2021/033/AN has now been recovered from the official SGG Gazette of 30 July 2021, pages 5–11, with promulgation Decree D/2021/237 on page 19. Articles 2 and 4 cover food import by any means; Article 40 requires industrial-food quality control before authorization for consumption (AMC); Article 52 penalizes unauthorized food import. No personal exception is stated in this Act. Article 44 delegates execution and certification rules, while Articles 45–48 describe local control results. The Act itself does not establish that this private parcel needs a sender-procured foreign certificate or a special advance application rather than routine arrival inspection/release. The signed Order A/2024/920/MCIPME/CAB/SGG of 10 June 2024 is now recovered from the official BNIQ library. Article 2 includes imports for final-consumer use; Article 3 limits its stated scope to merchandise transported in containers, trucks and other international rolling transport. Article 4 begins with an exemption statement, then says samples, gifts, objects not intended for sale and other noncommercial articles may be exempt. Article 5 excludes Article 4 goods from its separate electronic inspection declaration. For covered imports Article 6 requires foreign quality or quality-assurance certificates and, for plants and their products, origin official quarantine certificates. These are independent origin-document duties, unlike the positive arrival inspection report issued under Articles 10–12. The order does not expressly settle its Article 3 transport scope or Article 4 exception for a paid private air-post tea parcel. Its own plant-product provision also does not define manufactured tea. The BNIQ manual describes company registration with RCCM and NIF, not a private-post procedure.

**Evidence access:** The previous authenticated-law retrieval gap is closed. Current SGG home links its official Gazette archive; its observed 2021 year filter exposes the exact 30 July 2021 special issue. Direct public download succeeded HTTP 200 (14,150,631 bytes). Read complete Act and visually verified relevant pages and promulgation. GuineeLex is retained only as research history, no longer the source for this law. The signed 2024 BNIQ orders and current manual remain authentic official sources; their private-post scope questions remain.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** The recovered 2021 Act establishes industrial-food quality control/AMC but does not identify whether a private postal parcel must undergo special advance application or provide foreign certificates, as distinct from routine arrival inspection/release. Separately, for BNIQ Order A/2024/920, whether Article 3 containers/trucks/other rolling international transport covers the air-post parcel, and whether Article 4 non-sale/noncommercial exemption operates automatically through Article 5 or needs BNIQ approval. Organization Order A/2024/1102 Article 3 supplies exemption-approval power, not by itself a universal application duty. Article 6 foreign documents apply if the parcel is covered. A general operative postal/AMC rule can decide this; no bespoke tea ruling is intrinsically necessary.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2021/11/29/Loi_Phytosanitaire_République_de_Guinée.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.gn 2](<https://agriculture.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/PROCEDURE-DELIVRANCE-PERMIS-D-IMPORTATION.pdf>) · [magel.gov.gn 3](<https://magel.gov.gn/file/2023/01/PROCEDURE-DELIVRANCE-PERMIS-D-IMPORTATION.pdf>) · [service-public.gov.gn 4](<https://service-public.gov.gn/ova_sev/delivrance-dun-permis-dimportation/>) · [oncq-guinee.com 5](<https://oncq-guinee.com/service/controle-des-denrees-alimentaires-a-limportation/>) · [service-public.gov.gn 6](<https://service-public.gov.gn/ova_sev/demande-dautorisation-dimportation-de-bien-ou-marchandise/>) · [guineelex.com 7](<https://guineelex.com/lois/loi-l-2021-033-an>) · [guineelex.com 8](<https://guineelex.com/decrets-arretes/decret-d-2021-237-prg-sgg>) · [bniq.gov.gn 9](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Arrete-A2024920MCIPMECABSGG-%E2%80%94-Inspection-et-quarantaine-des-produits-entrants-et-sortants.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 10](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Inspection-et-Quarantaine-aux-entrees-et-sorties-en-Guinee-Plate-forme-de-Declaration-Manuel-dutilisation.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 11](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/documents/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 12](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/20260212-2/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 13](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Arrete-A20241102PMCABSGG-%E2%80%94-Creation-et-organisation-du-BNIQ.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 14](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Arrete-conjoint-AC20251274MCMBMEFSGG-%E2%80%94-Tarifs-des-prestations-des-activites-dinspection-et-de-mise-en-quarantaine-.pdf>) · [bniq.gov.gn 15](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/service-inspection-entree/>) · [bniq.gov.gn 16](<https://www.bniq.gov.gn/category/politiques/>) · [journal-officiel.sgg.gov.gn 17](<https://journal-officiel.sgg.gov.gn/JO/2021/guinee-jo-2021-07-30-sp.pdf>)

**Research trail:** OCR-read articles 2 and 7 of the law; searched tea and small-consignment implementing rules. Read the complete two-page national procedure PR 01-23, including its risk-analysis step, origin documentation and references to NIMP 5/32. Checked the government services portal and agriculture procedures index. No tea-specific category or personal-post waiver located. 30 September final primary-scope review: National procedure PR 01-23 section 4 lists NIMP 5 and NIMP 32 as reference documents. It contains no operative clause adopting a particular low-risk category as a permit/certificate exemption. Section 3.2 compares origin pests with the national quarantine list after the application; section 3.3 still specifies origin phytosanitary or disinsection documentation. No new national classification settling steamed sencha was located. 30 September independent food/conformity pass: The current ONCQ official food-import page describes documentary checks at commercial port/container and airport entry points including origin, quality and fumigation certificates. Government import-authorization service says natural/legal persons but requires company register, merchant card, tax identifier and ONCQ quality certificate. Neither describes a private postal order. A newly located legal-database text of Law L/2021/033/AN contains broad import scope and article 40 industrial-food control before release authorization, but a complete authenticated Gazette copy and personal implementation were not recovered. Official ONCQ and government services pages read through their current search index. GuineeLex direct retrieval returned 403; indexed provisions were read as a legislative lead, not treated as complete authenticated primary text. Its reported promulgation is Decree D/2021/237/PRG/SGG of 21 July 2021. 30 September bounded independent scope continuation: Recovered the complete six-page signed BNIQ Order A/2024/920 from the official document library and the complete platform manual. Rendered all order pages, OCR-read all provisions, and visually checked Articles 2–6 plus the stamped 10 June 2024 signature date. Read manual printed pages 5, 8 and 44–46 on company registration/import declarations. The order is effective on signature under Article 13; BNIQ's 12 February 2026 news describes continuing coordination and planned pilot rollout, so full national operational implementation is not inferred from publication alone. Checked the operative exceptions rather than treating paid sale or final-consumer use as determinative in isolation. No outcome change. 30 September common-definition review: Recovered the complete four-page Law L/92/027/CTRN from the official IPPC reporting file, visually read all pages and OCRed them. Article 2 risk-qualifies manufactured plant products; no default ISPM interpretation provision appears in the full law. Published NPPO procedure PR 01-23 section 4 expressly cites NIMP 5 and 32, and section 5 directs officers to apply the procedure: an actual operational standards reference, stronger than membership. This materially supports a Category 1 processing interpretation; it still does not remove the independently unresolved 2024 BNIQ transport/private-use scope. 30 September 2026 focused operative-scope recovery: Recovered both remaining enactments linked by the current BNIQ document library through the browser-visible links, downloaded complete six-page signed PDFs, rendered/OCRed all pages, and visually verified organization Article 3 and the 20 August 2024 signature, plus the 15 December 2025 tariff signature. Order A/2024/1102 Article 3 gives BNIQ the role of examining and approving exemptions of goods; this organizational power alone does not impose an application on every private recipient, but it is relevant context for the ambiguous may-be-exempt wording in inspection Order A/2024/920 Article 4. Joint Order AC/2025/1274 Article 2 expressly charges coffee/tea/mate/spices 0.50% for entry and exit inspection/quarantine. This is an inspection tariff, not itself a permit or origin-certificate duty or an amendment explicitly deciding the private exception. Current service guidance lists generic import documents, while the policies category contains no operative exemption text. The signed 920 transport scope and automatic/discretionary exemption remain actual legal questions. No separate tea ruling is intrinsically necessary; a general operative private-post interpretation would suffice. 30 September 2026 authenticated primary-law recovery: followed current SGG home to its official Gazette platform; used its observed year filter and recovered the complete 30 July 2021 special issue. Full Law L/2021/033/AN appears on pages 5–11 and promulgation Decree D/2021/237 on page 19. Read all 68 articles, visually checked pages 5, 6, 9, 10 and 19. Articles 2/4 cover imports by any means; Article 40 industrial-food quality control precedes AMC; Article 52 penalizes unauthorized import. Article 44 delegates execution/certification. This closes the authenticated-law gap but does not by itself establish foreign origin paperwork or advance application instead of routine local arrival clearance. Rechecked the saved signed BNIQ orders; their exact transport/exemption issue remains. No main source-array decision changed in this source-only proposal.

### Guadeloupe (GP)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

The expressly adopted risk-qualified manufactured-product definition and ISPM 32 tea Category 1 provide favorable processing evidence. However, current DGAL/SDSPV/2026-165 retains pre-2019 overseas implementation despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop; the actual local-order scope/compatibility remains unresolved. Order 96-323 of 16 April 1996 has not been recovered. Official passenger-only and outside-commercial-channel summaries cannot establish whether the order covers a paid personal postal purchase or which processing definition it uses. The newer statutory argument is favorable, but current administrative reliance on old rules prevents treating the unrecovered local measure as definitely superseded.

The 3 September 1990 overseas plant-health order limits certificate requirements to Annex IV commodities; its special dried-tea condition is Réunion-only. Finished tea is absent from the relevant Antilles schedule and from the complete Directive 2000/29 Annex V B incorporated by the current 24 May 2006 order (Arts 8 and 25 limit importer registration/certificates to listed goods). This supports no scheduled national tea-certificate requirement. A separate Guadeloupe order 96-323 of 16 April 1996 remains unresolved: current DAAF guidance describes an incoming passenger-channel ban, whereas an official environment report summarizes a restriction outside commercial channels. The actual local order was not recovered, so its scope for purchased personal postal manufactured tea is not established. Current Code rural expressly adopts a risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. Its statutory 2020 commencement conflicts with current Ministry 2026-165 guidance retaining the older framework; neither claim is omitted. This does not establish a tea prohibition.

**Personal post:** These destinations are separate from the EU plant-health territory. Neither metropolitan EU clearance nor a passenger allowance proves this personal postal route.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Order 96-323 of 16 April 1996 has not been recovered. Official passenger-only and outside-commercial-channel summaries cannot establish whether the order covers a paid personal postal purchase or which processing definition it uses. The newer statutory argument is favorable, but current administrative reliance on old rules prevents treating the unrecovered local measure as definitely superseded. Current statute and ministry implementation conflict; a general published processing/implementation clarification suffices without a bespoke tea letter.

**Research trail:** Read the 1990 overseas plant-health order and its territory-specific dried-tea entry; reviewed local DAAF guidance. The Réunion-only condition does not settle this territory’s current manufactured-tea scope. Second review checked local import-control guidance and the 2025 ministry instruction retaining pre-2019 overseas law. The March 2025 tea exemption found concerns VAT only and cannot establish phytosanitary clearance. 2026-09-30 independent scope review: checked the complete 2006 incorporated Directive 2000/29 Annex V B (official UK 1 September 2019 consolidation, printedpp 190–200); no finished tea, HS 0902 or Camellia leaf entry. Saved consolidated 2006 order and full directive. Identified Guadeloupe local arrêté96-32316 April 1996 through ANSES/current DAAF and official environment report Table 21 p 70. DAAF updated 25 March 2026 describes passenger restrictions, while report summarizes outside-commercial-channel scope. Exact-order/date/number and official publication searches did not recover the order text. No postal prohibition or waiver inferred from those summaries; remaining gap narrowed to actual local-order scope. 30 September bounded legal-scope peer review: The complete national 1990 Annex IV and incorporated 2006 Annex V B already support absence of a scheduled tea certificate. A tea-specific authority ruling is not needed for that list-based conclusion. The remaining barrier is the unrecovered local Order 96-323 of 16 April 1996: current official sources describe passenger or outside-commercial-channel restrictions differently, and neither establishes the scope for a paid private postal order. 30 September fresh domestic-definition review: read currentL 271-7 and 2019-1110 original/currentArticle 6, ratification by 2023-171 Art 39, and official indexed 2026-165/2026-278 implementation statements. Verified 2025-102 abrogated, with 2026-165 current. New actualTeaCategory 1 evidence supports a general processing inference, but present statute/agency implementation and concrete local-rule scope conflict persists; not merely membership or a missing bespoke tea ruling. ### Equatorial Guinea (GQ)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

No affirmative destination-specific no-document rule was located. Official regional land-border and customs measures do not determine the rules for a Japanese personal postal tea parcel, and the postal list preserves all underlying restrictions.

**Document-rule sources:** [ippc.int 1](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/equatorial-guinea/>) · [guineaecuatorialpress.com 2](<https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/index.php/noticias/decreto_por_el_que_se_adoptan_medidas_de_regulacion_comercial_en_las_fronteras_terrestres_entre_guinea_ecuatorial_camerun_y_gabon>) · [guineaecuatorialpress.com 3](<https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/orden_por_la_que_se_aprueba_sistema_aduanero_automatizado_para_la_tramitacion_de_los_procedimientos_relativos_a_la_entrada_y_salida_de_mercancias>) · [slpost.gov.lk 4](<https://slpost.gov.lk/documents/ListOfProhibitedArticles2025-09-25.pdf>)

No destination authority's current commodity rule was located that establishes the agricultural admissibility of a sealed personal tea parcel.

**Personal post:** A tariff heading or postal service alone does not establish agricultural clearance.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** An operative national plant law/commodity rule defining manufactured plant products and the applicable private postal route; neither regional border measures nor a postal prohibited-items list supplies that missing scope.

**Sources:** [ippc.int 1](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/equatorial-guinea/>) · [guineaecuatorialpress.com 2](<https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/index.php/noticias/decreto_por_el_que_se_adoptan_medidas_de_regulacion_comercial_en_las_fronteras_terrestres_entre_guinea_ecuatorial_camerun_y_gabon>) · [guineaecuatorialpress.com 3](<https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/orden_por_la_que_se_aprueba_sistema_aduanero_automatizado_para_la_tramitacion_de_los_procedimientos_relativos_a_la_entrada_y_salida_de_mercancias>) · [slpost.gov.lk 4](<https://slpost.gov.lk/documents/ListOfProhibitedArticles2025-09-25.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Searched official and IPPC sources in Spanish, French and English for tea, manufactured plant products, phytosanitary requirements and personal parcels; found no dispositive rule. Checked current NPPO country file and searched Spanish/French official legislation and government information. Read the official regional land-border decree: livestock certificate clauses and CEMAC tax concessions cannot be applied to Japanese tea by post. Checked the country’s February 2024 postal return in the UPU compilation: it excludes unauthorized restricted items but offers no tea or processed-food exemption. 30 September common-definition review: Existing official material concerns regional land-border trade and automated customs, not a plant-product definition or standards-incorporation provision. No full current plant law was available to test a definition bridge. No generic repeat search or membership-based conclusion was made.

<a id="gt"></a>
### Guatemala (GT)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

No ordinary-declaration-only route supported: the MAGA processed-plant-product constancia is special authorization the store will not obtain.

**Document-rule sources:** [tramites.gob.gt 1](<https://www.tramites.gob.gt/servicio/2824/>) · [maga.gob.gt 2](<https://www.maga.gob.gt/sitios/visar/facilitacion-de-comercio/>) · [maga.gob.gt 3](<https://www.maga.gob.gt/sitios/visar/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Manual-SEPA.pdf>) · [sigie.maga.gob.gt 4](<https://sigie.maga.gob.gt/ConsultaProducto?tipo=2>) · [mspas.gob.gt 5](<https://www.mspas.gob.gt/productos-control-de-alimentos/category/325-formularios-autorizacion-sanitaria-importacion-alimentos-procesados-tramite-presencial>) · [mspas.gob.gt 6](<https://www.mspas.gob.gt/productos-control-de-alimentos?task=download.send&id=3363&catid=325&m=0>) · [apps.maga.gob.gt 7](<https://apps.maga.gob.gt/simplificacion/Formularios/Details/778>) · [apps.maga.gob.gt 8](<https://apps.maga.gob.gt/normativas/Normativas/Download/1154>)

MAGA Agreement 341-2019 applies to every natural or legal person (article 2). Article 9 requires an import-authorization constancia even for processed plant products that do not pose phytosanitary risk; MAGA maintains the procedure in 2026. Therefore finished sencha does not obtain a no-document route by being processed. Separately, current MSPAS form FS-DRCA-07.2 exempts processed personal-consumption foods up to 10 kg/12 bottles from sanitary import certification, but it does not waive MAGA authorization.

**Personal post:** MAGA article 2 covers all natural/legal persons and import shipments, with no personal-post exception in the reviewed rule. The MSPAS small personal-use exemption is acknowledged separately and is not treated as a MAGA exemption.

**Quantity conditions:** MSPAS processed-food personal sanitary-certification exemption only; it does not remove the separate MAGA constancia.

**Checkout:** 16 pouches / 1.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 2 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No ordinary-declaration-only route supported: the MAGA processed-plant-product constancia is special authorization the store will not obtain.

**Sources:** [tramites.gob.gt 1](<https://www.tramites.gob.gt/servicio/2824/>) · [maga.gob.gt 2](<https://www.maga.gob.gt/sitios/visar/facilitacion-de-comercio/>) · [maga.gob.gt 3](<https://www.maga.gob.gt/sitios/visar/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/Manual-SEPA.pdf>) · [sigie.maga.gob.gt 4](<https://sigie.maga.gob.gt/ConsultaProducto?tipo=2>) · [mspas.gob.gt 5](<https://www.mspas.gob.gt/productos-control-de-alimentos/category/325-formularios-autorizacion-sanitaria-importacion-alimentos-procesados-tramite-presencial>) · [mspas.gob.gt 6](<https://www.mspas.gob.gt/productos-control-de-alimentos?task=download.send&id=3363&catid=325&m=0>) · [apps.maga.gob.gt 7](<https://apps.maga.gob.gt/simplificacion/Formularios/Details/778>) · [apps.maga.gob.gt 8](<https://apps.maga.gob.gt/normativas/Normativas/Download/1154>)

<a id="gu"></a>
### Guam (GU)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The stated tea undergoes steaming, repeated rolling/pressing and drying to create a finished food. Actual ISPM 32 Category 1 tea and MAFF manufacture evidence support applying Guam’s express minor-processing limit; section 10120 separately supplies affirmative dried-food admissibility. Section 10103 permit duties remain for covered plants/products, and 10100(f) broad plant-parts language is read with (g) rather than making its processing limitation ineffective. No certificate category names or describes this finished tea. This combined-source conclusion is interpretive, not a direct tea-specific agency quotation.

**Document-rule sources:** [guamcourts.org 1](<https://www.guamcourts.org/compileroflaws/GAR/08GAR/08GAR002-10.pdf>) · [doag.guam.gov 2](<https://doag.guam.gov/biosecurity-division/>) · [doag.guam.gov 3](<https://doag.guam.gov/resource/>) · [doag.guam.gov 4](<https://doag.guam.gov/wp-doag-content/uploads/2026/03/Import-Permit-Request-Plant-Products-Revised2026.03.09.pdf>) · [doag.guam.gov 5](<https://doag.guam.gov/wp-doag-content/uploads/2024/02/DOAG-Species-listing-reference.pdf>) · [fda.gov 6](<https://www.fda.gov/media/161541/download>) · [gov.si 7](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 8](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

Reasoned general-category application: finished steam-manufactured sencha falls outside the unprocessed/minor-processed plant-product definition in 8 GAR 10100(g). Section 10120 admits stored dried human food subject to pest freedom, while the 10102(a) certificate categories do not cover finished tea. Ordinary declaration and inspection remain. This is an inference from the territorial rules plus actual manufacturing evidence, not a Guam-issued tea ruling.

**Personal post:** The manufacturing definition and 10120 dried-food provision are commodity rules without a baggage-only restriction. The conclusion applies to the stated direct Japanese private postal parcel; no traveller exemption or mainland rule is extended.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [guamcourts.org 1](<https://www.guamcourts.org/compileroflaws/GAR/08GAR/08GAR002-10.pdf>) · [doag.guam.gov 2](<https://doag.guam.gov/biosecurity-division/>) · [doag.guam.gov 3](<https://doag.guam.gov/resource/>) · [doag.guam.gov 4](<https://doag.guam.gov/wp-doag-content/uploads/2026/03/Import-Permit-Request-Plant-Products-Revised2026.03.09.pdf>) · [doag.guam.gov 5](<https://doag.guam.gov/wp-doag-content/uploads/2024/02/DOAG-Species-listing-reference.pdf>) · [fda.gov 6](<https://www.fda.gov/media/161541/download>) · [gov.si 7](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 8](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

<a id="gw"></a>
### Guinea-Bissau (GW)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported conditionally for sealed uncontaminated plain Camellia tea made by industrial steaming, rolling and drying, applying the general domestic transformation boundary. No bespoke tea classification letter is needed for that inference. Article 8 other-objects wording is interpreted in the phytosanitary context of Articles 1/5, while infected or pest-carrying packaging remains controlled. Food Decree 62-E/1992 covers imports but Articles 10–11 and 14 delegate administrative-document/certification rules; these are not themselves an unavoidable private certificate. Decree-Law 8/2011 Article 10 abolishes prior trade-registration bulletins and routes the replacement simple declaration through Customs. Ordinary customs and food safety remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs23411.pdf>) · [fao.org 2](<https://www.fao.org/faolex/country-profiles/general-profile/en/?iso3=GNB>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs117287.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs118159.pdf>) · [wto.org 5](<https://www.wto.org/french/tratop_f/tpr_f/s362-04_f.pdf>) · [ippc.int 6](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 7](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Explicit legal-scope inference: Decree-Law 4/99 Article 2(a) defines living plants/parts; Article 2(b) covers unprocessed products or simple preparation. The stated industrial steaming, rolling and drying that manufactures sencha is inferred to be transformation beyond that class, not raw or merely dried herbs. Current-linked ISPM 32 tea Category 1 is corroborating technical evidence of manufacturing, not a directly incorporated Guinea-Bissau exemption. Article 8 permits for plants/products/other objects are read within Article 1 phytosanitary purpose and Article 5 contamination-related inspection powers, not as controlling every clean manufactured food. No contrary national rule specifically treating manufactured tea as simply prepared was identified in the reviewed primary sources.

**Personal post:** This is a product-definition interpretation applying to a paid private postal order, not a passenger/gift concession. The buyer consumes the sealed plain tea and does not resell it. Ordinary customs declaration and lawful arrival food/pest inspection remain.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Decree 62-E/1992 Articles 1–2 cover imported processed food. Article 3 establishes food safety and recognizes Codex conformity, provable by any means; Article 4 concerns market placement and possible future prior controls, not an immediate universal certificate. Articles 10–11 delegate inspection methods, administrative documents and specific import conditions; Article 14 leaves certification to specific regulation. No separate special-document duty for the private plain-tea order is established by these clauses. Decree-Law 8/2011 Article 10 abolishes BRPI/BRPE prior registration bulletins and provides a simple written declaration submitted through Customs; this does not itself waive SPS rules, whose manufactured-product scope is separately interpreted.

**Evidence access:** Complete Gazette extract downloaded from FAOLEX and text read. The file also contains tail material from a preceding tourism law, which was kept separate. No newer implementing certificate rule for private tea located.

**Checkout:** 82 pouches / 8.2 kg tea; highest carrier band 10 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs23411.pdf>) · [fao.org 2](<https://www.fao.org/faolex/country-profiles/general-profile/en/?iso3=GNB>) · [wto.org 3](<https://www.wto.org/french/tratop_f/tpr_f/s362-04_f.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs117287.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/gbs118159.pdf>) · [ippc.int 6](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 7](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

<a id="hn"></a>
### Honduras (HN)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Personal-use food imports require an ARSA Autorización Especial Personal (AEP). The enacted food regulation expressly covers noncommercial personal consumption in article 8 and prescribes the AEP application and documents in articles 87–88. The five-units-per-product/ten-products limit describes eligibility for authorization, not an exemption. This special food authorization conflicts with the store’s no-special-paperwork policy independently of the unresolved SENASA classification of manufactured tea.

**Document-rule sources:** [tsc.gob.hn 1](<https://www.tsc.gob.hn/web/leyes/Acuerdo-0632-ARSA-2023.pdf>) · [arsa.gob.hn 2](<https://arsa.gob.hn/donaciones-y-autorizaciones-especiales/>)

Enacted SENASA quarantine regulation 001-2023 articles 5–12 applies permits and specified certification to regulated imports for commercial or other purposes, including consumption. Article 11 provides exempt-product lists; a matching finished-tea exemption or Japan commodity entry was not located.

**Personal post:** Individual consumption is expressly included in permit applications; no postal small-quantity waiver established.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Does manufactured Japanese sencha appear on an article 11 exemption list, and otherwise which permit/phyto conditions apply?

**Sources:** [tsc.gob.hn 1](<https://www.tsc.gob.hn/web/leyes/Acuerdo-CD-SENASA-001-2023.pdf>) · [sistemas.senasa.gob.hn 2](<https://sistemas.senasa.gob.hn/senasa/index.php/sub-direcciones/sub-direccion-tecnica-de-sanidad-vegetal/requisitos-fitosanitarios-para-la-importacion-de-vegetales>)

**Research trail:** Read enacted 2023 regulation, specifically articles 7 and 11, and country commodity requirement tables; rejected the earlier 2022 draft as legal authority. No Japan/tea entry established. 2026-09-30: Read the enacted Acuerdo 0632-ARSA-2023 as published in La Gaceta 13 August 2024, especially articles 8 and 87–88, and the current ARSA personal-import service requirements. Saved full official PDF/text as sources/hn-food 2024.*. Personal foods require AEP; no small-parcel waiver is stated. Agricultural product classification remains separately unverified.

### Indonesia (ID)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [jdih.karantinaindonesia.go.id 1](<https://jdih.karantinaindonesia.go.id/repository/barantin-jdih/common/dokumen/Perba5.pdf>) · [ngkt.mofa.gov.vn 2](<https://ngkt.mofa.gov.vn/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/QD-IQA-09.2024.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 3](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **Q**. The 2025 amendment to the quarantine commodity list specifically provides K-9.3 for green tea leaves HS 09021010 in ready-for-consumption packaging (tea bags are an example); ordinary leaf tea remains K-9.2. K-9.3 records goods not subject to quarantine measures. Report the parcel for the appropriate determination rather than treating all processed tea as automatically exempt.

**Personal post:** The commodity list applies to imports generally and has no personal-mail exclusion; retail packaging route is product-based.

**Checkout:** 50 pouches / 5 kg tea; highest carrier band 6.08 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Confirm that the sealed loose-leaf sencha pouch qualifies as ready-for-consumption packaging for K-9.3; otherwise obtain the regular quarantine import documents. BPOM personal-food limits do not decide that classification.

<a id="iq"></a>
### Iraq (IQ)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Conservative exclusion based on the domestic plant-nature definition plus the explicit distinction between the postal certificate waiver and the seed-only licence waiver. Steaming can inactivate pests without turning the finished dried leaf into something other than a plant-origin product retaining plant nature. This is a moderate-confidence interpretation of the general law, not a tea-specific administrative ruling. ISPM 32 Category 1 is technical pest-risk evidence and does not itself replace Iraq’s differently worded scope or its permit requirement.

**Document-rule sources:** [moj.gov.iq 1](<https://www.moj.gov.iq/upload/pdf/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%AC%D8%B1%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D9%8A.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/iq_2012-76cons2020_pqact_en.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 3](<https://www.cosqc.gov.iq/files/Guide_files/9.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 4](<https://cosqc.gov.iq/files/Guide_files/10.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 5](<https://www.cosqc.gov.iq/wan/ns/f/58_1.pdf>) · [moj.gov.iq 6](<https://www.moj.gov.iq/upload/pdf/قانون الحجر الزراعي.pdf>) · [uomosul.edu.iq 7](<https://www.uomosul.edu.iq/public/files/datafolder_40/oldUo_20190816_054913_19_816.pdf>)

Iraq Agricultural Quarantine Law 76/2012, consolidated by the Ministry of Justice with the 2020 amendment, Article 1(4) covers plant-origin products prepared without diverting them from their plant nature. The original Arabic gazette uses طبيعتها النباتية. Factory sencha remains dried plant leaves after steaming and drying, so coverage is a reasonable conservative legal-scope inference. Article 7(1) requires an import licence for covered plant-product consignments. Article 9(1) expressly waives the phytosanitary certificate for qualifying non-agricultural consignments received by post up to 10 kg; Article 9(2) separately waives the import licence only for specified seed-registration samples. The described tea parcel is therefore unsuitable for the store’s no-permit route under this reading, rather than prohibited tea.

**Personal post:** Post offices are expressly included in Article 1(18); Article 9(1) expressly addresses mail as well as travellers and shipped consignments. The certificate-only waiver can plausibly cover this 100 g personal beverage parcel, but the separate import licence remains under the conservative plant-nature reading.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 10 kg product weight. Article 9(1) certificate exemption only for qualifying non-agricultural-purpose consignments; not an import-licence waiver or permission to ship. Law says weight of materials, without expressly defining net or gross.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. Current checkout also exceeds the cited quantity threshold. Reducing quantity does not remove the separate regulatory requirement; this route is excluded under the store constraint.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below. The current maximum order is outside the reviewed threshold.

**Sources:** [moj.gov.iq 1](<https://www.moj.gov.iq/upload/pdf/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%AC%D8%B1%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D9%8A.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/iq_2012-76cons2020_pqact_en.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 3](<https://www.cosqc.gov.iq/files/Guide_files/9.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 4](<https://cosqc.gov.iq/files/Guide_files/10.pdf>) · [cosqc.gov.iq 5](<https://www.cosqc.gov.iq/wan/ns/f/58_1.pdf>) · [moj.gov.iq 6](<https://www.moj.gov.iq/upload/pdf/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86%20%D9%86%D8%B8%D8%A7%D9%85%20%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%BA%D8%AF%D9%8A%D8%A9%20-%20Copy.pdf>) · [eia.krd 7](<https://www.eia.krd/english/about-eia/health-department>) · [moj.gov.iq 8](<https://www.moj.gov.iq/upload/pdf/قانون الحجر الزراعي.pdf>) · [uomosul.edu.iq 9](<https://www.uomosul.edu.iq/public/files/datafolder_40/oldUo_20190816_054913_19_816.pdf>)

<a id="jm"></a>
### Jamaica (JM)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude under the no-certificate constraint: Food and Drugs Act section 4(1) independently requires an origin-law/free-sale certificate for imported food. This is an explicit inference from unqualified food-import wording, not a tea-specific MOHW ruling. No automatic personal postal exception was established; this certificate is not a phytosanitary certificate.

**Document-rule sources:** [moa.gov.jm 1](<https://www.moa.gov.jm/sites/default/files/Import-Requirement-and-Permit-Application-Process.pdf>) · [moa.gov.jm 2](<https://www.moa.gov.jm/sites/default/files/Import%20Process%20BROCHURE.pdf>) · [jamaicatradeportal.gov.jm 3](<https://jamaicatradeportal.gov.jm/en-gb/site/commodity/0902100000>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 4](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/legislation/subsids/P/The%20Plants%20%28Quarantine%29%20Act.pdf>) · [moa.gov.jm 5](<https://moa.gov.jm/content/plants-import-permits-requirements>) · [moh.gov.jm 6](<https://www.moh.gov.jm/standards-and-regulation-division/>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 7](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/statute/the-food-and-drugs-act/download>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 8](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/legislation/subsids/F/The%20Food%20and%20Drugs%20Act.pdf>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 9](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/statute/the-food-and-drugs-act>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 10](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/subsidiary-legislation/the-food-and-drugs-act>)

Food and Drugs Act section 4(1) requires any imported food to conform to origin-country law and be accompanied by a certificate of that conformity and lawful sale/consumption there. Section 2 defines food and importer broadly; section 4(2) separately regulates subsequent sale. The provision is not limited to resale, and no automatic personal-post exception was established in the current published regulations. Applying that unqualified food duty to retail sencha is an explicit legal-scope inference. The separate plant-quarantine processing/classification question remains unresolved but does not remove this food-certificate requirement.

**Personal post:** The exclusion is inferred from the enacted duty on any person importing any food, without a commercial/resale or traveler qualifier. No automatic small personal postal food-certificate exception was found; baggage allowances and veterinary exemptions do not establish one for tea.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No ordinary-declaration-only route supported by the published food-import default. A specific operative personal-food certificate exemption could change this conclusion, but none was established. The separate PQPI manufactured-tea permit/phytosanitary classification does not need to be resolved to exclude this no-certificate route.

**Sources:** [moa.gov.jm 1](<https://www.moa.gov.jm/sites/default/files/Import-Requirement-and-Permit-Application-Process.pdf>) · [moa.gov.jm 2](<https://www.moa.gov.jm/sites/default/files/Import%20Process%20BROCHURE.pdf>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 3](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/legislation/subsids/P/The%20Plants%20%28Quarantine%29%20Act.pdf>) · [moa.gov.jm 4](<https://moa.gov.jm/content/plants-import-permits-requirements>) · [moh.gov.jm 5](<https://www.moh.gov.jm/standards-and-regulation-division/>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 6](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/statute/the-food-and-drugs-act/download>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 7](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/legislation/subsids/F/The%20Food%20and%20Drugs%20Act.pdf>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 8](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/statute/the-food-and-drugs-act>) · [laws.moj.gov.jm 9](<https://laws.moj.gov.jm/library/subsidiary-legislation/the-food-and-drugs-act>)

No finished-tea entry or personal postal exemption confirmed. 2026-09-30: Rechecked current Jamaica Trade Portal commodity 0902100000 and linked measures 1293/1294. They describe coffee-specific JACRA licences even though mapped to wider chapter 09; rejected this overbroad mapping as evidence for tea. Jamaica Post/UPU herbal-tea PSD provisions do not establish Camellia tea treatment. Restored MOA requirements page indexed, but complete finished-tea entry unavailable. 2026-09-30: Retrieved and OCR-read both pages of the official Import Process brochure (2025 edit), correcting the assertion that no processed-product exemption was published. Downloaded Ministry of Justice consolidated regulations and read the complete First Schedule Part II and regulations 6/22. Public commodity page states its list is incomplete and directs to JSWIFT for the full database. MOHW general Foods and Drugs blue-form requirement has no clear published plain-tea personal-post exception. Kept unresolved instead of treating plant permit exemption as automatic certificate/food-approval exemption. 2026-09-30: Retrieved Ministry of Justice Food and Drugs Act and full 76-page Food and Drugs Regulations 1975 consolidation. Read Act section 2 definitions and all of section 4; OCR-read all 42 image-only regulation pages, including general rules, administration and schedules, to avoid missing an exception. No automatic personal-food import certificate exception found. Current statute/subsidiary indexes identify latest amendments as 1996/2014. Ministry of Health general prior-approval guidance and targeted official personal/certificate searches supply no plain-tea postal waiver. Classified as no-document exclusion by explicit general-law inference, preserving the unresolved plant-quarantine classification separately.

Jordan’s 2021 Plant Quarantine Instructions Article 3 covers designated plants/products and requires agricultural release at both public and private postal centres. Article 8 requires an original phytosanitary certificate for covered imports, with exemptions only for products separately designated by ministerial decision. A current designation covering or exempting manufactured sencha was not found.

**Personal post:** 2021 Plant Quarantine Instructions Article 3 expressly includes public/private postal centres. The 2026 Tawasal personal-parcel consultation is confined to medicines, pharmaceutical products and specified infant foods; it does not establish ordinary-tea clearance requirements.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** The operative Article 3 commodity-designation decisions and Article 8(c) ministerial exception decisions, or another current general processing rule, sufficient to place finished sencha inside or outside the controlled class. The full 2021 instructions themselves no longer need recovery. No complete commodity/exemption list appears on any of the 13 pages.

**Sources:** [moa.gov.jo 1](<https://moa.gov.jo/ebv4.0/root_storage/ar/eb_list_page/%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%85%D8%A7%D8%AA_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%AC%D8%B1_%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%86%D8%A8%D8%A7%D8%AA%D9%8A_%D9%84%D8%B3%D9%86%D8%A9_2021.pdf>) · [tawasal.gov.jo 2](<https://tawasal.gov.jo/Consultations/Public/1436/Guideline_for_the_Import_of_Personal_Parcels>) · [tawasal.gov.jo 3](<https://tawasal.gov.jo/api/Attachments/abbdf5ea-2ed0-4e01-ac56-23a9f5f6cfbf>) · [tawasal.gov.jo 4](<https://tawasal.gov.jo/api/Attachments/6a217327-bb72-4b8d-92eb-7f7ff6ef9479>) · [services.customs.gov.jo 5](<https://services.customs.gov.jo/JCcits/sections.aspx>) · [tradeportal.customs.gov.jo 6](<https://tradeportal.customs.gov.jo/>) · [jfda.jo 7](<https://jfda.jo/Ar/List/القوانين>) · [jiacc.gov.jo 8](<https://www.jiacc.gov.jo/Ar/List/القوانين>) · [jordanianlaw.com 9](<https://jordanianlaw.com/القوانين/قانـون-الغــذاء/>) · [faolex.fao.org 10](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/jor149617.pdf>) · [pm.gov.jo 11](<https://www.pm.gov.jo/Ar/Pages/NewsPaperDetails/5762>) · [moa.gov.jo 12](<https://www.moa.gov.jo/AR/List/%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%82%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%8A%D9%86>) · [moa.gov.jo 13](<https://www.moa.gov.jo/ebv4.0/root_storage/ar/eb_list_page/دليل_العمليات_الزراعية_للعام_2024.pdf>) · [ammonnews.net 14](<https://www.ammonnews.net/article/653206>)

**Research trail:** Read ministry’s 2021 instructions, Articles 3 and 8, including public/private post. Searched ministry tea certificate/exemption decisions in Arabic and English and JFDA personal-parcel guidance; no enacted finished-tea postal exemption identified. 30 September final review: downloaded and read Tawasal consultation draft and response report. Draft scope under Medicine Import Instructions 2026 Article 16 is medicines, vitamins/minerals, natural pharmaceutical products and specified infant foods; Article 14 requires final pharmaceutical form. Removed its enactment status as an ordinary-tea research question. Navigated official Jordan Customs integrated tariff through section 02/chapter 09 and retrieved HS 09021000000: green tea in immediate packaging not exceeding 3 kg, duty 0%. Available detail links cover statistics, taxes, agreements and tariff tree; no plant-quarantine classification was published there. The 3 kg is a tariff packaging descriptor, not a personal import allowance. Checked the official trade portal’s tea procedures; the listed tea route is export of locally purchased tea, not personal imports. Further Arabic searches of MOA, Customs and JFDA did not locate the current tea quarantine designation or ordinary-food personal postal exception. 30 September bounded independent-food follow-up: Checked the official JFDA and Integrity Commission law indexes. Both list the 2015 Food Law, but direct requests returned HTTP 451 and the web index did not expose a usable official PDF link. Reviewed the published legal-text reproduction Articles 2-4, 11-16 and 29-33: inspection/fitness and special-food approval are distinct; the latter cannot automatically be applied to ordinary sencha. Article 12 delegates inspection/health procedures and Article 29 preserves older implementing rules. No origin certificate or private-postal waiver was inferred from those enabling provisions. 30 September fresh official publication route: current Prime Ministry Gazette 5762 index explicitly links 2021 InstructionsG/12 PlantQuarantine through public DocuWare document 57362. The web retriever reached a client-only viewer without the document text; direct retrieval returnedHTTP 451. The Ministry current laws index lists amended AgricultureLaw 13/2015 but exposes no extractable download in the parsed page, and direct catalogue request also returned 451. This is a specific inaccessible operative source route, not an absent-law inference. Recovered complete AgricultureLaw 13/2015 Arabic text from FAOLEX. Article 2 defines plants broadly including leaves/other parts and products as plant-origin material retaining its botanical nature until consumption; Article 6 ties measures to scientific/international standards with risk-analysis alternatives; Article 25(b) requires quarantine formalities and permits ministerial exemptions. Unlike Bahrain/Maldives, this source does not provide a clear living-plants/risk-qualified manufactured-product boundary. No automatic ISPM 32 exemption is inferred from the measure-setting duties. The FAO copy is not claimed to be the freshly recovered ministry consolidated/amended text. Used an exact Arabic title/date search to locate a contemporaneous gazette facsimile at Ammon, downloaded all linked instruction-page images, and visually checked every page including Articles 3, 8, 11 and 37. Did not retry the official 451 file or DocuWare denial.

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The government import-health table specifically requires Tea Board approval and a KEPHIS plant import permit for tea. No published personal-post waiver has been established; the documented route requires paperwork the store will not obtain.

**Document-rule sources:** [porthealth.health.go.ke 1](<https://porthealth.health.go.ke/import-health-clearance>) · [kephis.go.ke 2](<https://kephis.go.ke/phytosanitary-services>) · [new.kenyalaw.org 3](<https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/2020/23/eng%402020-12-31>)

The government's import-health table specifically lists tea with Tea Board approval and a KEPHIS plant import permit. KEPHIS requires the exporting authority's phytosanitary certificate for controlled plant consignments; Tea Act section 31 also regulates tea imports.

**Personal post:** No personal postal waiver of the published tea-specific route was found.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No document-free shipping route established. Do not obtain the documented approvals: the store will not use this route. A future published automatic exemption would require reassessment.

**Sources:** [porthealth.health.go.ke 1](<https://porthealth.health.go.ke/import-health-clearance>) · [kephis.go.ke 2](<https://kephis.go.ke/phytosanitary-services>) · [new.kenyalaw.org 3](<https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/act/2020/23/eng%402020-12-31>)

### Cambodia (KH)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Inference from the current complete commodity schedules: HS 0902 has no import-permit or exporting-country-certificate treatment. This is supported by the explicit personal-value exemption and the current individual postal clearance rules. Routine postal customs declarations, inspection and any taxes remain applicable. The separate food-control question is covered by Prakas 263 C Article 3, which exempts personal-use food imports from that procedure.

**Document-rule sources:** [tools.customs.gov.kh 1](<https://tools.customs.gov.kh/documents/reader/16357/17170663763358226/THE%20ENFORCEMENT%20OF%20THE%20LIST%20OF%20PROHIBETED%20AND%20RESTRICTED%20GOODS>) · [data.opendevelopmentcambodia.net 2](<https://data.opendevelopmentcambodia.net/km/dataset/5d272c32-0dd8-4e60-99f3-d21c062ae247/resource/4477cddc-6fe1-4430-8fc6-1885b28de093/download/ennnn.pdf>) · [tools.customs.gov.kh 3](<https://tools.customs.gov.kh/documents/reader/17709/17373560374410226/Manual%20on%20Customs%20Procedures%20in%20Cambodia%20%28English%29>) · [cambodiantr.gov.kh 4](<https://cambodiantr.gov.kh/en/commodity/leaves/>) · [cambodiantr.gov.kh 5](<https://cambodiantr.gov.kh/en/document/?title=sub-decree-no-015-on-phytosanitary-inspection>) · [cambodiantr.gov.kh 6](<https://cambodiantr.gov.kh/en/document/?title=prakas-no-346-phytosanitary-inspection>) · [cambodiantr.gov.kh 7](<https://cambodiantr.gov.kh/en/search-commodity/>) · [fia.maff.gov.kh 8](<https://fia.maff.gov.kh/dfptq/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/MoC-No-263-C-Risk-levels-of-food-import.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/cam211050.pdf>) · [ccfdg.gov.kh 10](<https://www.ccfdg.gov.kh/en/laws-regulations/prakas/>)

Current Sub-Decree 370 (28 December 2023; effective 1 May 2024) sets the permit/certificate lists. Plain green leaf tea HS 09021010 / heading 0902 is absent from its annexes. Annex 5 additionally exempts noncommercial imports of customs value US$1,000 or less from Annex 3 exporter certificates. The 2025 Customs manual provides ordinary postal/e-commerce clearance for individuals; unrestricted goods arriving through Cambodia Post do not need prior GDCE approval. Prakas 263 C (16 September 2019), Article 3, expressly excludes personal-use food imports from its imported-food procedure; its annex also classifies processed tea as low-risk.

**Personal post:** The Customs manual explicitly covers postal parcels and cross-border e-commerce purchases, including imports by individuals. Keep this reviewed route to personal consumption and parcel FOB value below US$1,000. Over US$50, the recipient supplies a simplified declaration, invoice/payment evidence and identification; this is ordinary customs clearance, not an agricultural certificate.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** No outstanding permit/certificate issue identified for the specified plain finished tea within the reviewed personal postal route. Any added herbs, untreated plant material or medicinal claims require a separate assessment.
