<a id="ki"></a>
### Kiribati (KI)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Manufactured tea is within the Act’s unqualified plant-product/regulated-article definitions, but that alone does not establish a permit or certificate: sections 25–26 expressly condition those documents on commodity specifications. Failure to locate a specification online is not proof that none exists; section 80 makes the register available at the Director’s office and Island Council offices. Section 27 would require an unacceptable advance application only if there is no existing specification. Routine arrival clearance/inspection is not treated as a special-paperwork blocker. The published personal-food concession does not settle the missing biosecurity specification.

**Document-rule sources:** [melad.gov.ki 1](<https://www.melad.gov.ki/index.php?Itemid=380&id=43&option=com_quix&view=page>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir147512.pdf>) · [melad.gov.ki 3](<https://www.melad.gov.ki/images/ald/applications/application_1.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir233800.pdf>) · [wipolex-res.wipo.int 5](<https://wipolex-res.wipo.int/edocs/lexdocs/laws/en/ki/ki038en.html>) · [agriculture.melad.gov.ki 6](<https://agriculture.melad.gov.ki/>) · [fao.org 7](<https://www.fao.org/gm-platform/browse-information/country-details/kiribati/en>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir51665.pdf>) · [gov.si 9](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>)

Biosecurity Act 2011 section 2 unconditionally includes products manufactured wholly or partly from plants within plant products, and all plant products within regulated articles. Finished sencha therefore falls within statutory scope (application to this product is an inference). Sections 25–26 make permits/certificates conditional on the applicable import specification; section 27 requires an advance written specification application only where no specification already exists. Section 31 exemptions require advance written requests and apply to a single importation. Food Regulations 2014 section 56 separately exempts personal-consumption food up to 10 kg per person from food-law requirements, not the Biosecurity Act. Section 26(2)(c) limits specifications to reasonably necessary risk measures, while section 26(3) requires a risk assessment and regard to SPS/international standards but permits precaution. These are rules for making the operative specification, not a self-executing exemption for processed tea.

**Personal post:** The Act establishes postal holding areas, and MELAD names incoming mail. The requirements therefore cannot be dismissed because the recipient consumes the tea personally.

**Checkout:** 100 pouches / 10 kg tea; highest carrier band 12.08 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Current section 26 specification/saved earlier instrument covering finished plant-origin foods, or the relevant complete register showing none exists and thus triggering section 27. ISPM 32 tea risk evidence is relevant to the Director’s section 26 assessment but does not remove the Act’s unconditional manufactured-product scope.

**Sources:** [melad.gov.ki 1](<https://www.melad.gov.ki/index.php?Itemid=380&id=43&option=com_quix&view=page>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir147512.pdf>) · [melad.gov.ki 3](<https://www.melad.gov.ki/images/ald/applications/application_1.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir233800.pdf>) · [wipolex-res.wipo.int 5](<https://wipolex-res.wipo.int/edocs/lexdocs/laws/en/ki/ki038en.html>) · [agriculture.melad.gov.ki 6](<https://agriculture.melad.gov.ki/>) · [fao.org 7](<https://www.fao.org/gm-platform/browse-information/country-details/kiribati/en>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kir51665.pdf>) · [gov.si 9](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [kiribati.tradeportal.org 10](<https://kiribati.tradeportal.org/procedure/119/step/141>)

**Research trail:** Read MELAD incoming-mail and import-permit services. Downloaded Biosecurity Act 2011 and read the plant-product definition, sections 24–28 and the written-exemption mechanism in section 31. Searched the authority for tea and processed-food import specifications; no tea-specific determination found. Read Food Regulations and Standards 2014 sections 56–57: personal-consumption exemption up to 10 kg per person and ordinary food identification/inspection provisions. Kept this separate from plant biosecurity. Checked the agriculture ministry’s newer website and Kiribati’s FAO country submission, which allocates prepacked processed-food inspection to Health; neither publishes a legal tea-specific biosecurity waiver. Continued exact tea/Camellia/import-specification searches; no published applicable specification or written exemption found. 30 September independent scope reread: Act PDF pp.17–18 confirms unqualified manufactured-product and regulated-article definitions; pp.41–46 confirms conditional permit/certificate provisions and the no-existing-specification trigger for advance application. Section 80, p.91, provides for an office-based public register, so unsuccessful online searches cannot prove absence of a specification. Rechecked MELAD service text and the national FAO submission allocating processed/prepackaged food to Health; neither supplies the missing tea specification or a biosecurity waiver. 30 September independent food-document review: reread Food Regulations and Standards 2014 sections 53–64 in the FAO-hosted enacted text. Section 56 expressly exempts personal-consumption imports up to 10 kg from food-legislation requirements; section 57 permits identity-document checks for personal food. Sections 58–59 assess accompanying sanitary/analysis certificates and section 64 provides an inspector-issued arrival release permit. These provisions do not establish an advance foreign certificate or special registration obligation for every 100 g personal parcel; ordinary identity/arrival clearance is distinct from special advance paperwork. The existing biosecurity-specification gap remains unresolved. Saved text as pacific-food-sources/ki-food 2014.*. 30 September bounded remaining-scope review: Biosecurity Act 2011 section 107(2)–(4) preserves compatible old subsidiary rules, treating relevant import-treatment provisions as section 26 specifications. The 1976 Plants Ordinance itself was repealed; its sections 6 and 14 depend on notices/permits and its two schedules are forms/export fees, not an applicable tea specification. The missing existing specification therefore may be a saved older instrument, not necessarily a new 2011 publication. No such operative tea notice was recovered. 30 September definition/ISPM review: read full Act sections 2, 9, 24–31, 80 and 107 against the verified ISPM 32 Category 1 tea example. Section 26(3)(b) requires regard to international standards; it does not replace the import-specification mechanism or exclude manufactured plant products from section 2. ISPM 32 does not itself establish whether the existing Kiribati specification permits this parcel without special paperwork. No new request to inaccessible agriculture routes was made. Retained unresolved without adding a hypothetical food-law blocker. 30 September additional primary review: Biosecurity Act 2011 section 2 unconditionally includes manufactured plant products among regulated articles. Section 26 determines whether particular goods require a permit or PC, not a blanket permit rule. Section 27 expressly requires advance written application if the type has no specification. Sections 26(8), 79 and 80 provide a public register and office/Island Council inspection routes; electronic keeping is permissive. Compatible earlier instruments may survive through section 107. Re-read the full enacted statute including the public-register mechanism and current ministry incoming-mail/permit service page. The saved trade-portal procedure 119/step 141 is a meat workflow, not a general plant-food import specification. No public general processing exemption or complete online register was established; no repeated failed source routes pursued.

<a id="km"></a>
### Comoros Islands (KM)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for the stated sealed 100 g plain Japanese industrially steamed, rolled and dried Camellia tea, by the combined national-law/ISPM 32 interpretation. No permit, phytosanitary certificate, independent food certificate, advance plant-agency notice or special registration is established for this nonregulated manufactured product. This does not apply to raw/dried-only herbs, seeds, fresh leaves, blends or contaminated packaging. Package information must satisfy Food Law Articles 126–129, including translation into at least one official language (French is a practical option), identity/composition, manufacturer, origin/processing location, durability/storage/use, lot and accurate quantity.

**Document-rule sources:** [justice.gouv.km 1](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/decret-n-17-103pr-du-5-octobre-2917-promulguant-la-loi-n-17-004au-portant-protection-des-vegetaux-aux-comores.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 2](<https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n17-103-pr-du-5-octobre-2017-promulguant-la-loi-n17-004-au-portant-protection-des-vegetaux-aux-comores/>) · [justice.gouv.km 3](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi-n-17-005au-du-10-juin-2017-portant-legislation-alimentaire-en-union-des-comores.pdf>) · [douane.gov.km 4](<https://douane.gov.km/produits%20interdits.php>) · [wto.org 5](<https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/com_e/WT_ACC_COM_37_LEG1.pdf>) · [wto.org 6](<https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/com_e/WT_ACC_COM_37_LEG7.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 7](<https://justice.gouv.km/texte/loi-n21%E2%80%90013-au-du-29-juin-2021-sur-le-commerce-exterieur-promulguee-par-le-decret-n22%E2%80%90004-pr-du-18-janvier-2022/>) · [justice.gouv.km 8](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/code_des_douanes_union_des_comores_2024.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 9](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi-n-11-001au-du-26-mars-2011-portant-code-de-la-sante-2011.pdf>) · [ippc.int 10](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 11](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Combined-source legal interpretation: Plant Law 17-004/AU Article 3 limits manufactured plant products to those capable of carrying the relevant pest risk. Article 4 directs undefined terms to the IPPC/ISPMs; Article 39 requires phytosanitary measures to rest on pest-risk analysis and/or comply with current ISPMs; Article 46 applies that same standard to certificates. Read with the current-linked ISPM 32 Category 1 tea illustration and the actual industrial steaming/rolling/drying, plain manufactured sencha is inferred outside the regulated product class that triggers Article 44 permits/certificates. This is an explicit processing/classification inference, not a tea-specific Comoros ruling. Food Law 17-005/AU includes personal food but Article 63 refers plant foods to phytosanitary regulation and the examined certificate provisions do not create a separate universal private-tea certificate.

**Personal post:** The manufacturing-based interpretation applies to the goods, including paid personal postal orders; it does not borrow a passenger allowance. Plant Law Article 59 expressly covers post when imported goods are regulated. Ordinary customs declaration, lawful entry, arrival inspection and food safety remain applicable. Food Law Article 6(I)(g) includes personal consumption.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Food Law 17-005/AU Article 6(I)(g) includes personal use, so private consumption is not a general food-law exemption. Article 63 subjects raw/processed plant foods to phytosanitary regulation; Articles 71–72 apply previously specified certificates and market/distribution conditions, while Article 73 checks certificates supplied by importing businesses. None creates an independent unqualified certificate duty for the private plain-tea buyer. Article 126 requires package identity/composition/manufacturer, origin/processing location, durability/storage/use and lot information; Articles 127–128 require truthful information and accurate quantity; Article 129 requires information in at least one official language. Health Code 2011 Article 43 approval concerns additives, not plain tea. Foreign Trade Law 21-013/AU Articles 16–19 delegate technical-control lists/methods; delegation alone is not a document duty. Blank-number/date 2022 WTO-filed drafts are not adopted regulations and cannot establish one.

**Evidence access:** Official Ministry source-index text read for articles 16–19 and current Customs Code excerpt. Direct full trade-law page retrieval failed; no new full adopted implementing instrument was downloaded.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [justice.gouv.km 1](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/decret-n-17-103pr-du-5-octobre-2917-promulguant-la-loi-n-17-004au-portant-protection-des-vegetaux-aux-comores.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 2](<https://justice.gouv.km/texte/decret-n17-103-pr-du-5-octobre-2017-promulguant-la-loi-n17-004-au-portant-protection-des-vegetaux-aux-comores/>) · [justice.gouv.km 3](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi-n-17-005au-du-10-juin-2017-portant-legislation-alimentaire-en-union-des-comores.pdf>) · [douane.gov.km 4](<https://douane.gov.km/produits%20interdits.php>) · [wto.org 5](<https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/com_e/WT_ACC_COM_37_LEG1.pdf>) · [wto.org 6](<https://www.wto.org/english/thewto_e/acc_e/com_e/WT_ACC_COM_37_LEG7.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 7](<https://justice.gouv.km/texte/loi-n21%E2%80%90013-au-du-29-juin-2021-sur-le-commerce-exterieur-promulguee-par-le-decret-n22%E2%80%90004-pr-du-18-janvier-2022/>) · [justice.gouv.km 8](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/code_des_douanes_union_des_comores_2024.pdf>) · [justice.gouv.km 9](<https://justice.gouv.km/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/loi-n-11-001au-du-26-mars-2011-portant-code-de-la-sante-2011.pdf>) · [ippc.int 10](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 11](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

<a id="kn"></a>
### Saint Christopher and Nevis (KN)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Act 10 of 2024 section 2 limits plants to living plants/parts and includes manufactured plant products only if their nature or processing may introduce or spread pests. Sections 16–17 impose permit/certificate duties within those covered categories. Applying the actual sencha manufacturing evidence and ISPM 32 tea example to that risk-qualified definition supports treating this finished tea as outside the plant-product permit/certificate class. A local tea-named waiver is not logically necessary when the statutory definition already excludes the factual product. The regulated-article definition also contains the capability/deemed-necessary qualification, and packaging is defined by use with covered articles; clean new retail/postal packaging does not independently establish a permit duty for an otherwise uncovered food. Section 15 and 17(4) science/standards principles reinforce this reading but are not an independent exemption. The Department of Agriculture’s 2020 import notice expressly covers other unprocessed plant products, consistent with this distinction. Its separate dried-tea statement concerns exports and is not used as an import waiver. The actual product is pure Camellia sinensis sencha manufactured by steaming to arrest enzyme activity, repeated rolling/pressing with hot air, and drying, then supplied sealed for infusion. MAFF describes this manufacture. ISPM 32 section 2 and Appendix 2 identify manufactured tea as Category 1, supporting the factual conclusion that this differs from merely dried raw botanical material. The international standard is used as technical evidence when applying the local definition, not as an automatically binding local exemption. Ordinary inspection, food safety and pest freedom remain conditions; this is not a route for raw/sun-dried leaves, seeds, blends or contaminated goods.

**Document-rule sources:** [sknis.gov.kn 1](<https://www.sknis.gov.kn/2020/06/26/plant-and-plant-product-import-requirements/>) · [lawcommission.gov.kn 2](<https://lawcommission.gov.kn/wp-content/documents/Annual-Laws/2024/ACTs/Act-10-2024-Plant-Protection-Act.pdf>) · [lawcommission.gov.kn 3](<https://lawcommission.gov.kn/wp-content/documents/Act02and09TOC/Ch-09_26-Sale-of-Food-Drugs-Act.pdf>) · [lawcommission.gov.kn 4](<https://lawcommission.gov.kn/wp-content/documents/Revised-Acts-of-St-Kitts-and-Nevis/Revised-Acts-of-St-Kitts-and-Nevis-2020/Ch-09_21-Public-Health-Act.pdf>) · [miticca.gov.kn 5](<https://www.miticca.gov.kn/wp-content/uploads/2021/07/Import-Export-Procedures-Manual.pdf>) · [ippc.int 6](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/saint-kitts-and-nevis/reportingobligation/4>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/saint-kitts-and-nevis/reportingobligation/2012/12/phytosanitary-requirements/>) · [assets.ippc.int 8](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/04/10/1133187921307_PHYTOSANITARY_REQUIREMENTS_2013042321-18En.doc>) · [ippc.int 9](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/saint-kitts-and-nevis/reportingobligation/2012/12/phytosanitary-restrictions/>) · [assets.ippc.int 10](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2015/04/10/1133187600333_PHYTOSANITARY_RESTRICTIONS_AND_PROHIBITIONS_2013042321-18En.doc>) · [gov.si 11](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastlin/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 12](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

Act 10 of 2024 section 2 limits plants to living plants/parts and includes manufactured plant products only if their nature or processing may introduce or spread pests. Sections 16–17 impose permit/certificate duties within those covered categories. Applying the actual sencha manufacturing evidence and ISPM 32 tea example to that risk-qualified definition supports treating this finished tea as outside the plant-product permit/certificate class. A local tea-named waiver is not logically necessary when the statutory definition already excludes the factual product. The regulated-article definition also contains the capability/deemed-necessary qualification, and packaging is defined by use with covered articles; clean new retail/postal packaging does not independently establish a permit duty for an otherwise uncovered food. Section 15 and 17(4) science/standards principles reinforce this reading but are not an independent exemption. The Department of Agriculture’s 2020 import notice expressly covers other unprocessed plant products, consistent with this distinction. Its separate dried-tea statement concerns exports and is not used as an import waiver.

**Personal post:** This conclusion follows commodity scope in the applicable local law, not a baggage, gift or noncommercial-seller exception. It applies to the stated private direct postal purchase and clean new packaging.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

### South Korea (KR)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The cited agricultural rule supports this finished retail tea without an import permit, phytosanitary certificate or special agricultural approval; ordinary postal/customs declarations and the stated product/quantity conditions remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [ebook.qia.go.kr 1](<https://ebook.qia.go.kr/src/viewer/main.php?category=1&host=main&page=330&search=AI&site=20260107_101021>) · [customs.go.kr 2](<https://customs.go.kr/kcs/cm/cntnts/cntntsView.do?cntntsId=2807&mi=8457>)

QIA 2026 plant-quarantine compilation, page 330, exempts dried or steam-treated retail tea sealed in polypropylene, foil, cans or equivalent packaging under processed products unable to harbor pests. Verify the manufacturer pouch is equivalent sealed packaging.

**Personal post:** This is a commodity exemption, not a baggage concession. Postal customs personal-use thresholds still apply.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 5 kg product weight. Personal-use agricultural product threshold under Customs simplified clearance. Separate Korea Customs personal-use agricultural-product clearance threshold, not a phytosanitary exemption limit.

**Checkout:** 50 pouches / 5 kg tea; highest carrier band 6.08 kg gross. Current maximum is within this numeric threshold; this does not settle permits, value limits, frequency, packaging or genuine personal use.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

<a id="kw"></a>
### Kuwait (KW)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

PAFN Resolution 6/2023 Article 10 expressly covers personal noncommercial food but still requires the recipient to register identity and all food details with the Imported Food Department and give a written personal-use undertaking. PAFN operates a separate approved individual-account and personal clearance-request process. This special food-agency registration/clearance route is outside the store operating constraint. Article 10 only expressly exempts Articles 12–13 labelling, not all import documents; the scope of the 31 March 2026 temporary document relaxation remains unverified, so this decision does not rely on asserting that every Article 18 certificate is still required.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/kuw221743E.pdf>) · [raqeb.pafn.gov.kw 2](<https://raqeb.pafn.gov.kw/ar/Pages/Individuals.aspx>) · [betacweb.pafn.gov.kw 3](<https://betacweb.pafn.gov.kw/en/Pages/default.aspx>) · [customs.gov.kw 4](<https://www.customs.gov.kw/PdfViewer?FileName=CustomsAnnouncement/9413357300_pdfFile.pdf>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. PAFN Resolution 6/2023 Article 10 permits small personal-food consignments within authority-set quantities, with recipient name, residence, civil ID, food details and a written personal-use undertaking; inspection may apply. That food concession does not expressly waive plant quarantine.

**Personal post:** MAFF evidence is Japan postal manufactured green tea. PAFN Articles 8–10 concern non-commercial imported food and are not limited to accompanied baggage.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No ordinary-post-only route is supported because of the separate personal-food agency registration and clearance procedure. The precise scope of the 2026 temporary certificate exception and the agricultural processing exemption remain legal questions, not instructions for the store to obtain documents.

**Sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [pafn.gov.kw 2](<https://www.pafn.gov.kw/assets/images/Uploads/40118122204.pdf>) · [assets.ippc.int 3](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2025/10/27/The_regulation_of_imported_food.pdf>)

<a id="ky"></a>
### Cayman Islands (KY)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported as of 30 September 2026 by the current DoA processed-tea guidance: sealed commercial packaging, ordinary declaration and arrival agricultural inspection. The new September Gazette does not change current operation: Commencement Order 2026 paragraph 2 starts Act 2023 on 1 November 2026; Regulations 2026 regulation 1(2) immediately follows. The future law has a risk-qualified manufactured-product category and Director exemption powers, so this review does not independently establish the processed-tea outcome after that transition.

**Document-rule sources:** [doa.gov.ky 1](<https://doa.gov.ky/faqs/>) · [doa.gov.ky 2](<https://doa.gov.ky/service/agricultural-health-inspection-services/>) · [legislation.gov.ky 3](<https://legislation.gov.ky/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1983/1983-0029/1983-0029.pdf>) · [doa.gov.ky 4](<https://doa.gov.ky/wp-content/uploads/2023/09/FAQ-AHIS-May-24-2022.pdf>) · [gov.ky 5](<https://gov.ky/documents/35692/0/PlantProtectionAct2023%28Commencement%29Order2026_MADE.pdf/38f453eb-ffb3-c44c-a7f9-d8da8397ab7b?t=1790010790398>) · [gov.ky 6](<https://gov.ky/documents/35692/0/PlantProtectionRegulations2026_MADE.pdf/76eafe43-f892-f897-e542-cc9ed529849f?t=1790010649002>) · [legislation.gov.ky 7](<https://legislation.gov.ky/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/2023/2023-0010/2023-0010.pdf>) · [doa.gov.ky 8](<https://doa.gov.ky/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/Conditions-to-Import-Low-Risk-Plant-Products.pdf>)

As of 30 September 2026, DoA explicitly allows commercially packaged processed tea, subject to declaration to Customs and agricultural arrival inspection. The newly published Plant Protection Act 2023 and Regulations 2026 start on 1 November 2026, not their 21 September publication date. Review the processed-tea position under the incoming regime before relying on this conclusion for imports on or after 1 November.

**Personal post:** DoA places its processed-tea answer under general Import/Export, separately from its expressly baggage-only concessions. Applying this general commodity guidance to a paid private postal packet is a stated scope inference; it gives no baggage limitation or separate permit/certificate requirement for packaged processed tea. The incoming Act is also not restricted to commercial imports, but is not yet operative on the review date.

**Checkout:** 82 pouches / 8.2 kg tea; highest carrier band 10 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

<a id="lb"></a>
### Lebanon (LB)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The actual enacted Decision 101/1 Article 1 requires an appropriate listed food certificate with every imported consignment, while Article 2 expressly covers packaged tea. Customs’ official HS 0902 entry corroborates the origin-country certificate requirement. Health/health-supervision, manufactured-product export or HACCP certification are relevant listed alternatives; the quality-certificate option specifically concerns frozen fruit/vegetables. The decision contains no personal-use or small-quantity exception. Applying its unqualified wording to a paid personal postal tea order is an explicit legal-scope inference. Decision 208/1 of 4 May 2009 changes only Article 2’s first paragraph on laboratory testing, leaving Article 1 intact; Decision 349/1 of 8 August 2022 regulates laboratory-result validity/retesting without waiving food certificates. The store cannot satisfy this independent requirement without certificates. This is not a finding that tea is prohibited, nor a resolution of manufactured tea’s separate phytosanitary classification. LibanPost’s unspecified preapproval threshold does not establish a certificate exemption.

**Document-rule sources:** [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 1](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/Backoffice-mvc/API/File/Law%20778_20220819115205.pdf>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 2](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/en>) · [agriculture.gov.lb 3](<https://www.agriculture.gov.lb/Media/News/2026/%D9%88%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%B1%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D8%AA%D9%85%D8%AF-%D8%AA%D8%AF%D8%A7%D8%A8%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%AB%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%AA%D8%B3%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D9%8A%D8%B1%D8%A7>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 4](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/en/product-details/173?headerId=4>) · [customs.gov.lb 5](<https://www.customs.gov.lb/Files/Government_Controls/Circular_No1_16-07-2021.pdf>) · [libanpost.com 6](<https://libanpost.com/english/tools-support>) · [libanpost.com 7](<https://www.libanpost.com/Library/Assets/Documents/ImportToLebanon.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/leb81057.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/leb88608.pdf>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 10](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/backoffice-mvc/API/File/decision%20349%20full_20220912141057.pdf>)

Law 778/2006 Article 2 risk-qualifies manufactured plant products; Article 4 includes postal imports and Article 9 requires phytosanitary certification for covered products. Finished sencha’s phytosanitary classification remains unresolved. Independently, enacted Ministry of Agriculture Decision 101/1 of 17 March 2007 Article 1 requires a listed food certificate with every imported consignment; its Article 2 table expressly includes packaged tea. The official Customs compilation applies this rule to HS 0902 packaged tea and identifies the certificates as originating in the country of origin. This independent food-certificate duty prevents a no-certificate route under the broad scope of the enacted rule.

**Personal post:** Plant Law 778 Article 4 expressly includes public/private post and traveller consignments, but finished-tea pest-risk classification remains unresolved. Decision 101/1 Article 1 uses an unqualified every-imported-consignment duty and contains no personal-use, postal or quantity exception in its complete two-page text. Applying that food-certificate rule to a paid 100 g sealed tea parcel for personal consumption is a stated legal-scope inference; personal postal tea is not separately named in that decision. LibanPost’s unspecified food-preapproval threshold concerns a separate approval and supplies no waiver from the independent food-certificate duty.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** The phytosanitary classification of manufactured sencha under Law 778 and LibanPost’s separate food-preapproval threshold remain unresolved. Neither issue removes the independent food-certificate barrier identified in Decision 101/1. No personal/postal waiver from that unqualified certificate duty was located.

**Sources:** [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 1](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/Backoffice-mvc/API/File/Law%20778_20220819115205.pdf>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 2](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/en>) · [agriculture.gov.lb 3](<https://www.agriculture.gov.lb/Media/News/2026/%D9%88%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%B1%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D8%A9-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D8%AA%D9%85%D8%AF-%D8%AA%D8%AF%D8%A7%D8%A8%D9%8A%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%AB%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%84%D8%AA%D8%B3%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%B9-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D9%8A%D8%B1%D8%A7>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 4](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/en/product-details/173?headerId=4>) · [customs.gov.lb 5](<https://www.customs.gov.lb/Files/Government_Controls/Circular_No1_16-07-2021.pdf>) · [libanpost.com 6](<https://libanpost.com/english/tools-support>) · [libanpost.com 7](<https://www.libanpost.com/Library/Assets/Documents/ImportToLebanon.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/leb81057.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/leb88608.pdf>) · [regulations.agriculture.gov.lb 10](<https://regulations.agriculture.gov.lb/backoffice-mvc/API/File/decision%20349%20full_20220912141057.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read current ministry-hosted Law 778 definitions, postal scope and certificate provisions. Checked ministry product portal (green tea listed) and 14 March 2026 temporary certificate-copy measure; neither establishes personal finished-tea exemption. 30 September: The Ministry of Agriculture green-tea commodity page (HS 090210/090220) provides labelling and laboratory rules but no explicit personal-postal phytosanitary exemption. Its health-certificate decision concerns EXPORTS from Lebanon, so it cannot establish an import requirement or waiver for Japanese sencha. Located Lebanon Customs 2021 consolidated controls, page 101: packaged tea HS 0902 appears under laboratory examination and one of origin export/quality/HACCP certificates (Decision 1/101 of 3 April 2007). Its exact personal-parcel applicability and interaction with 2022/349 food-lab rules require verification; it is not used to conclude that all private tea parcels require certificates. 30 September final review: downloaded and read the actual Customs consolidated controls PDF. Pages 98–99 list HS 0902 agricultural endorsement; page 101 lists packaged tea for laboratory examination plus an origin export/quality/HACCP certificate under Decision 1/101 (3 April 2007); page 102 lists tea pesticide-residue testing under Decision 1/586 (31 July 2018). No explicit personal waiver appears in these entries. Read current LibanPost support page and its linked ImportToLebanon guide: food exceeding a minimum threshold requires prior Ministry of Commerce approval, but the amount is not stated. Did not assume this threshold is exceeded by 100 g. The ministry’s 2022/349 laboratory validity provisions and 2026 temporary acceptance of certificate copies do not resolve that personal scope question. Recovered and visually read the complete original Decision 101/1 from Official Gazette No 18 of 29 March 2007, printed pp 1774–1775 (FAOLEX leb 81057.pdf). Article 1 expressly requires a listed certificate with every imported consignment. Article 2’s table includes packaged coffee, tea, herbs and spices; no personal-use, postal or small-quantity exception appears in the complete text. Applying the broad duty to this personal postal order is a legal-scope inference, supported by Customs’ explicit HS 0902 listing. Corrected the instrument date from the Customs compilation: the original decision is signed 17 March 2007 and was published 29 March 2007; Customs’ 3 April 2007 date is inconsistent with the original. The later Decision 208/1 also identifies Decision 101/1 as dated 17 March 2007. Recovered and visually read Decision 208/1 of 4 May 2009, Official Gazette No 21 of 7 May 2009, pp 3131–3132 (FAOLEX leb 88608.pdf). Its Article 1 substitutes only the first paragraph of Article 2 of Decision 101/1, establishing initial testing and subsequent six-month testing for the described sealed branded products; the Article 2 table remains unchanged. It does not amend Article 1’s certificate duty. Downloaded and visually read the actual two-page Decision 349/1 of 8 August 2022 linked by the current Ministry green-tea page. It sets the validity of laboratory results in customs custody and requires retesting after expiry. Its repeal clause addresses conflicting provisions, especially Decision 833/1 of 11 September 2012; nothing in this text waives the independent food-certificate duty. Independent consistency review concluded that retaining the agricultural status as not_verified while excluding the no-certificate shipping route accurately distinguishes the still-unresolved phytosanitary pest-risk question from the separate enacted food-certificate requirement. LibanPost’s unspecified approval threshold is not treated as exceeded by 100 g and is not used as a certificate waiver.

<a id="lc"></a>
### Saint Lucia (LC)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude from the no-special-document route on a conservative, explicit inference that intact steamed/dried tea leaves remain tea parts under the current Schedule 3 row. That row prohibits non-propagative tea parts from all countries, and regulation 7(5) preserves Schedule 3 restrictions despite food processing. This is not a claim that the NPPO has specifically ruled on packaged sencha: the Act’s pest-capability definition and the 2010 discussion of cooked foods are recorded caveats, but no published sencha exemption was established.

**Document-rule sources:** [attorneygeneralchambers.com 1](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/schedule-3-01>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 2](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-7-01>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 3](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-2>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 4](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-12>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 5](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-16-01>) · [govt.lc 6](<https://www.govt.lc/media.govt.lc/www/resources/legislation/PlantProtectionAct.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/lc3-1995-66cons1995-71pqreg.pdf>) · [caribbeaninvasives.org 8](<https://caribbeaninvasives.org/wp-content/uploads/2012/09/NISS-Pathways-final-2010.pdf>)

Saint Lucia’s current Revised Laws 2023, Plant Protection Regulations Schedule 3, expressly prohibits tea (Thea sinensis / Camellia spp.) parts other than vegetative propagation material and seed from all countries. The same schedule expressly exempts some processed commodities, including roasted coffee and milled rice; it gives no processed-tea exception. Applying the tea-leaf row to steamed/dried loose-leaf sencha is an explicit scope inference. The Act’s pest-capability definition and a 2010 project report’s discussion of cooked foods remain interpretation caveats, not verified sencha exemptions.

**Personal post:** Act section 4 and regulation 12 expressly apply the plant rules to mail. No small personal-post exception to the tea schedule was found. Ordinary customs declaration and inspection do not remove a Schedule 3 restriction.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** To reverse this conservative exclusion, the NPPO would need a published determination that commercially steamed/dried Camellia leaves fall outside the Schedule 3 tea-parts prohibition and applicable permit/certificate controls. The existing cooking discussion does not identify sencha or override the specific schedule.

**Sources:** [attorneygeneralchambers.com 1](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/schedule-3-01>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 2](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-7-01>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 3](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-2>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 4](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-12>) · [attorneygeneralchambers.com 5](<https://attorneygeneralchambers.com/laws-of-saint-lucia/plant-protection-act/section-16-01>) · [govt.lc 6](<https://www.govt.lc/media.govt.lc/www/resources/legislation/PlantProtectionAct.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/lc3-1995-66cons1995-71pqreg.pdf>) · [caribbeaninvasives.org 8](<https://caribbeaninvasives.org/wp-content/uploads/2012/09/NISS-Pathways-final-2010.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read the Plant Protection Act product definition and regulations 12 (mail) and 16 (permit). The pest-carrying threshold leaves heat-processed retail tea classification unresolved. Read the Attorney General’s current Revised Laws 2023 Schedule 3 tea row directly, and compared its express roasted-coffee and milled/polished/parboiled-rice exceptions. Verified the matching tea row on printed page 320 of the original regulations hosted by Germany’s NPPO. This is tea-specific adverse evidence, not a generic assumption about all plant products. Read current regulation 7(5): processed fruits and vegetables made innocuous as pest carriers are enterable only if not otherwise prohibited, particularly by Schedule 3. Read regulation 17 linking the schedule to pest-risk crops, and mail regulation 12. The official 2005 consolidated PDF has placeholders instead of much of Schedule 3, so the current AG HTML and original SI text supply the tea row. Read Saint Lucia National Invasive Species Strategy—Pathways (Guy Mathurin, September 2010), printed page 20: dried herbs are treated as minimally processed with required permits, whereas cooking/canning/pickling/baking can remove plant-health inspection. It does not establish which category steamed-and-dried sencha occupies and is not a commodity-specific legal exemption. Retained this limitation alongside the exact current tea schedule. Targeted current ministry, Attorney General, tea/processed-food and personal-post searches found no published manufactured-tea or small-parcel override. Corrected the broken definition URL ending section-2-02 to section-2.

Processed plant products, explicitly including dried tea, need no phytosanitary certificate or phytosanitary inspection. Switzerland and Liechtenstein share the plant-health import system.

<a id="mc"></a>
### Monaco (MC)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Legal inference: the France–Monaco Customs Convention Articles 1 and 3 apply French customs and sanitary rules in Monaco. Under the French/EU regime, manufactured dried tea does not need phytosanitary certification; fresh tea-leaf controls do not describe steamed/dried sencha. This is a personal-consumption route; comply with the stated packaging and quantity conditions and ordinary postal/customs declarations.

### Moldova (MD)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Decision 938/2018 Annex 5 permits personal, non-commercial plant-product consignments up to 5 kg without a phytosanitary certificate, excluding seeds and planting material. Plain dried sencha fits the non-propagating plant-product scope; quarantine inspection can still occur. This is a personal-consumption route; comply with the stated packaging and quantity conditions and ordinary postal/customs declarations.

Decision 938/2018 Annex 5 permits personal, non-commercial plant-product consignments up to 5 kg without a phytosanitary certificate, excluding seeds and planting material. Plain dried sencha fits the non-propagating plant-product scope; quarantine inspection can still occur.

**Personal post:** Annex 5 expressly includes small consignments ordered at a distance and delivered to consumers through postal services.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 5 kg product weight. Personal plant-product consignment; phytosanitary certificate exemption, not a carrier or tax allowance.

Saint Martin is outside the EU plant-health territory. A Dutch NPPO country sheet lists specific dried plant goods needing certification, but does not list tea and dates its substantive rules to 2012; that cannot verify the present Japanese postal route. Current Code rural L 273-6-2 expressly applies the overseas plant-health framework in L 271-5 to L 271-8 to Saint Martin; this confirms the governing framework. The unresolved question concerns the separate local restriction, not absence of a finished-tea line in the national certificate schedules. Organic Law 2007-223 Article 18 IX preserves applicable pre-2007 provisions and substitutes the new territorial references. This supplies a continuity basis for inherited national schedules, whose finished-tea list-based treatment does not itself require a new tea-specific ruling. Current Code rural expressly adopts a risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. Its statutory 2020 commencement conflicts with current Ministry 2026-165 guidance retaining the older framework; neither claim is omitted. This does not establish a tea prohibition.

**Personal post:** The continuity of national scheduled rules does not by itself resolve any inherited local personal-import restriction. The exact scope of the 1996 Guadeloupe order for a paid private postal purchase remains unverified.

**Research trail:** Reviewed the full NVWA Saint Martin country sheet, including its 2012/2013 revision history and third-country plant-product list; searched French local import guidance. Current tea-specific scope remains unverified. Second review searched 2025/2026 local tea exclusions. The 2025 ministry overseas instruction explicitly excludes Saint Barthélemy/Saint Martin regulations from its analysis; it does not extend a mainland exemption. Read current Code rural L 273-1 to L 273-7, including L 273-6-2’s express extension of the overseas plant-health framework. This strengthens the legal-framework evidence but is not a tea-specific schedule or a personal-post exemption. 2026-09-30 bounded independent French-territory peer review: Organic Law 2007-223 Article 18 IX supplies express continuity and territorial-reference substitution for pre-existing Guadeloupe rules; the official October 2025 Guide de legistique confirms this rule for Saint-Martin. Current Code rural L 273-6-2 additionally applies the overseas framework. This is a legal chain for using inherited national schedules, not a mainland-EU substitution. Those list-based rules do not require a tea-specific permission simply because the territory changed status. The same continuity can preserve the local 1996 Guadeloupe restriction, whose original territory/channel scope remains unrecovered. 30 September fresh domestic-definition review: read currentL 271-7 and 2019-1110 original/currentArticle 6, ratification by 2023-171 Art 39, and official indexed 2026-165/2026-278 implementation statements. Verified 2025-102 abrogated, with 2026-165 current. New actualTeaCategory 1 evidence supports a general processing inference, but present statute/agency implementation and concrete local-rule scope conflict persists; not merely membership or a missing bespoke tea ruling. Source/territory findings in french-definition-review/peer-conclusions.json.

<a id="mg"></a>
### Madagascar (MG)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Not compatible with the no-certificate constraint: final enacted Law 2017-048 article 12 requires origin food-safety certification, independently of the unresolved phytosanitary classification. Applying the unqualified imported-food rule to a private postal purchase is an explicit legal-scope inference; no import exemption follows from the domestic private-preparation exception. The decision is based on the foreign-authority document duty, not on ordinary arrival inspection or the separate market-placement certificate.

**Document-rule sources:** [pic.commerce.mg 1](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/L86-017.pdf>) · [douanes.gov.mg 2](<https://www.douanes.gov.mg/prohibitions-et-interdictions/>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mad159986.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 4](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/D2013-260.pdf>) · [douanes.gov.mg 5](<https://www.douanes.gov.mg/srcs/uploads/2026/01/TARIF-DES-DOUANES-2026.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 6](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/A4736-2002.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/mg3-2002-4736einfuhranforderungen_fr.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 8](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/fr/node?page=97>) · [pic.commerce.mg 9](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/L2017-048-VF.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 10](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/fr/loi-ndeg-2017-048-regissant-la-securite-sanitaire-des-denrees-alimentaireset-de-lalimentation>) · [hcc.gov.mg 11](<https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=3130>) · [textes.lexxika.com 12](<https://textes.lexxika.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Decret-2018-592-28-Juin-2018-Regissant-les-controles-sanitaires-officiels-des-denrees-alimentaires-dorigine-vegetale.pdf>)

Ordinance 86-013 and Decree 86-310 include products prepared by drying. Adopted Order 4736/2002 articles 3–4 require an import permit and origin phytosanitary certificate for covered plant products; article 6 repeats the certificate requirement. The complete annex provides tea conditions for propagation material/seeds, not manufactured drinking tea, and no general manufactured-tea exemption was found. Some other commodities have specific inspection-only conditions. The current Customs summary names certificate controls for chapters 6, 7, 8 and 24.01 but does not settle HS 0902 tea. Whether steamed/dried sencha remains in the regulated simple-preparation category is unresolved.

**Personal post:** The law covers food imports through the final consumer and does not restrict articles 11–12 to resale or business importers. Applying that general scope to this paid personal postal purchase is an inference. Domestic preparation/handling/storage for private consumption is excluded; importing purchased food is not that activity.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Law 2017-048, promulgated 8 February 2018, article 12 requires every imported food to have documents certifying origin and food safety issued by the competent authority of the country of provenance. Article 11 separately requires prior authorization. Article 2 covers imported food through the final consumer; its private-use exclusions concern domestic primary production, domestic preparation, handling and storage, not importing purchased food. Article 66 expressly includes raw or processed plant foods for human consumption. Decree 2018-592 articles 38–43 corroborate incoming plant-food consignment controls: article 40 calls for accompanying sanitary certificates, and article 43 requires documentary checks of all consignments. These incoming-consignment provisions are distinguished from the decree’s earlier export-control chapters; final Law article 12 remains decisive.

**Evidence access:** The decisive source is the downloaded final official PIC-hosted law, not the FAO draft with unfilled enactment metadata. The implementing decree is a CNLEGIS text mirrored by Lexxika; the final law independently establishes the origin-certificate duty. Decree 2018-592 has both earlier export-control chapters and incoming-consignment provisions at articles 38–43; only the latter corroborate the import finding, while Law article 12 independently establishes it.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Agricultural classification remains unresolved: Which current commodity condition applies to manufactured steamed/dried tea, and is its private postal import exempt from the general permit/certificate pathway? The independent food/conformity/registration requirement already defeats the no-special-document scenario.

**Sources:** [pic.commerce.mg 1](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/L86-017.pdf>) · [douanes.gov.mg 2](<https://www.douanes.gov.mg/prohibitions-et-interdictions/>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mad159986.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 4](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/D2013-260.pdf>) · [douanes.gov.mg 5](<https://www.douanes.gov.mg/srcs/uploads/2026/01/TARIF-DES-DOUANES-2026.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 6](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/A4736-2002.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/mg3-2002-4736einfuhranforderungen_fr.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 8](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/fr/node?page=97>) · [pic.commerce.mg 9](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/sites/default/files/2023-01/L2017-048-VF.pdf>) · [pic.commerce.mg 10](<https://www.pic.commerce.mg/fr/loi-ndeg-2017-048-regissant-la-securite-sanitaire-des-denrees-alimentaireset-de-lalimentation>) · [hcc.gov.mg 11](<https://www.hcc.gov.mg/?p=3130>) · [textes.lexxika.com 12](<https://textes.lexxika.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Decret-2018-592-28-Juin-2018-Regissant-les-controles-sanitaires-officiels-des-denrees-alimentaires-dorigine-vegetale.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read product scope and articles 18,20,22; searched processed-tea and personal-mail derogations. Cross-checked current customs commodity restrictions; no explicit HS 0902 entry establishing the finished-tea requirement was found. 30 September: read complete Order 4736/2002, including Annex IV Camellia sinensis entry on printed pages 43–44. It addresses propagation material and seeds, not finished drinking tea; it does not resolve the manufactured-sencha classification. Also reviewed Decree 2013-260 food-safety agency controls, articles 39–44; personal-post application remains to be established. 30 September: read the current 2026 customs tariff green-tea entry, which supplies tax rates but no dispositive plant-health exemption. ANMCC safeguard notices examined concern pasta and condensed milk rather than tea; they were not extended to this product. Independently downloaded and compared the complete 56-page FAO and 55-page German NPPO copies of Order 4736/2002, plus the official PIC-hosted 4-page body. The search title PROJET is stale PDF metadata: all actual texts bear adopted instrument number 4736/2002, date 7 October 2002, and the minister signature attribution. PIC indexes it as a ministerial order. Read the full annex beyond tea propagation; no blanket processed/dried-food exemption was found, but specific milled-rice/grain entries show why an unlisted processed commodity must not automatically receive the propagation conditions. Preserved the unresolved tea classification rather than using the document-title artefact to dismiss the order or claiming that every processed food is exempt. 30 September independent food/conformity review: Recovered the complete final CNLEGIS law from the official PIC portal after detecting adoption/HCC placeholders in the earlier FAO copy. Final copy records parliamentary adoption on 7 and 13 December 2017, HCC decision 09-HCC/D 3 of 31 January 2018, and presidential promulgation on 8 February 2018. Compared final articles 2, 3, 11, 12 and 66. Read the complete 23-page CNLEGIS copy of Decree 2018-592 from a legal mirror, including import articles 38–45; transit/transshipment procedural derogations do not supply a personal-post exemption.

<a id="mh"></a>
### Marshall Islands (MH)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for fully manufactured shelf-stable tea in an unopened hermetically sealed retail pouch, declared for personal use. QP 8 requires inspection rather than a phytosanitary certificate for stored dried foods. The reviewed enabling law and food law do not add a personal-recipient licence or general health-certificate requirement.

**Document-rule sources:** [rmicourts.org 1](<https://rmicourts.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/05/Plant-and-Animal-Quarantine-Regulations.pdf>) · [rminitijela.com 2](<https://rminitijela.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/1966-0004_2.pdf>) · [rminitijela.com 3](<https://rminitijela.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/2010-0037_1.pdf>)

Quarantine Regulation 20(g)(i) exempts fully manufactured, hermetically sealed shelf-stable foods from import permits. Part IV QP 8 subjects stored dried foods to inspection and treatment if contaminated; its certificate provisions concern fresh produce, propagating plants and seeds, not stored manufactured tea. The Food Safety Act 2010 registers food-business premises, while ordinary personal recipients are not food businesses. The actual pouch must meet the hermetic-seal condition.

**Personal post:** The exemption applies to importation generally; it is not limited to passenger baggage.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** Confirm the actual manufacturer packaging is hermetically sealed; a loosely closed or resealable-only pouch is insufficient evidence.

**Sources:** [rmicourts.org 1](<https://rmicourts.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/05/Plant-and-Animal-Quarantine-Regulations.pdf>) · [rminitijela.com 2](<https://rminitijela.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/1966-0004_2.pdf>) · [rminitijela.com 3](<https://rminitijela.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/2010-0037_1.pdf>)

### Mali (ML)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

An import permit is required for dried plant products, including postal imports. Applying that explicit category to the dried sencha leaves is a stated inference. Because the store will not obtain permits, exclude this destination under the available rule; this is a documentary requirement, not an absolute tea ban.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mli153268.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/ml3-2002-305pqreg_fr-de.pdf>)

Decree 02-305 article 6 requires every importer of plants/plant products to present an import permit; an origin phytosanitary certificate is additionally required where applicable. Article 8 expressly applies this pathway to postal consignments. Treat the scoped identifiable steamed/dried tea leaves as a dried plant product under that definition. This application is an inference from the explicit dried-product wording, not a published sencha-specific determination; steaming and retail packing do not by themselves establish an exemption.

**Personal post:** Decree 02-305 article 8 expressly covers post, and article 6 applies to every person importing covered products. No personal-use or small-parcel exception was established.

<a id="mp"></a>
### Northern Mariana Islands (MP)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The better reading excludes a completely permit-free route. Section 204(a) expressly requires an import permit for a plant or product. In its plant-quarantine context, product reasonably includes manufactured tea without needing a separate bespoke commodity ruling or exact equivalence to the defined term plant material. Section 010(qq) includes leaves without a living/raw limit and 010(ss) expressly includes manufactured plant-origin items. Unlike Guam, these definitions supply no general processing exclusion. Section 238 permits dried food entry subject to pest freedom; it does not waive the independently imposed permit. Section 201 exempts non-regulated articles, not every admissible food. The ISPM 32 tea example supports a low-risk factual classification but is not incorporated as an exemption from this affirmative local permit duty. Section 010(cc) allows an import permit to be written or oral. Section 108(a) requires both oral and written permits for its specified higher-control plant shipments and describes an application route; it does not prove that this 100 g pouch necessarily requires a written permit or an advance application. Nonetheless an oral permit remains an authorization under the separate permit duty, not an automatic synonym for customs declaration or inspection. Sections 104/116/204/501 distinguish declaration, permit compliance, inspection and release. No retrieved provision makes ordinary postal inspection automatically satisfy the permit. The proposed exclusion rests on the user refusing permits at all, not on inventing a mandatory foreign certificate or claiming every permit must be written.

**Document-rule sources:** [cnmilaw.gov 1](<https://cnmilaw.gov/docs/admincode/T85/T85-60.2.pdf>) · [finance.gov.mp 2](<https://www.finance.gov.mp/customs-quarantine.php>) · [finance.gov.mp 3](<https://www.finance.gov.mp/faq.php>) · [finance.gov.mp 4](<https://www.finance.gov.mp/cus-import-requirements.php>) · [fda.gov 5](<https://www.fda.gov/media/161541/download>) · [gov.si 6](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 7](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

CNMI Administrative Code 85-60.2-204(a) requires an import permit for plant products; the broad definitions in 010(qq)/(ss) include leaves and manufactured plant material. Applying those provisions to finished sencha is a reasoned general-category interpretation. Section 238 makes dried food admissible subject to pest freedom but does not waive the permit. The phytosanitary-certificate list in section 202(a) does not describe finished tea.

**Personal post:** The permit provision applies to any person importing the covered goods, and section 501 expressly applies the same regulations to postal entry. No resale or commercial-importer condition is used. A traveler food FAQ is not treated as a postal permit exemption.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [cnmilaw.gov 1](<https://cnmilaw.gov/docs/admincode/T85/T85-60.2.pdf>) · [finance.gov.mp 2](<https://www.finance.gov.mp/customs-quarantine.php>) · [finance.gov.mp 3](<https://www.finance.gov.mp/cus-import-requirements.php>) · [fda.gov 4](<https://www.fda.gov/media/161541/download>) · [gov.si 5](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 6](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

<a id="mq"></a>
### Martinique (MQ)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

The expressly adopted risk-qualified manufactured-product definition and ISPM 32 tea Category 1 provide favorable processing evidence. However, current DGAL/SDSPV/2026-165 retains pre-2019 overseas implementation despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop; the actual local-order scope/compatibility remains unresolved. Current DAAF guidance invokes Order 043448 of 22 November 2004 for personal introductions/imports and the 1990 definitions. The actual local order is unrecovered. The newer risk-qualified statutory scope is a material counterargument, not a confirmed repeal or postal processing exemption.

**Document-rule sources:** [daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr 1](<https://daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr/quels-sont-les-vegetaux-soumis-a-un-contole-au-pec-lors-de-leur-introduction-a373.html>) · [daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr 2](<https://daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr/importation-des-vegetaux-et-produits-vegetaux-a362.html>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 3](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000000343648/>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 4](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102/telechargement>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000039297041/2026-07-22>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 8](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/2031/oj?locale=fr>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 9](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/FR/TXT/?uri=CELEX%3A02016R2031-20250105>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 11](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/supima/8b94ce04-a4a9-4f01-a12f-3fba8de8f85a>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 12](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 13](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-278/telechargement>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Martinique DAAF expressly cites the 22 November 2004 order prohibiting personal plant-product imports from all origins, using the 1990 definition of unprocessed or simply prepared material. The effect on factory-steamed/dried sencha is not explicitly resolved; the Réunion-specific dried-tea rule is not applied here. Current Code rural expressly adopts a risk-qualified manufactured-product definition. Its statutory 2020 commencement conflicts with current Ministry 2026-165 guidance retaining the older framework; neither claim is omitted. This does not establish a tea prohibition.

**Personal post:** The local rule concerns personal introductions/imports generally; no manufactured-tea postal exception established.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Current DAAF guidance invokes Order 043448 of 22 November 2004 for personal introductions/imports and the 1990 definitions. The actual local order is unrecovered. The newer risk-qualified statutory scope is a material counterargument, not a confirmed repeal or postal processing exemption. Current statute and ministry implementation conflict; a general published processing/implementation clarification suffices without a bespoke tea letter.

**Sources:** [daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr 1](<https://daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr/quels-sont-les-vegetaux-soumis-a-un-contole-au-pec-lors-de-leur-introduction-a373.html>) · [daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr 2](<https://daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr/importation-des-vegetaux-et-produits-vegetaux-a362.html>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 3](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000000343648/>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 4](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102/telechargement>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/loda/id/JORFTEXT000039297041/2026-07-22>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 8](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/2031/oj?locale=fr>) · [eur-lex.europa.eu 9](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/FR/TXT/?uri=CELEX%3A02016R2031-20250105>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 11](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/supima/8b94ce04-a4a9-4f01-a12f-3fba8de8f85a>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 12](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2025-102>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 13](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-278/telechargement>) · [ippc.int 14](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 15](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr 16](<https://daaf.martinique.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/PHYTOVOTRE2014-11-20141_cle85efe1.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed the DAAF controlled-goods FAQ expressly citing the 2004 personal-import ban, the 1990 product definition and the ministry’s 2025 transitional-law instruction. Searched current local processing exclusions without finding a finished-tea ruling. 2026-09-30 bounded independent French-territory peer review: Current DAAF guidance expressly describes the 22 November 2004 local restriction as applying to personal introductions/imports using the 1990 Article 1 product definition, not merely the national certificate schedule. National-list absence therefore does not settle the separate ban. The original local order was not recovered. 30 September fresh domestic-definition review: read currentL 271-7 and 2019-1110 original/currentArticle 6, ratification by 2023-171 Art 39, and official indexed 2026-165/2026-278 implementation statements. Verified 2025-102 abrogated, with 2026-165 current. New actualTeaCategory 1 evidence supports a general processing inference, but present statute/agency implementation and concrete local-rule scope conflict persists; not merely membership or a missing bespoke tea ruling. Source/territory findings in french-definition-review/peer-conclusions.json. 30 September historical official DAAF source check: The official newsletter describes the 2004 private-import restriction as prohibiting covered plants/products without an official origin sanitary certificate, including mainland France/EU. The current DAAF page describes a personal-import prohibition using the 1990 definitions. Neither gives an express finished manufactured tea exception. This adds historical administrative interpretation, not the unrecovered full 2004 order, and does not establish tea coverage or an unconditional prohibition.

<a id="mr"></a>
### Mauritania (MR)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The personal-consumption exemption in Order 1256/2002 Article 8 expressly covers post and waives the preceding plant import controls for up to 10 kg. Use only within that exemption; no permit or phytosanitary certificate is obtained. Keep the entire parcel below 10 kg to avoid relying on an unconfirmed net-weight interpretation.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mau159976.pdf>)

Order MDRE 1256 of 12 November 2002 article 8 exempts plant products up to 10 kg for personal consumption from the preceding import-control provisions. It expressly covers private baggage and postal consignments. Above the exemption, the annex separately lists tea leaves for consumption.

**Personal post:** An explicit personal-post exception applies within 10 kg; larger checkout quantities cannot rely on it.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 10 kg product weight. Plant products for personal consumption in baggage or post. Text states 10 kg of products, without a separate gross/net definition; keep shipment below threshold if interpretation is uncertain.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. Current checkout exceeds the cited personal/exemption route. Reduce the cap; a larger route requiring permits or certificates is unavailable to this store. The legal weight basis needs confirmation; the stated bag equivalent assumes net product weight.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions. The current maximum order is outside the reviewed threshold.

**To resolve:** The existing 24.9 kg net checkout maximum exceeds the exemption. A conservative ceiling is 82 retail pouches including the recorded pouch and outer packaging weights, with no other contents. Above the exemption is not supported under the store constraint.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mau159976.pdf>)

<a id="ms"></a>
### Montserrat (MS)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Full published plant-control and trade-licence schedules do not regulate this tea from Japan; the 2025 plant amendment concerns citrus only. The Public Health Act and published subsidiary regulations inspected do not impose a separate prior personal food import certificate for this product.

**Document-rule sources:** [gov.ms 1](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/9.02-Plant-Protection-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 2](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/SRO-No.-4-of-2025-Plant-Protection-Amendment-of-Schedules-Regulations.pdf>) · [gov.ms 3](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/15.15-External-Trade-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 4](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/14.01-Public-Health-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 5](<https://www.gov.ms/2025/09/15/montserrat-transitions-to-digital-import-licence-system/>) · [gov.ms 6](<https://www.gov.ms/government/legal-department/attorney-generals-chambers/sros/>)

The government legal collection republished in February 2026 applies plant import prohibitions/conditions only to commodities in the Plant Protection Regulations First and Second Schedules (regulations 2–3). Neither schedule lists manufactured Camellia tea; the broad agricultural-produce-in-bags restriction is limited to Cuba, Haiti, Santo Domingo and Venezuela, not Japan. SRO 4/2025 changes citrus entries only. The External Trade Import Restriction Order schedules likewise omit tea/0902.

**Personal post:** Schedule-based rules apply to imports generally, so this finding does not depend on a traveler or gift exemption. A sealed personal purchase of manufactured Japanese tea is outside the listed permit/certificate commodities. Ordinary postal customs declaration, duties and inspection still apply.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**To resolve:** None for the specified 100 g manufactured pure Japanese tea under the published schedules; recheck if contents or origin change.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/msr98141.pdf>) · [gov.ms 2](<https://www.gov.ms/2025/03/07/citrus-fruits-now-allowed-for-importation-to-montserrat/>) · [gov.ms 3](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/9.02-Plant-Protection-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 4](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/SRO-No.-4-of-2025-Plant-Protection-Amendment-of-Schedules-Regulations.pdf>) · [gov.ms 5](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/15.15-External-Trade-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 6](<https://www.gov.ms/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/14.01-Public-Health-Act.pdf>) · [gov.ms 7](<https://www.gov.ms/2025/09/15/montserrat-transitions-to-digital-import-licence-system/>) · [gov.ms 8](<https://www.gov.ms/government/legal-department/attorney-generals-chambers/sros/>)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

The NPPO expressly requires a plant import permit for every controlled consignment, personal or commercial. Green and black tea require prior NAPRO approval before the plant import permit; the exporting country's phytosanitary certificate is also specified.

**Personal post:** The personal-use application explicitly includes courier/post. Small quantity is not an automatic waiver.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.
