<a id="mv"></a>
### Maldives (MV)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported by the domestic section 36 living/unmanufactured/risk-qualified manufactured-product boundary applied to fully manufactured sencha, with ISPM 32 tea Category 1 as technical evidence and the current MFDA personal-food allowance. This is an interpretive conclusion from published general law, not an express Maldives tea-specific ruling or a personal exemption from controls on raw plant material.

**Document-rule sources:** [agriculture.gov.mv 1](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/announcements/important-notice-for-plant-importers>) · [agriculture.gov.mv 2](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/downloads>) · [health.gov.mv 3](<https://health.gov.mv/en/downloads/standard-for-importing-food-item-for-personal-use>) · [health.gov.mv 4](<https://health.gov.mv/en/publications/viyafaari-beynun-fiyavai-ehenihen-beynumah-dhivehiraaajeah-kaaboathakethi-inpoatkurumugai-amalukuranvee-gothuge-mingandu>) · [health.gov.mv 5](<https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/XqP12zoB/xafwwjjj.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.mv 6](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/mandate-1>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/maldives/reportingobligation/4>) · [assets.ippc.int 8](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2025/06/12/Plant_Protection_Act.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mdv88996.pdf>) · [wto.org 10](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s461_e.pdf>) · [aos.egov.mv 11](<https://aos.egov.mv>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 12](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/dv/legislations/201/consolidations/238>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 13](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/storage/uploads/6nL8gRnQ/dsemrcxg.pdf>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 14](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/dv/legislations/201/consolidations/238/pdf>) · [gov.si 15](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 16](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

The Attorney General’s current consolidated Plant Protection Act 12/2011, section 36, defines plants as living plants/parts (including seeds) and distinguishes unchanged/unmanufactured plant material, including grain, from manufactured products whose manner of manufacture leaves a risk of introducing or spreading pests. Sections 19 and 22 require certificates and permits for those defined controlled goods. Section 20(b) ties phytosanitary measures to applicable international standards or technical justification. Applied to the specified factory-made sencha (steamed, repeatedly rolled/pressed with hot air, dried and finished), these domestic category boundaries support a no-special-phytosanitary-document conclusion: it is a manufactured food, not living or merely raw dried plant material, and ISPM 32 section 2 Category 1 / Appendix 2 expressly identifies tea as an example of manufacture that removes plant-pest risk. This is a reasoned application of Maldivian statutory scope, not a claim that ISPM membership itself creates a waiver. The December 2025 and March 2026 ministry instructions govern covered plants/products and do not expressly expand section 36 to all finished food. Separately, MFDA-FCD STAN 6-2021 Rev.1 November 2025 permits personal processed food up to 5 kg/5 litres with intact identifiable packaging; its special advance postal form is for breast-milk substitutes, not tea. Section 37 of the 2011 Act expressly repeals Plant Quarantine Regulation 2008, so its old packed-food language is not relied upon as current authority.

**Personal post:** General commodity-scope interpretation applies equally to postal imports. Act section 25(b) expressly names post and courier services when handling covered plants/products/regulated articles; it does not make an otherwise outside-scope finished food a controlled commodity. This conclusion does not borrow a passenger allowance. The separate current MFDA standard governs the recipient’s noncommercial food use, including small parcels.

**Quantity conditions:** Tea-item cap: 50 × 100 g pouches (5 kg tea). Up to 5 kg product weight. Processed-food category collectively; allowance is not stated per tea variety. Category-wide aggregation in one consignment is the conservative application; the text does not explicitly define a parcel/time interval. MFDA-FCD STAN 6-2021 Rev.1 November 2025, section 4.1, printed page 4: personal processed foods must not exceed 5 kg NET WEIGHT (5 litres for liquids). The dry tea limit uses net grams.

**Checkout:** 50 pouches / 5 kg tea; highest carrier band 6.08 kg gross. Current maximum is within this numeric threshold; this does not settle permits, value limits, frequency, packaging or genuine personal use.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [agriculture.gov.mv 1](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/announcements/important-notice-for-plant-importers>) · [agriculture.gov.mv 2](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/downloads>) · [health.gov.mv 3](<https://health.gov.mv/en/downloads/standard-for-importing-food-item-for-personal-use>) · [health.gov.mv 4](<https://health.gov.mv/en/publications/viyafaari-beynun-fiyavai-ehenihen-beynumah-dhivehiraaajeah-kaaboathakethi-inpoatkurumugai-amalukuranvee-gothuge-mingandu>) · [health.gov.mv 5](<https://health.gov.mv/storage/uploads/XqP12zoB/xafwwjjj.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.mv 6](<https://agriculture.gov.mv/en/mandate-1>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/maldives/reportingobligation/4>) · [assets.ippc.int 8](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2025/06/12/Plant_Protection_Act.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 9](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/mdv88996.pdf>) · [wto.org 10](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s461_e.pdf>) · [aos.egov.mv 11](<https://aos.egov.mv>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 12](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/dv/legislations/201/consolidations/238>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 13](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/storage/uploads/6nL8gRnQ/dsemrcxg.pdf>) · [mvlaw.gov.mv 14](<https://mvlaw.gov.mv/dv/legislations/201/consolidations/238/pdf>) · [gov.si 15](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 16](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

<a id="mw"></a>
### Malawi (MW)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Not compatible with the no-special-registration constraint: tea is expressly scheduled and paragraph 8 mandates separate MBS importer registration on Form QDCO. Application to a private postal purchaser is an explicit inference from the unqualified every-importer wording, paragraph 4 applying regardless of end use, and the form making firm details optional. This finding does not claim that a phytosanitary certificate or pre-export conformity certificate is established for the parcel.

**Document-rule sources:** [portal.trade.gov.mw 1](<https://portal.trade.gov.mw/en-gb/search-measure/view/180>) · [portal.trade.gov.mw 2](<https://portal.trade.gov.mw/en-gb/site/display/59>) · [malawilii.org 3](<https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2018/19/eng%402018-08-24>) · [wto.org 4](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s460_e.pdf>) · [mbsmw.org 5](<https://mbsmw.org/files/Cess-gazette-2016.pdf>) · [mbsmw.org 6](<https://mbsmw.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/2024-Catalogue-of-Malawi-Standards-.pdf>) · [mbsmw.org 7](<https://mbsmw.org/?p=2214>) · [tradebarriers.org 8](<https://www.tradebarriers.org/ntm/regulations/view/2325>)

The trade portal maps retail green-tea HS 090210 to plant-import permission, but its explanation cites unmanufactured products and the old act. The 2018 Plant Protection Act uses a pest-risk definition for manufactured products. The portal's old tea-plant ban cannot safely be applied to finished sencha.

**Personal post:** Private-post application is inferred from the enacted rule covering every listed-product importer regardless of end use/purpose, with optional firm fields in the registration form. No small personal-post waiver was found in the five-page order. This is separate from commercial-importer IQMS guidance.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Official MBS Gazette Government Notice 21/2016, Malawi Bureau of Standards (Quality Development Cess) (Revocation) Order, paragraphs 3–4 apply to every importer of listed products regardless of end use or purpose. Paragraph 8 requires every such importer to furnish Form QDCO to the Director General for entry in the MBS register; the First Schedule item 91 expressly lists tea. The Second Schedule application is separate from a customs declaration and makes a firm stamp/designation optional. The cess payment itself is not treated as a prohibited document requirement.

**Evidence access:** The exact official GN 21/2016 PDF was downloaded and its operative paragraphs and schedules visually inspected. It bears the unusual title including Revocation but its body imposes the cess and registration and revokes the earlier order; it is not merely a repeal notice. The older catalogue/article URLs now return migrated-site content, so their indexed text is supporting context only.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Agricultural classification remains unresolved: Does the current permit measure apply to sealed steamed tea, and are personal postal quantities exempt? The independent food/conformity/registration requirement already defeats the no-special-document scenario.

**Sources:** [portal.trade.gov.mw 1](<https://portal.trade.gov.mw/en-gb/search-measure/view/180>) · [portal.trade.gov.mw 2](<https://portal.trade.gov.mw/en-gb/site/display/59>) · [malawilii.org 3](<https://malawilii.org/akn/mw/act/2018/19/eng%402018-08-24>) · [wto.org 4](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s460_e.pdf>) · [mail.portal.trade.gov.mw 5](<https://mail.portal.trade.gov.mw/en-gb/site/display/43>) · [mbsmw.org 6](<https://mbsmw.org/files/Cess-gazette-2016.pdf>) · [mbsmw.org 7](<https://mbsmw.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/2024-Catalogue-of-Malawi-Standards-.pdf>) · [mbsmw.org 8](<https://mbsmw.org/?p=2214>) · [tradebarriers.org 9](<https://www.tradebarriers.org/ntm/regulations/view/2325>)

**Research trail:** Compared the HS-specific portal measure with the 2018 law and historical tea entry; searched current implementing regulations. WTO 2024 review paragraph 3.90 confirms the old import/export regulations remain in use under the 2018 Act. The exact green retail tea HS 09021000 portal page still attaches a permit measure whose text expressly covers unmanufactured products; this conflict cannot establish that processed tea requires or is exempt from the permit. 30 September: checked the Control of Goods agriculture order and its personal-consumption exception (paragraph 5(2)(b)); tea is in the export schedule, not the inspected import schedule, and paragraph 9 preserves other laws. This does not settle Plant Protection Act treatment of manufactured tea. 30 September independent food/conformity review: Retrieved GN 21/2016 from the current public MBS website law-download link and visually read the Gazette body, tea item 91 and Form QDCO. Indexed 2024 MBS catalogue identifies green-tea MS 896:2021 as mandatory, but actual GN 23/2016 IQMS regulations were not recovered and are not the basis for this decision. WTO 2024 review corroborates the IQMS/cess context; a tax by itself was not treated as a certificate obstacle.

<a id="my"></a>
### Malaysia (MY)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [ezhs.customs.gov.my 1](<https://ezhs.customs.gov.my/public-prob-import>) · [lom.agc.gov.my 2](<https://lom.agc.gov.my/act-view.php?type=pua&no=P.U.%20(A)%20117/2023>) · [customs.gov.my 3](<https://www.customs.gov.my/en/pg/Customs%20Order/PU%28A%29%20117-%20Perintah%20Kastam%20%28Larangan%20Mengenai%20Import%29%202023.pdf>)

The tea-specific row in Customs (Prohibition of Imports) Order 2023, pp.124–125, subjects green tea leaves HS 0902101000 to MAQIS inspection/approval in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan; Sabah/Sarawak additionally require a Department of Agriculture import permit. The general processed-plant exclusion does not erase this specific tea row.

**Personal post:** Commodity import rule is not confined to commercial consignments or baggage; no personal-post tea exemption was located.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Confirm destination state and arrange MAQIS clearance; for Sabah/Sarawak obtain the permit and its phytosanitary conditions before posting.

<a id="mz"></a>
### Mozambique (MZ)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported for the stated sealed 100 g industrially steam-manufactured plain Camellia tea by Article 9(c), the domestic risk-qualified definitions and current-linked ISPM 32 tea Category 1 evidence. This explicit combined-source inference removes the controlled-goods permit/certificate route; it is not a bespoke Mozambique tea ruling or an approval inferred from an empty portal. Portuguese Article 16 refers to produto consignado (defined in Article 1(37) as controlled goods); Article 17(1) concerns controlled products/growing media and other products specified in Annex 1, so its 14-day inspection application is not extended to every nonregulated food. PAC Article 4 directly exempts postal parcels without a special application. Ordinary customs/arrival inspection and general food safety remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [geoportal.agricultura.gov.mz 1](<https://geoportal.agricultura.gov.mz/en/imp_req.html>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/mz_2009-5cons2019_pqreg_en.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/moz221794.pdf>) · [innoq.gov.mz 4](<https://www.innoq.gov.mz/?download_id=1435&sdm_process_download=1>) · [innoq.gov.mz 5](<https://www.innoq.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/BROCHURA-PERGUNTAS-MAIS-FREQUENTE-PAC-MAR%C3%87O-2024.pdf>) · [masa.gov.mz 6](<https://www.masa.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Decreto_5_2009_regulamento_fitosanitario.pdf>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 8](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

Combined-source legal interpretation: Decree 5/2009 Article 1(29), (30) and (38) distinguishes living plants and pest-capable controlled/manufactured products. Article 9(c) requires inspectors to ensure compliance with international phytosanitary standards. Current-linked ISPM 32 explicitly illustrates tea in Category 1; the stated industrially steamed/rolled/dried sencha is consequently inferred outside the pest-risk-qualified controlled category. Articles 12(1–2) require permits/certificates for controlled goods, including unlisted controlled goods; mere omission from Annex 1 is not the exemption. The Annex tea entry concerns rooted plants, propagation and seeds, not finished tea. PAC Diploma Ministerial 98/2023 Article 4 separately exempts postal parcels from conformity assessment.

**Personal post:** The plant classification is product-based and applies to personal post without borrowing a traveler allowance. Article 18 permits inspection at postal centres. The independent PAC Article 4 exception expressly covers postal parcels separately from personal gifts, so a purchased Japan Post parcel qualifies without a gift declaration. Ordinary customs and arrival inspection remain.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Diploma Ministerial 98/2023, published in the official Gazette on 14 July 2023, article 4 explicitly excludes postal parcels from the PAC conformity-assessment programme. It separately excludes personal gifts, commercial samples and products with FOB value up to USD 2,000. This instrument does not impose an application for its postal exception.

**Evidence access:** The enacted Gazette PDF was downloaded from FAOLEX. The official INNOQ document link confirms provenance but direct INNOQ attempts returned access/security pages; those pages are not treated as retrieved document content.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [geoportal.agricultura.gov.mz 1](<https://geoportal.agricultura.gov.mz/en/imp_req.html>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/mz_2009-5cons2019_pqreg_en.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/moz221794.pdf>) · [innoq.gov.mz 4](<https://www.innoq.gov.mz/?download_id=1435&sdm_process_download=1>) · [innoq.gov.mz 5](<https://www.innoq.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/BROCHURA-PERGUNTAS-MAIS-FREQUENTE-PAC-MAR%C3%87O-2024.pdf>) · [masa.gov.mz 6](<https://www.masa.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2018/01/Decreto_5_2009_regulamento_fitosanitario.pdf>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 8](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>)

<a id="ng"></a>
### Nigeria (NG)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Operational exclusion by application of broad enacted food law, independently of the unresolved agricultural classification: Food, Drugs and Related Products (Registration) Act Cap F 33 section 1(1) requires registration before importing processed food, with no personal-quantity qualification; section 13 includes processed or packaged drink ingredients. The 1996 Food Products (Registration) Regulations require original analysis and foreign official manufacture/free-sale certificates for registration. No existing product registration or applicable small-personal-post exemption is established for this store. Because the store will not undertake special registration or certification, the published route is incompatible with its constraint. Applying the unqualified import provision to a 100 g personal postal purchase is an inference, not a published sencha-specific ruling or a tea prohibition. These originals are registration documents, not proven mandatory enclosures in every parcel.

**Document-rule sources:** [nafdac.gov.ng 1](<https://www.nafdac.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/Files/Resources/Regulations/NAFDAC_Acts/FOOD-DRUGS-AND-RELATED-PRODUCTS-REGISTRATION-ACT-Cap.F.33.pdf>) · [nafdac.gov.ng 2](<https://www.nafdac.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/Files/Resources/Regulations/NAFDAC_Acts/NAFDAC-ACT-Cap-N.-1-LFN-2004.pdf>) · [wto.org 3](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s462_e.pdf>)

NAQS publishes a plant-import permit/certificate route, but the reviewed public documents do not classify fully steamed and dried retail tea. Historical tea restrictions refer to vegetative planting material and seeds, not proof of a finished-tea ban.

**Personal post:** The enabled small-packet postal service is not agricultural clearance; no personal postal tea exemption was established.

**Checkout:** 16 pouches / 1.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 2 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Separate agricultural question remains: does NAQS treat sealed steamed tea as regulated plant produce? An existing applicable product registration or published personal-import exemption would also be needed to revisit the food-registration exclusion.

**Sources:** [naqs.gov.ng 1](<https://naqs.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/2020/07/NAQS_SOP.pdf>) · [naqs.gov.ng 2](<https://naqs.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/Plant_Import_Guidelines.pdf>) · [naqs.gov.ng 3](<https://naqs.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/NAQS-Act-2018-1.pdf>) · [wto.org 4](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s462_e.pdf>) · [trade.gov.ng 5](<https://trade.gov.ng/trade/nafdac/Simulation.do>) · [nafdac.gov.ng 6](<https://www.nafdac.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/Files/Resources/Regulations/NAFDAC_Acts/FOOD-DRUGS-AND-RELATED-PRODUCTS-REGISTRATION-ACT-Cap.F.33.pdf>) · [nafdac.gov.ng 7](<https://www.nafdac.gov.ng/wp-content/uploads/Files/Resources/Regulations/NAFDAC_Acts/NAFDAC-ACT-Cap-N.-1-LFN-2004.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read NAQS import guidance and SOP section 6.1; compared historical tea planting-material rules and searched personal/processed-food exclusions. Rechecked current NAFDAC sources, the 2024 WTO review paragraphs 3.9 and 3.113–3.116, and downloaded the 2025 food-registration text. The latter is marked Closed for comments with blank commencement and making dates; it is not used as proof of enacted law. WTO mentions large personal quantities but specifies no small-parcel food waiver. Personal-use export limits are not an import exemption. 30 September: the public single-window NAFDAC simulation index was readable through the web index, but certificate/tariff child pages failed and the live host failed DNS resolution; no commodity result could be obtained. 30 September: downloaded and OCR-read the 2018 NAQS Act interpretation provisions (section 27) and border powers. The Act does not define manufactured tea or provide a personal-food waiver; the agency-establishment text cannot resolve the commodity classification. 30 September: independently reviewed enacted Food, Drugs and Related Products (Registration) Act Cap F 33 sections 1 and 13 and the 2 January 1996 Food Products (Registration) Regulations in the NAFDAC official compilation, PDF pages 55–56. Distinguished registration application certificates from consignment certificates. No small-personal-import exception was found; the 2025 draft and export-only personal allowances are not relied upon.

Read full enacted Resolution 73-2026 (19 February 2026), gazette pages 2046–2047, saved as sources/ni-gaceta 20260224.*. <a id="nr"></a>
### Nauru (NR)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

A phytosanitary certificate is required for postal plant material by section 16(1), even where the hermetically sealed manufactured-food permit exemption applies. The store cannot meet that separate certificate requirement.

**Document-rule sources:** [justice.gov.nr 1](<https://justice.gov.nr/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Agricultural-Quarantine-Act-1999-Service-22.pdf>)

Agricultural Quarantine Act 1999, section 16(1), requires a health certificate for all plants and plant material entering other than in passenger baggage. Section 2 defines plant material to include items manufactured wholly or partly from plants. For plants, the health certificate is a phytosanitary certificate. Regulation 19(3)(a) exempts hermetically sealed manufactured shelf-stable food from an import permit, but does not waive the Act’s separate postal certificate requirement.

**Personal post:** Section 16(1) explicitly covers imports other than passenger baggage, including postal tea sent to a private consumer.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [justice.gov.nr 1](<https://justice.gov.nr/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Agricultural-Quarantine-Act-1999-Service-22.pdf>)

<a id="om"></a>
### Oman (OM)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Official full-text recovery resolved the separate food-law gap: Food Safety Regulation 2/2010 Article 30 requires an application for an import licence before any food import and prescribes refusal/re-export for noncompliance. It has no business/resale or quantity limitation. Decision 170/2025 expressly preserves that licence provision while adding a discretionary pre-licence establishment check. Personal-use food is exempt from registration under Decision 69/2024 Article 3(1), but that waiver does not waive the separate advance licence. Conservative legal-scope inference excludes this personal posted tea from the store’s no-special-approval route, while agricultural pest-risk classification remains unverified.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [customs.gov.om 2](<https://www.customs.gov.om/en/business-services/permit/permit-to-import-plant-products>) · [customs.gov.om 3](<https://customs.gov.om/en/import-of-goods-and-personal-effects/>) · [micor.agriculture.gov.au 4](<https://micor.agriculture.gov.au/plants/oman_om/tea>) · [qanoon.om 5](<https://qanoon.om/p/2006/moaf20060032/>) · [customs.gov.om 6](<https://www.customs.gov.om/media/idwfzthg/tarfah.pdf>) · [oman.om 7](<https://oman.om/docs/default-source/default-document-library/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%AC%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D9%8A.pdf?sfvrsn=5312ff95_2>) · [qanoon.om 8](<https://qanoon.om/p/2024/mafw20240069/>) · [data.qanoon.om 9](<https://data.qanoon.om/ar/md/mafw/2024-0069.pdf>) · [qanoon.om 10](<https://qanoon.om/p/2008/rd2008084/>) · [qanoon.om 11](<https://qanoon.om/p/2010/mrmwr20100002/>) · [data.qanoon.om 12](<https://data.qanoon.om/ar/md/mrmwr/2010-0002.pdf>) · [qanoon.om 13](<https://qanoon.om/p/2025/mafw20250170/>) · [nsg.gov.om 14](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/royalDecrees>) · [nsg.gov.om 15](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/data/1bc7e394-9983-47c0-ac4e-a94673d0d197.pdf>) · [nsg.gov.om 16](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/data/MR_No_170-2025%20amending%20some%20provisions%20of%20the%20Food%20Safety%20Regulations.pdf>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. Agricultural processing classification remains unresolved. Independently, Food Safety Regulation 2/2010 Article 30 requires every person wishing to import food to obtain an import licence before import. The no-special-document route is excluded by a conservative inference from that unqualified provision; it is not a blanket prohibition on tea.

**Personal post:** Article 30 is not limited to food businesses, resale, traveller baggage or commercial quantities. Applying its every-person import duty to a purchased personal 100 g parcel is an explicit legal-scope inference. Decision 69/2024 Article 3(1) exempts personal food from product registration, not expressly from the separate Article 30 advance import licence.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No unresolved question prevents the conservative no-special-document exclusion based on the separate advance food-import licence. Agricultural classification of steamed/dried sencha under the pest-risk-qualified manufactured-product definition remains unresolved and is not claimed resolved by the food rule. An actual published personal-parcel waiver of Regulation 2/2010 Article 30 could change this conclusion.

**Sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [customs.gov.om 2](<https://www.customs.gov.om/en/business-services/permit/permit-to-import-plant-products>) · [customs.gov.om 3](<https://customs.gov.om/en/import-of-goods-and-personal-effects/>) · [micor.agriculture.gov.au 4](<https://micor.agriculture.gov.au/plants/oman_om/tea>) · [qanoon.om 5](<https://qanoon.om/p/2006/moaf20060032/>) · [customs.gov.om 6](<https://www.customs.gov.om/media/idwfzthg/tarfah.pdf>) · [oman.om 7](<https://oman.om/docs/default-source/default-document-library/%D9%82%D8%A7%D9%86%D9%88%D9%86-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AD%D8%AC%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B2%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%B9%D9%8A.pdf?sfvrsn=5312ff95_2>) · [qanoon.om 8](<https://qanoon.om/p/2024/mafw20240069/>) · [data.qanoon.om 9](<https://data.qanoon.om/ar/md/mafw/2024-0069.pdf>) · [qanoon.om 10](<https://qanoon.om/p/2008/rd2008084/>) · [qanoon.om 11](<https://qanoon.om/p/2010/mrmwr20100002/>) · [data.qanoon.om 12](<https://data.qanoon.om/ar/md/mrmwr/2010-0002.pdf>) · [qanoon.om 13](<https://qanoon.om/p/2025/mafw20250170/>) · [nsg.gov.om 14](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/royalDecrees>) · [nsg.gov.om 15](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/data/1bc7e394-9983-47c0-ac4e-a94673d0d197.pdf>) · [nsg.gov.om 16](<https://www.nsg.gov.om/data/MR_No_170-2025%20amending%20some%20provisions%20of%20the%20Food%20Safety%20Regulations.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read Oman Customs permit and personal import services. Cross-checked current Japan postal matrix against Australian MICOR exact processed-tea entry; retained the origin/date conflict. Rechecked exact Australian MICOR processed-tea consumption entry: phytosanitary certificate yes, import permit no, NPPO conditions verified 2013 and page reviewed September 2024. Japan MAFF retains PQ footnote 11. Oman public commodity requirements lookup is CAPTCHA-protected; no automated bypass attempted. Destination-specific manufactured-tea exemption remains unverified. 30 September final review: located Royal Decree 47/2004 agricultural-quarantine law and implementing Decision 32/2006. Older 1983/2000 rules were not treated as current. The current GCC-style definition retains a pest-risk qualification for manufactured products; searches did not locate a tea exemption. Checked Oman Customs current tariff: HS 090210000000 is green tea in immediate retail packaging, with 0% duty. Its blank procedural fields do not establish a phytosanitary exemption. The official commodity-condition lookup remains CAPTCHA-protected; no bypass attempted. Rechecked Australian MICOR’s 2013 NPPO verification/2024 page review and preserved its origin/date limitation. 30 September bounded independent-food follow-up: Read the reproduced enacted text of Decision 69/2024 Articles 2-3 and commencement; retrieved the linked Gazette scan (text encoding is damaged). Personal-use food is explicitly exempt from registration. Read Law 84/2008 Articles 1, 10-14 and the 170/2025 amendment adding Article 30 bis. Do not turn routine authority release in Law Article 12 into a requirement to obtain an origin certificate. The original 2/2010 regulation is a nine-page image PDF: direct download returned 403, web extraction had no text, and the public document viewer could not preview it. The 2025 provision is discretionary pre-licence establishment verification, not proof that this personal parcel requires that licence. Retain the separate agricultural commodity-classification gap. 30 September bounded independent processing/list peer review: reread complete reproduced Decision 32/2006 on Qanoon. Article 1 inherits the parent-law definitions; articles 10–11 require prior permit and certificate for covered consignments. Article 12 certificate concession expressly concerns accompanied traveller goods (10 kg or 20 units, whichever is less). Article 34 expressly covers post up to 20 kg and no more than two parcels per person per year, but waives fees only; it does not waive permits, certificates or classification. The annex concerns honeybee-product pests, not a complete commodity exemption list. No tea-processing rule was found in the text. The separate original food regulation 2/2010 PDF again returned 403 and remains unreadable in this pass. Retained unresolved; saved the reproduced text/HTML and access results in middle-east-definition-peer-review/. 30 September fresh online source recovery: found North Al Sharqiyah Governorate’s current official legislation directory and downloaded its complete nine-page 2/2010 Gazette scan after Qanoon PDF access repeatedly failed. Visually read all pages, including Article 30’s unqualified advance food-import licence on printed 50. Retrieved the same official directory’s 170/2025 amendment, which expressly references the surviving licence. Cross-checked the explicit personal-use registration exemption in 69/2024, keeping licence and registration separate. Saved original PDFs, provenance, selected renders and key-provision transcription under official-online-recovery-2026-09-30/asia/. Proposed no-doc exclusion on stated legal-scope inference; agricultural status remains not_verified.

<a id="pa"></a>
### Panama (PA)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Prior online APA personal-consumption approval/notification with ID and invoice is required even for the processed-plant exemption. This is a special regulatory approval beyond an ordinary postal declaration.

**Document-rule sources:** [sit.apa.gob.pa 1](<https://sit.apa.gob.pa/aupsa/requisitos/RES055_2016MuestraConsumoPersonalEventosDonacionesTODOS.pdf>) · [sit.apa.gob.pa 2](<https://sit.apa.gob.pa/ConsumoPersonal/login.aspx?AspxAutoDetectCookieSupport=1>) · [apa.gob.pa 3](<https://apa.gob.pa/la-apa-te-informa-sobre-los-alimentos-que-pueden-ingresar-losviajeros-a-traves-del-aeropuerto-internacional-de-tocumen/>)

Panama Resolution 055-2016 admits commercially manufactured, labelled dehydrated plant parts without plant-health certification. Personal mail requires an approved online personal-consumption notification, ID and invoice before entry. Maximum 5 kg per person or 10 kg per family; no more than three postal/courier notifications yearly.

**Personal post:** Article 6 explicitly covers private or public messaging/postal services, and applies the weight caps to both travelers and such shipments.

**Quantity conditions:** Tea-item cap: 50 × 100 g pouches (5 kg tea). Up to 5 kg product weight. All personal food in a consignment per person; 10000 g per family; no more than 3 postal/courier notifications per year.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. Current checkout also exceeds the cited quantity threshold. Reducing quantity does not remove the separate regulatory requirement; this route is excluded under the store constraint.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below. The current maximum order is outside the reviewed threshold.

**Sources:** [sit.apa.gob.pa 1](<https://sit.apa.gob.pa/aupsa/requisitos/RES055_2016MuestraConsumoPersonalEventosDonacionesTODOS.pdf>) · [sit.apa.gob.pa 2](<https://sit.apa.gob.pa/ConsumoPersonal/login.aspx?AspxAutoDetectCookieSupport=1>) · [apa.gob.pa 3](<https://apa.gob.pa/la-apa-te-informa-sobre-los-alimentos-que-pueden-ingresar-losviajeros-a-traves-del-aeropuerto-internacional-de-tocumen/>)

<a id="pe"></a>
### Peru (PE)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

No advance plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate requirement identified for the finished-tea route; comply with ordinary postal/customs declaration, any published personal quantity limit, and arrival inspection. SENASA classifies loose green tea in retail packs of 3 kg or less (0902.10.00.00 P 2) as phytosanitary risk category 2. Postal procedure 6.2.1 waives import permit and origin phytosanitary certificate, but requires inspection on arrival. A 100 g loose-leaf pouch is not the category-1 tea-filter/capsule exemption. Separately, SUNAT legal report 031-2016 confirms noncommercial personal industrialized food is not restricted by DIGESA food-registration rules and needs no sanitary registration.

**Document-rule sources:** [senasa.gob.pe 1](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2017/08/Listado-mercancias-agrarias-reguladas-09.12.2020.pdf>) · [senasa.gob.pe 2](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2014/11/49.pdf>) · [senasa.gob.pe 3](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2015/02/12.pdf>) · [sunat.gob.pe 4](<https://www.sunat.gob.pe/legislacion/oficiosAd/2016/informes/2016-INF-031-5D1000.pdf>)

SENASA classifies loose green tea in retail packs of 3 kg or less (0902.10.00.00 P 2) as phytosanitary risk category 2. Postal procedure 6.2.1 waives import permit and origin phytosanitary certificate, but requires inspection on arrival. A 100 g loose-leaf pouch is not the category-1 tea-filter/capsule exemption.

**Personal post:** The official procedure is expressly for postal consignments. The 3 kg threshold describes immediate packaging for tariff classification, not a total personal allowance.

**Checkout:** 211 pouches / 21.1 kg tea; highest carrier band 25.4 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** The July 2026 list landing page could not be retrieved; latest accessible detailed list and standing postal procedure agree on CRF 2 treatment.

**Sources:** [senasa.gob.pe 1](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2017/08/Listado-mercancias-agrarias-reguladas-09.12.2020.pdf>) · [senasa.gob.pe 2](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2014/11/49.pdf>) · [senasa.gob.pe 3](<https://www.senasa.gob.pe/senasa/descargasarchivos/2015/02/12.pdf>)

<a id="pf"></a>
### French Polynesia (PF)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Current postal guidance supplies a published general cooked-plant-food exemption. Actual steam-manufactured pure sencha reasonably falls within it; that is a stated inference, supported by the authority’s current ISPM 32 framework. No special permit or foreign phytosanitary certificate is required under this published route. Ordinary declaration and arrival clearance remain.

**Document-rule sources:** [service-public.pf 1](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/particuliers/apporter-ou-faire-venir-en-pf-particulier/jarrive-en-polynesie-francaise/>) · [service-public.pf 2](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/18-03-2026-nouvelle-reglementation-concernant/>) · [ippc.int 3](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 4](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [lexpol.cloud.pf 5](<https://lexpol.cloud.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&idr=0&np=1&lang=fr>) · [service-public.pf 6](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/professionnels-2/importer-en-polynesie-francaise-2/denrees-alimentaires/denrees-stockees-dorigine-vegetale-refonte/produits-transformes-dorigine-vegetale/>) · [lexpol.pf 7](<https://lexpol.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&idr=0&np=1&lang=fr>) · [service-public.pf 8](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/reglementation-dbs/>) · [lexpol.cloud.pf 9](<https://lexpol.cloud.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&lang=fr>)

DBS’s current private-import guidance, updated 5 August 2026 and citing Order 324 CM, expressly covers postal parcels. It waives both import permits and phytosanitary certificates for plant food that has undergone cooking, whether commercially/industrially prepared or otherwise. Camellia is absent from its listed prohibited cooked species. Applying that provision to the stated steam-manufactured sencha is an explicit processing inference. The authority’s reform notice confirms use of ISPM 32; the standard illustrates tea as Category 1. Full Annex 5 was not independently retrieved, so this finding rests on current DBS guidance, not the repealed 1996 order.

**Personal post:** The current DBS page expressly includes post. Declare the tea accurately, keep it commercially sealed and labelled, and allow arrival inspection. This assessment depends on actual steaming during manufacture; it does not cover merely dried raw leaves or herbal mixtures.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [service-public.pf 1](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/particuliers/apporter-ou-faire-venir-en-pf-particulier/jarrive-en-polynesie-francaise/>) · [service-public.pf 2](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/18-03-2026-nouvelle-reglementation-concernant/>) · [ippc.int 3](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 4](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [lexpol.cloud.pf 5](<https://lexpol.cloud.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&idr=0&np=1&lang=fr>) · [service-public.pf 6](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/professionnels-2/importer-en-polynesie-francaise-2/denrees-alimentaires/denrees-stockees-dorigine-vegetale-refonte/produits-transformes-dorigine-vegetale/>) · [lexpol.pf 7](<https://lexpol.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&idr=0&np=1&lang=fr>) · [service-public.pf 8](<https://www.service-public.pf/biosecurite/accueil/reglementation-dbs/>) · [lexpol.cloud.pf 9](<https://lexpol.cloud.pf/LexpolAfficheTexte.php?texte=1037079&lang=fr>)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

No current official rule establishes ordinary-post-only admission for this finished tea. The unresolved issue is the specific classification or current source identified below; this is not a finding that all tea is prohibited. The missing source is PNG Biosecurity Authority’s current commodity specification/exemption for finished Japanese Camellia sinensis tea in personal postal parcels, including whether a recipient import permit or origin phytosanitary certificate is required.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/oc/papua_new_guinea/index.html>) · [naqia.gov.pg 2](<http://www.naqia.gov.pg>) · [customs.gov.pg 3](<https://customs.gov.pg/trade/infogeneral>) · [pmnec.gov.pg 4](<https://www.pmnec.gov.pg/pm-marape-hails-83-0-passage-of-biosecurity-bill-2025-as-a-definingmoment-for-pngs-agricultural-future-sovereignty-and-trade/>) · [ippc.int 5](<https://www.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2017/08/11/PNG_Draft_Biosecurity_Bill.pdf>) · [customs.gov.pg 6](<https://www.customs.gov.pg/pdf/pages/prohibited_imports.pdf>) · [iccc.gov.pg 7](<https://iccc.gov.pg/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/Food-Sanitation-Act-1991.pdf>) · [customs.gov.pg 8](<https://customs.gov.pg/pdf/pages/prohibited_imports.pdf>) · [facilitation.nto.gov.pg 9](<https://facilitation.nto.gov.pg/pngba/>) · [facilitation.nto.gov.pg 10](<https://facilitation.nto.gov.pg/download/national-agriculture-quarantine-and-inspection-authority-act-1997/>)

PNG’s current official authorities apply agricultural biosecurity import controls, but no current official commodity condition for sealed manufactured Camellia sinensis tea in personal post was located. The government confirms the Biosecurity Bill’s passage in June 2025 and current NPPO sources use PNG Biosecurity Authority. An old IPPC draft and unrelated tea-tree mulch conditions cannot establish this product’s current requirements. Separately, Food Sanitation Act 1991 section 30 limits advance food-import notification to imports for sale or business purposes; section 31 inspection does not establish a mandatory foreign certificate for this private parcel.

**Personal post:** The evidence does not verify a personal-postal exemption or identify an applicable processed-tea permit/certificate condition.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Enacted Biosecurity Act 2025 definitions, entry-duty and transition provisions plus any preserved/current commodity specification or exemption for finished manufactured tea; no need for a tea-specific administrative letter if the general operative scope settles it.

**Research trail:** Checked the Japan MAFF NPPO directory and the linked PNG quarantine authority. Searched the authority, Customs and MICOR for Camellia sinensis, tea, processed foods and personal imports. Rejected tea-tree mulch guidance (Melaleuca alternifolia) as an unrelated product and did not use Customs tariff treatment as agricultural clearance. Checked the Prime Minister’s official June 2025 Biosecurity Bill passage announcement and current NPPO references; did not treat the 2017-uploaded draft as enacted law. Checked the postal prohibited/restricted list and PNG Customs references; food labelling provisions do not settle tea’s agricultural status. Rejected MPI guidance about small personal food quantities presented on an arrival card because it concerns passenger/animal-food imports rather than a mailed tea parcel. Repeated official tea/Camellia/processed-food searches did not locate current commodity conditions. 30 September independent food-law review: obtained the ICCC-hosted enacted Food Sanitation Act 1991. Section 30, printed p.17, limits advance import notification to food imported for sale or business purposes; section 31 provides examination of imported food. Sections 33–35 license prescribed businesses, not every private consumer. The current Customs food entry identifies labelling compliance under the 2007 Regulation, not a blanket private-parcel health/free-sale certificate. No independent mandatory special food document for this private sencha parcel was established, so the agricultural commodity gap remains unresolved. Saved primary Act as pacific-food-sources/pg-food 1991.*. 30 September bounded remaining-scope review: Retrieved the official National Trade Office PNGBA page and inspected its download links. It names the new authority but links the 1997 NAQIA Act; some displayed Act dates/titles are internally inconsistent. It supplies neither the enacted 2025 law nor a current Japanese tea specification. The 2017 draft was not substituted for enacted law. Read the current PG row and previous tariff/food/source access trail. Recovered the newly migrated official Parliament page and public JavaScript. Followed its documented wp-json/parliament-pg/v 1/get-bills endpoint and exact textSearch filter. Saved unfiltered and filtered official responses without authentication. Biosecurity/bio searches returned zero while a 2025 title search returned 39 records. Did not retry the already failed 509 tariff paths or apply business-food registration to a private parcel. 30 September additional primary review: New 2026 AIIB official project matrix confirms the Biosecurity Act 2025 and reports implementing regulations and related instruments still under preparation, with completion targeted for end 2026. It refers to disposal/destruction authority under the new Act and, transitionally, older animal/plant laws. This is institutional transition evidence, not the enacted import definition/tea requirement. Saved full AIIB matrix and text. Current NPPO-linked NAQIA HTTPS request fails because the certificate is expired; the government printer public home request returned 406. The previously recovered live Parliament search remains negative for biosecurity despite returning other certified 2025 acts; it was not re-queried. Targeted commencement/act-number search supplied no operative instrument.

<a id="pm"></a>
### St. Pierre and Miquelon (PM)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Current Code rural L 274-12/L 271-7 expressly imports a risk-qualified manufactured-plant-product definition, supporting a general-category argument for finished sencha. However, current Ministry instruction 2026-165 expressly includes PM while retaining pre-2019 rules pending a decree, despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop. The local 2009 order’s simple-preparation scope and current authorization/certificate provisions must be reconciled; no-document shipment remains unresolved. This is an operative-law conflict, not a rule requiring a bespoke tea classification. ISPM 32 tea Category 1 is technical evidence, not an independent domestic waiver.

**Document-rule sources:** [legifrance.gouv.fr 1](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/id/LEGISCTA000032693993>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 2](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000035419827>) · [saint-pierre-et-miquelon.gouv.fr 3](<https://www.saint-pierre-et-miquelon.gouv.fr/contenu/telechargement/812/4383/file/Recueil%20administratif%20%20-%20Septembre%202009.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 4](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/section_lc/LEGITEXT000006071367/LEGISCTA000032694023/2026-06-17>) · [saint-pierre-et-miquelon.developpement-durable.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.saint-pierre-et-miquelon.developpement-durable.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/2010_espececs-exotiques-envahissantes_frag_iles.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329664>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 8](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [agriculture.gouv.fr 9](<https://agriculture.gouv.fr/telecharger/111725>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [gov.si 11](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 12](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

Current Code rural L 274-12/L 271-7 expressly imports a risk-qualified manufactured-plant-product definition, supporting a general-category argument for finished sencha. However, current Ministry instruction 2026-165 expressly includes PM while retaining pre-2019 rules pending a decree, despite the statutory 14 April 2020 longstop. The local 2009 order’s simple-preparation scope and current authorization/certificate provisions must be reconciled; no-document shipment remains unresolved.

**Personal post:** The enacted 2009 local order expressly covers private and commercial postal/express shipments. The favorable current statutory definition is commodity-based, not a traveller concession. No Japan allowance follows from the Newfoundland-only exception.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Resolve the publicly documented statutory/administrative implementation conflict and the applicable general manufacturing boundary for the local 2009 order. A current published general processing clarification or operative implementation text would suffice; a tea-specific permit letter is not inherently required.

**Sources:** [legifrance.gouv.fr 1](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/id/LEGISCTA000032693993>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 2](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000035419827>) · [saint-pierre-et-miquelon.gouv.fr 3](<https://www.saint-pierre-et-miquelon.gouv.fr/contenu/telechargement/812/4383/file/Recueil%20administratif%20%20-%20Septembre%202009.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 4](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/section_lc/LEGITEXT000006071367/LEGISCTA000032694023/2026-06-17>) · [saint-pierre-et-miquelon.developpement-durable.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.saint-pierre-et-miquelon.developpement-durable.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/2010_espececs-exotiques-envahissantes_frag_iles.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 6](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329664>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 7](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000039329636>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 8](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/jorf/id/JORFTEXT000039297041>) · [agriculture.gouv.fr 9](<https://agriculture.gouv.fr/telecharger/111725>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-165/telechargement>) · [gov.si 11](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 12](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed current territory-specific Code rural provisions D 274-26 onwards and the prefectural import order; distinguished professional registration and Newfoundland concessions from the Japanese personal postal case. Second review confirmed current August 2026 Code rural text D 274-29 and D 274-30: certificate controls and the Newfoundland-only personal exception remain; no processed-sencha ruling found. 30 September bounded legal-scope peer review: Independently retrieved the complete current Code rural D 274-26 to D 274-31. D 274-29 imposes certification on covered plants/plant products generally rather than only an exhaustive commodity list. D 274-27/28 have the authorization/application route; D 274-26-1 registration is professional only and is not the basis for a private exclusion. D 274-30 exempts private imports from Newfoundland, which does not cover a direct Japan parcel. The local 2009 text reproduced in the official 2010 environment report defines products by no/simple processing and explicitly covers postal and express consignments. Thus there is a genuine processing-definition issue, not a missing tea line in an exhaustive schedule. 30 September independent definition review recovered the express L 274-12 -> L 271-7 -> EUArt 2(2) legal chain and full enacted Ordinance 2019-1110. Actual ISPM 32 tea Category 1 plus MAFF manufacture is favorable. Coordinated peer recovery of DGAL 2026-165 p 4 expressly naming PM reveals current administrative continuation of pre-2019 law against the statutory longstop; preserve the genuine implementation/local-definition conflict rather than require a bespoke ruling. Full notes: wf-pm-definition-review/review-notes.json.

### Pitcairn (PN)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [government.pn 1](<https://www.government.pn/laws>) · [government.pn 2](<https://www.government.pn/s/2024-03-03_Biosecurity_Ordinance_signed_and_dated_with_legal_report-fpka.pdf>)

Biosecurity Ordinance 2024 section 4(3) includes all plant products, and Schedule 1 expressly includes products manufactured wholly or partly from plants. Section 16(10) requires an import permit where no commodity specification applies. No published tea specification or exemption was located.

**Personal post:** The rule covers imported goods and manufactured plant products regardless of personal use; the permit application precedes departure from the origin.

**Checkout:** 82 pouches / 8.2 kg tea; highest carrier band 10 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Obtain the Chief Biosecurity Officer permit or the applicable published tea specification/exemption before dispatch.

**Sources:** [government.pn 1](<https://www.government.pn/laws>) · [government.pn 2](<https://www.government.pn/s/2024-03-03_Biosecurity_Ordinance_signed_and_dated_with_legal_report-fpka.pdf>)

<a id="pr"></a>
### Puerto Rico (PR)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Do not ship under the no-extra-regulatory-paperwork constraint: manufactured Camellia tea is agriculturally admissible without APHIS permit/certificate, but food purchased online and mailed by the store requires FDA prior notice before posting. FDA expressly excludes store-shipped purchases from its enforcement discretion for individual-to-individual gifts. Puerto Rico is included in prior-notice territory.

**Document-rule sources:** [aphis.usda.gov 1](<https://www.aphis.usda.gov/ace/generally-authorized-non-propagative-plant-products>) · [ecfr.gov 2](<https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-7/subtitle-B/chapter-III/part-319/subpart-D/section-319.7>) · [fda.gov 3](<https://www.fda.gov/industry/prior-notice-imported-foods/prior-notice-policy-sending-gifts-your-friends-and-family>) · [fda.gov 4](<https://www.fda.gov/media/161541/download>)

APHIS expressly authorizes dried Camellia sinensis tea without plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate; all consignments remain subject to arrival inspection. Current 7 CFR 319.7 expressly includes Puerto Rico and the US Virgin Islands within the United States for these import provisions.

**Personal post:** The general commodity authorization is not a baggage concession. The regulatory movement definition expressly includes mail. No tea-specific agricultural weight ceiling found.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [aphis.usda.gov 1](<https://www.aphis.usda.gov/ace/generally-authorized-non-propagative-plant-products>) · [ecfr.gov 2](<https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-7/subtitle-B/chapter-III/part-319/subpart-D/section-319.7>)

<a id="pw"></a>
### Palau (PW)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported under the manufactured-food exemption when the pouch is hermetically sealed and shelf-stable. No phytosanitary certificate is specified for stored dried food. The recipient may need routine biosecurity/customs arrival clearance; this is not a requirement to obtain an exporter certificate or advance import permit.

**Document-rule sources:** [palaugov.pw 1](<https://www.palaugov.pw/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/Plant-Animal-Quarantines-and-Regulation-with-Amendments.pdf>) · [bcbp.pw 2](<https://bcbp.pw/?page_id=166>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/pau155748.pdf>) · [bcbp.pw 4](<https://bcbp.pw/?page_id=3153>)

Quarantine Regulation 2.14(g)(i) exempts fully manufactured hermetically sealed shelf-stable food from an import permit; Part IV QP 8 imposes inspection of stored dried food without a phytosanitary-certificate requirement. Biosecurity Act 2014 (enacted 2016), section 84, preserves these regulations. Sections 20–22 retain arrival clearance and inspection; current Customs guidance says personal postal imports are processed by Customs. The actual retail pouch must be hermetically sealed.

**Personal post:** Product-based import exemption, not a passenger-only allowance.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** Verify the actual retail pouch is hermetically sealed and shelf-stable; a merely resealable bag does not establish this exemption.

**Sources:** [palaugov.pw 1](<https://www.palaugov.pw/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/Plant-Animal-Quarantines-and-Regulation-with-Amendments.pdf>) · [bcbp.pw 2](<https://bcbp.pw/?page_id=166>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/pau155748.pdf>) · [bcbp.pw 4](<https://bcbp.pw/?page_id=3153>)

### Paraguay (PY)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Do not ship under the volunteer-store policy: the published route requires a prior permit/authorization or special regulatory documents. Category 1 avoids category-2 AFIDI/phyto requirements but still requires prior import authorization through VUI under the published sanctions schedule.

**Document-rule sources:** [senave.gov.py 1](<https://www.senave.gov.py/documentos/resoluciones/6902467e5fc82a2f6c338fd335bf3ff9.pdf>) · [senave.gov.py 2](<https://www.senave.gov.py/documentos/resoluciones/25dc78257a43b42519e9b47871d61090.pdf>)

SENAVE explicitly distinguishes retail-packed tea for direct consumption (risk category 1) from bulk dried tea (category 2). Category 1 avoids category-2 AFIDI/phyto requirements but still requires prior import authorization through VUI under the published sanctions schedule.

**Personal post:** General import requirement is not limited to commercial resale; no personal postal exemption verified.

**Checkout:** 16 pouches / 1.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 2 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Confirm how an individual obtains category-1 VUI authorization for a small postal purchase; do not demand bulk-tea AFIDI by default.

<a id="qa"></a>
### Qatar (QA)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The specific official commodity table closes the former manufactured-tea agricultural-classification gap: 090210 green tea retail≤3 kg is subject only to food control, unlike products independently assigned agricultural control. The same authority publishes an explicit personal-food exemption from the food guide documentary requirements. No permit, origin certificate, special registration or advance approval is identified for the stated one-packet personal-use shipment. This combines two sources; the table is not itself a written exemption from the Agricultural Quarantine Law.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [almeezan.qa 2](<https://www.almeezan.qa/PrintArticle_section.aspx?LawTreeSectionID=12350&language=ar&lawId=3818>) · [emsfsa.moph.gov.qa 3](<https://emsfsa.moph.gov.qa/en/moph_documents/guides%20and%20instructions/food%20importers%20guide.pdf>) · [customs.gov.qa 4](<https://www.customs.gov.qa/Arabic/Procedures/Guide-to-Clearance-of-Imported-Goods/Pages/Chapter1.aspx>) · [almeezan.qa 5](<https://www.almeezan.qa/LawView.aspx?LawID=210&language=ar&opt=>) · [almeezan.qa 6](<https://www.almeezan.qa/LawArticles.aspx?LawArticleID=8416&LawId=210&language=ar>) · [customs.gov.qa 7](<https://customs.gov.qa/Arabic/Tariff/Pages/Restricted-Goods.aspx>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. Qatar Customs current published active restricted-goods schedule (14 January 2025) explicitly assigns retail green tea HS 090210000000 only to Ministry of Public Health food control (agency 5), with no agricultural-control agency 27 entry. All five HS 0902 tea entries have the same allocation; the complete schedule separately assigns both agencies to products where both controls apply. Combined with Customs published GCC food-guide footnote 3 exempting personal-use food from its document requirements, this supports sealed 100 g ordinary green tea for personal consumption with adequate packaging and origin-establishment identification, ordinary customs declaration and inspection. This is an inference from the published commodity allocation plus an express personal-food exemption.

**Personal post:** The customs commodity schedule is an import control allocation, not a traveller concession. Food-guide footnote 3 refers to food imported for personal use without an accompanied-baggage restriction; applying it to one sealed 100 g personal parcel is a scope inference. Ordinary declaration and possible recipient inspection/clearance remain applicable.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [maff.go.jp 1](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>) · [almeezan.qa 2](<https://www.almeezan.qa/PrintArticle_section.aspx?LawTreeSectionID=12350&language=ar&lawId=3818>) · [emsfsa.moph.gov.qa 3](<https://emsfsa.moph.gov.qa/en/moph_documents/guides%20and%20instructions/food%20importers%20guide.pdf>) · [customs.gov.qa 4](<https://www.customs.gov.qa/Arabic/Procedures/Guide-to-Clearance-of-Imported-Goods/Pages/Chapter1.aspx>) · [almeezan.qa 5](<https://www.almeezan.qa/LawView.aspx?LawID=210&language=ar&opt=>) · [almeezan.qa 6](<https://www.almeezan.qa/LawArticles.aspx?LawArticleID=8416&LawId=210&language=ar>) · [customs.gov.qa 7](<https://customs.gov.qa/Arabic/Tariff/Pages/Restricted-Goods.aspx>)

<a id="re"></a>
### Reunion (RE)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The still-published national 1990 Annex VI B(4) expressly requires an official finding that the production country of dried tea is free from Exobasidium vexans. Japan is listed by EPPO as present. The 2025 local order cites that national order, preserves other existing provisions in article 5, and article 11 repeals only the 2011 local order. Its fresh-leaf-only documentary schedule is therefore insufficient evidence that the national dried-tea condition vanished. Without a verified overriding exception, the no-certificate route is not supported.

**Document-rule sources:** [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 1](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/conditions-requises-pour-importer-des-vegetaux-a-la-reunion-a-compter-du-14-a3881.html>) · [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 2](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/ap_salim_2025_8_d_avec_annexe.pdf>) · [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 3](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/annexev.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 4](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/section_lc/JORFTEXT000000343648/LEGISCTA000021087043/>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000021240384/2026-05-04>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 6](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?menuid=28&downloadid=2542&reporeid=140>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?menuid=28&downloadid=1240&reporeid=140>) · [gd.eppo.int 8](<https://gd.eppo.int/taxon/EXOBVE/distribution/JP>)

The 2025 local import order's annex controls tea under HS 0902 only as fresh leaves. However, the still-published national 1990 overseas order has a Réunion-specific dried-tea condition involving origin and Exobasidium vexans. The local repeal provision does not clearly repeal that national condition.

**Personal post:** Fresh-plant postal prohibitions alone cannot decide dried tea; the relationship between the two legal instruments must be resolved.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Is there an authoritative current exemption specifically displacing the national 1990 dried-tea country-freedom condition for manufactured Japanese tea? No such exemption was found; retain exclusion under the no-special-document operating policy.

**Sources:** [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 1](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/conditions-requises-pour-importer-des-vegetaux-a-la-reunion-a-compter-du-14-a3881.html>) · [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 2](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/ap_salim_2025_8_d_avec_annexe.pdf>) · [daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr 3](<https://daaf.reunion.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/annexev.pdf>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 4](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/section_lc/JORFTEXT000000343648/LEGISCTA000021087043/>) · [legifrance.gouv.fr 5](<https://www.legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000021240384/2026-05-04>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 6](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?menuid=28&downloadid=2542&reporeid=140>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?menuid=28&downloadid=1240&reporeid=140>) · [gd.eppo.int 8](<https://gd.eppo.int/taxon/EXOBVE/distribution/JP>)

**Research trail:** Read 2025 order, article 11 repeal and Annex V, December amendment, and national Annex VI; retained the unresolved legal overlap rather than assuming repeal. Read German NPPO full translations of both orders: 2025 article 5 preserves other existing provisions, article 9 substitutes compliance for an import authorization but permits individual derogations only on application, and article 11 repeals only the 2011 local instrument. Verified exact national dried-tea row in Annex VI B(4), printed p. 67, and the EPPO Japan pest-distribution page. Preserved the separate agricultural-status uncertainty while recording a no-special-document exclusion.

<a id="rs"></a>
### Serbia (RS)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The January 2026 customs plant-health control list expressly includes retail green tea HS 090210. Plant Health Law Article 72 obliges importers/customs agents to notify the phytosanitary inspector and submit an inspection request before customs release. Even if this product can be inspected without a Japanese phytosanitary certificate under Article 67(4), the mandatory separate plant-health application conflicts with the store’s ordinary-customs-only constraint. The 2011 small-quantity exception does not cover this Japanese postal product.

**Document-rule sources:** [carina.rs 1](<https://www.carina.rs/upload/media/2025/12/31/66788/%D0%A4%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%BE-%D0%B1%D0%B8%D1%99%D0%B5_01_01_2026.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=1052&menuid=28&reporeid=126>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/srb161040.pdf>)

Serbian Customs’ plant-health control list effective 1 January 2026 expressly includes HS 090210 and other green/black tea codes, including retail packs. The published small-quantity plant exemption is for natural persons entering Serbia with specified European-origin material; it does not establish an exemption for Japanese tea sent by post.

**Personal post:** The current customs list establishes phytosanitary control, but does not by itself resolve whether this personal postal pouch needs a Japanese phytosanitary certificate or only arrival inspection.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** The exact Japanese phytosanitary certificate requirement remains unresolved, but a separate inspection application and phytosanitary release are mandatory, so the route does not fit the store’s no-special-paperwork policy.

**Sources:** [carina.rs 1](<https://www.carina.rs/upload/media/2025/12/31/66788/%D0%A4%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%BE-%D0%B1%D0%B8%D1%99%D0%B5_01_01_2026.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=1052&menuid=28&reporeid=126>) · [faolex.fao.org 3](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/srb161040.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read the current Customs plant-health commodity list and confirmed ordinary tea HS 0902 is included. Read Plant Health Law Articles 70–71 and the complete small-quantity implementing rule: it concerns entry by natural persons, European-origin products and specified quantities, without a finished-tea postal exception. Searched for the current certificate schedule applicable to manufactured tea; did not verify whether the certificate or inspection-only route applies. Read Plant Health Law Article 72 in the consolidation amended through 2019: obligatory notification, application for inspection and phytosanitary release for Article 67 consignments. This resolves operating-policy exclusion independently of the unanswered certificate-versus-inspection classification.

<a id="sa"></a>
### Saudi Arabia (SA)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

SFDA expressly instructs private food importers without a commercial registration to register using a civil ID/residence number, register the food items, complete examination procedures and submit Form 105. This is an additional regulatory registration/clearance pathway, independently incompatible with the stated ordinary-post-only operation. The separate agricultural processed-tea exception remains unverified; do not claim that the FAQ itself establishes a phytosanitary-certificate requirement.

**Document-rule sources:** [sfda.gov.sa 1](<https://www.sfda.gov.sa/ar/faq/60173>) · [sfda.gov.sa 2](<https://www.sfda.gov.sa/sites/default/files/2021-12/Q105_Ver%202.doc>) · [sfda.gov.sa 3](<https://www.sfda.gov.sa/sites/default/files/2026-08/ConditionsRequirementsFoodClearance_0.pdf>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **PQ*11**. SFDA April 2025 clearance conditions expressly provide for personal-use food, requiring no local-market display and importer responsibility for safety. Those conditions do not expressly waive agricultural quarantine.

**Personal post:** MAFF concerns postal Japanese manufactured tea. SFDA personal-food clearance is a non-commercial shipment pathway, not solely a traveller allowance.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

MICOR gives certificate yes/permit no for Australian tea, with a 2015 NPPO verification date; do not transfer that permit exception to Japan post. Confirm SFDA accepts the actual quantity as personal.

<a id="sb"></a>
### Solomon Islands (SB)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Supported within the published personal dried-plant route: at most 1 kg per product type, fully dried and non-propagable, botanically labelled and free of seeds, insects, soil and other quarantine material. The authority lists arrival inspection and release upon satisfying these conditions, without requiring a permit or phytosanitary certificate for that personal route. This is an inference from the complete specific conditions, not a general exemption for all plant products.

**Document-rule sources:** [biosecurity.gov.sb 1](<https://www.biosecurity.gov.sb/Importers/faq-importers-and-arrivals>) · [biosecurity.gov.sb 2](<https://www.biosecurity.gov.sb/Portals/93/Content/Documents/Resources/Biosecurity%20Regulations%202015.pdf>)

Biosecurity Solomon Islands allows personal consignments of thoroughly dried, non-propagable plant parts for consumption up to 1 kg per product type. Botanical labelling and freedom from seeds, live insects, soil and quarantine-risk material are required; arrival inspection applies.

**Personal post:** The same FAQ explicitly applies biosecurity rules to internet/mail-order purchases and international mail/courier parcels. Use the published 1 kg dried-plant route rather than assume that steaming qualifies for a separate cooked-food exception.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 1 kg product weight. Dried plant product, per product type. Personal dried-plant products, per product type; larger shipments require commercial conditions.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. Current checkout exceeds the cited personal/exemption route. Reduce the cap; a larger route requiring permits or certificates is unavailable to this store.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions. The current maximum order is outside the reviewed threshold.

**Sources:** [biosecurity.gov.sb 1](<https://www.biosecurity.gov.sb/Importers/faq-importers-and-arrivals>) · [biosecurity.gov.sb 2](<https://www.biosecurity.gov.sb/Portals/93/Content/Documents/Resources/Biosecurity%20Regulations%202015.pdf>)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The Biosecurity Act includes goods manufactured wholly or partly from plants and requires an import permit unless an applicable exemption has been granted. **Document-rule sources:** [mofbe.gov.sc 1](<https://mofbe.gov.sc/agriculture-department/border-control-and-support-division/>) · [tradeportal.sc 2](<https://www.tradeportal.sc/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/Animal-and-Plant-Biosecurity-Act-2014.pdf>) · [tradeportal.sc 3](<https://www.tradeportal.sc/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/SI-80-Animal-and-Plant-Biosecurity-Biosecurity-Import-Permit.pdf>)

The 2014 Animal and Plant Biosecurity Act broadly includes products manufactured wholly or partly from plants. Section 68 requires a permit before export unless an applicable exemption is granted. The ministry's import process also covers post and specifies origin phytosanitary documentation.

**Personal post:** No automatic personal-use or retail-tea exemption found; a written exemption or issued permit is needed.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.
