<a id="sl"></a>
### Sierra Leone (SL)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

The private food-law review does not establish a separate special-document duty. For agriculture, Cap 185 section 2 expressly includes dead severed leaves and applies to subsidiary rules unless context requires otherwise. The 1976 Notice cooking exception is limited to that Notice; without the complete 1974 Rules and Rule 9 context its effect on an independent permit duty remains unsettled. This is a specific legal-scope gap, not a requirement for a tea-specific exemption or personal agency ruling.

**Document-rule sources:** [archive.gazettes.africa 1](<https://archive.gazettes.africa/archive/sl/1976/sl-government-gazette-dated-1976-02-19-no-14.pdf>) · [wto.org 2](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s470_e.pdf>) · [archive.gazettes.africa 3](<https://archive.gazettes.africa/archive/sl/1976/sl-government-gazette-dated-1976-02-12-no-13.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sie216530.pdf>) · [parliament.gov.sl 5](<https://www.parliament.gov.sl/uploads/bill_files/The%20Food%20and%20Feed%20Safety%20Authority%20Act%2C%202017.pdf>) · [slsb.gov.sl 6](<https://slsb.gov.sl/inspectorate-overview/>) · [slsb.gov.sl 7](<https://slsb.gov.sl/product-certification/>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sie216504.pdf>) · [publications.lawreform.gov.sl 9](<https://publications.lawreform.gov.sl/download_file/929dedee-5526-48c7-8e0e-f13f1a850535/349>)

The 1976 Plant Phytosanitary (Import Restriction) Notice expressly extends vegetative material, seeds and fruit to dried, frozen and chilled goods, but excludes goods described as cooked, or pickled and imported in bottles or cans. The actual printed item 64 contains the awkward wording "and mentioned herein" and requires certificates; it must not be silently rewritten as "not mentioned". The tea entry names vegetative material and seeds, not finished retail sencha. For this steamed product, whether the cooking exception applies and how the container qualification operates remain unresolved. The 2025 WTO review confirms the 1974 rules as amended in 1976 remain the operative starting point. The official historical Cap 185 section 2 applies its broad definition of plant, including dead severed leaves, to rules made under it unless context otherwise requires. The 1976 exception is expressly for purposes of that Notice, issued under Rule 9; the full 1974 parent Rules were not recovered to establish their exceptions or its effect on an independent parent duty.

**Personal post:** The retrieved business-registration/for-sale food-import provisions were not extended to the private end consumer. Final enacted operative wording and a personal-post implementation rule were not recovered.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** The publicly retrievable Food and Feed Safety Act 2017 text labels itself a Bill, with unfilled enactment metadata. Its section 30 importer registration is expressly for import/export businesses, and section 33 food-import requirements are framed around imports for sale within Sierra Leone. The text therefore does not establish a special certificate duty for this private purchaser. WTO 2025 reports adoption and later enacted legislation references the food-safety Act, so an unavailable final copy must not be described as proof that no law was enacted. Current SLSB material describes arrival inspection/testing, not a verified separate pre-import certificate duty for private tea parcels.

**Evidence access:** The downloadable FAO/Parliament 2017 text is explicitly a Bill, not a verified final promulgated version. WTO 2025 and the 2021 enacted cross-reference demonstrate that food-safety legislation exists, but do not establish an unqualified private-import document duty in the unavailable final text.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Recover the complete operative P.N.66/1974, especially definitions, Rule 2 exceptions and Rule 9 scope, or an authoritative general rule that determines how the 1976 cooked-food exclusion affects the parent import duty. Then apply the cooking clause to industrial steaming and its container qualification. No bespoke tea ruling is required if those generic rules decide the question.

**Sources:** [archive.gazettes.africa 1](<https://archive.gazettes.africa/archive/sl/1976/sl-government-gazette-dated-1976-02-19-no-14.pdf>) · [wto.org 2](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s470_e.pdf>) · [archive.gazettes.africa 3](<https://archive.gazettes.africa/archive/sl/1976/sl-government-gazette-dated-1976-02-12-no-13.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sie216530.pdf>) · [parliament.gov.sl 5](<https://www.parliament.gov.sl/uploads/bill_files/The%20Food%20and%20Feed%20Safety%20Authority%20Act%2C%202017.pdf>) · [slsb.gov.sl 6](<https://slsb.gov.sl/inspectorate-overview/>) · [slsb.gov.sl 7](<https://slsb.gov.sl/product-certification/>) · [faolex.fao.org 8](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sie216504.pdf>) · [publications.lawreform.gov.sl 9](<https://publications.lawreform.gov.sl/download_file/929dedee-5526-48c7-8e0e-f13f1a850535/349>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed the gazetted tea entry and searched newer plant-quarantine legislation and processed-tea/personal-post guidance. 30 September: read WTO 2025 report paragraphs 3.88–3.94, distinguishing current rules from a proposed replacement. Retrieved indexed primary notice text including its dried-material/cooked-bottled-or-canned distinction and catch-all item 64; the original PDF download was unavailable (403), so ambiguous OCR of item 64 was not used as a conclusive new rule. The full 1974 rules and an authoritative manufactured-tea interpretation were not retrieved. Downloaded the 19 February 1976 Gazette directly and visually verified printed pages 252 and 254 (PDF pages 12 and 14), after browser retrieval failures. Item 64 literally prints "and mentioned herein"; the unusual wording is not an OCR error. Verified the cooking/pickling/container exception in the introductory paragraph. Because sencha is steamed before drying, did not collapse the analysis to the explicit dried category or assume that every cooked product must be bottled. Rechecked 2025 WTO review sections 3.88–3.94 for current legal context. 30 September independent food/conformity review: Read the complete public Food and Feed Safety bill, separating section 30 business registration and section 33 imports for domestic sale from a private postal order. Checked current SLSB inspection/product-certification descriptions, WTO 2025 implementation context and the later enacted Breast-Milk Substitutes Act reference to food-safety legislation. Did not treat the bill’s blank adoption metadata as proof that the food law never took effect, or extend commercial product-mark certification to every private shipment. 30 September common-definition review: The 1976 Notice has a real cooked-food exception, and a reading cooked OR (pickled and bottled/canned) is linguistically possible. The exception is expressly for purposes of this Notice issued under Rule 9 of the parent 1974 Rules. The full parent Rules were not recovered in this bounded exact search; the Notice alone cannot establish absence of an independent parent-rule permit duty. A secondary review quotes a broad parent Rule 2, but was used only as a retrieval lead, not as the legal conclusion. No operative ISPM definition bridge was recovered. 30 September independent food/conformity review: Read the complete public Food and Feed Safety bill, separating section 30 business registration and section 33 imports for domestic sale from a private postal order. Checked current SLSB inspection/product-certification descriptions, WTO 2025 implementation context and the later enacted Breast-Milk Substitutes Act reference to food-safety legislation. Did not treat the bill’s blank adoption metadata as proof that the food law never took effect, or extend commercial product-mark certification to every private shipment. 30 September 2026 parent-rule recovery: recovered the complete 7-page official Law Reform Commission Cap 185 compilation and read sections 2 and 3. Verified the Gazette 1974 index has 0 documents, the official Commission 1974 Instruments folder is empty, global phytosanitary search has no file, SierraLII all-legislation inventory has no 1974 year, and the actual IPPC legislation category has no reporting obligations. Exact indexed FAOLEX/title/number searches did not recoverPN 66. The RSPO partialRule 2 quotation remained a retrieval lead, not a verified full parent rule. New evidence supports a real parent-duty scope gap beyond the Notice container grammar; it does not establish a prohibition. 30 September additional current Gazettes.Africa primary-archive index review: the available Sierra Leone years include 1972 and 1976 but not 1974. The authenticated parent Cap 185 Agricultural Ordinance has enabling rule-making powers and broad living/dead/manufactured definitions, but does not itself impose the missing 1974 import-document duty. No absent archive result is treated as a waiver or proof of duty. New index evidence saved in sl-parent-gazette-recovery/.

<a id="sm"></a>
### San Marino (SM)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Legal inference: San Marino applies Union phytosanitary legislation under its customs/cooperation agreement and Decision 1/2010. Under that regime, manufactured dried tea is exempt from phytosanitary certification; the EU fresh-leaf control does not apply to steamed/dried sencha. This is a personal-consumption route; comply with the stated packaging and quantity conditions and ordinary postal/customs declarations.

**Document-rule sources:** [eur-lex.europa.eu 1](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec/2010/1%284%29/oj/eng>) · [food.ec.europa.eu 2](<https://food.ec.europa.eu/horizontal-topics/international-affairs/eu-entry-conditions/sanitary-and-phytosanitary-agreements_en>) · [jordbruksverket.se 3](<https://jordbruksverket.se/languages/english/swedish-board-of-agriculture/plants/bringing-in-or-ordering-plants-and-plant-products-to-sweden-as-a-private-individual>)

Legal inference: San Marino applies Union phytosanitary legislation under its customs/cooperation agreement and Decision 1/2010. Under that regime, manufactured dried tea is exempt from phytosanitary certification; the EU fresh-leaf control does not apply to steamed/dried sencha.

**Personal post:** Decision 1/2010 concerns import legislation generally. The EU private-order guidance expressly covers goods ordered from abroad, rather than only carried in luggage.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Sources:** [eur-lex.europa.eu 1](<https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/dec/2010/1%284%29/oj/eng>) · [food.ec.europa.eu 2](<https://food.ec.europa.eu/horizontal-topics/international-affairs/eu-entry-conditions/sanitary-and-phytosanitary-agreements_en>) · [jordbruksverket.se 3](<https://jordbruksverket.se/languages/english/swedish-board-of-agriculture/plants/bringing-in-or-ordering-plants-and-plant-products-to-sweden-as-a-private-individual>)

<a id="sn"></a>
### Senegal (SN)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Treat this paid store order as a commercial tea import under Annex I D 48 and do not dispatch without the required phytosanitary certificate. Application to a personal-consumption purchase is explicitly an inference from the law; no verified automatic exemption supports the store’s no-documents route.

**Document-rule sources:** [sante.gouv.sn 1](<https://www.sante.gouv.sn/sites/default/files/5.%20f917f_sn3-1960-121einfuhr.pdf>) · [senegalcommerce.sec.gouv.sn 2](<https://senegalcommerce.sec.gouv.sn/ProceduresImportDetails.aspx>)

Decree 60-121, retained in the government’s June 2026 import procedure, requires phytosanitary certification for Class D goods. Annex I D 48 expressly lists commercial tea imports. Treating a paid store shipment as commercial even when the recipient consumes it is a reasoned application of that wording, not a published ruling specifically on a 100 g private parcel.

**Personal post:** No automatic small-postal or private-consumption exemption was found. The optional tea inspection in Table III concerns exports under article 3 and does not waive the import certificate.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** An applicable automatic exemption for paid personal-use postal tea would be needed to change this operational decision; the store will not obtain the certificate.

**Sources:** [sante.gouv.sn 1](<https://www.sante.gouv.sn/sites/default/files/5.%20f917f_sn3-1960-121einfuhr.pdf>) · [senegalcommerce.sec.gouv.sn 2](<https://senegalcommerce.sec.gouv.sn/ProceduresImportDetails.aspx>)

<a id="sr"></a>
### Suriname (SR)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Legal-scope inference: domestic article 1(2) incorporates the applicable IPPC/SPS term meanings and associated standards/guidelines; the current-linked ISPM 32 tea example resolves the manufactured-product risk threshold in article 1(1)(bb) for the stated processed retail sencha. No phytosanitary permit/certificate route under articles 5–8 is needed for a product outside that scope. This is not reliance on IPPC membership alone, nor a claim that the reference-only Appendix itself overrides a specific national restriction.

**Document-rule sources:** [dna.sr 1](<https://www.dna.sr/media/dc5fihzb/sb_2020___78.pdf>) · [gov.sr 2](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Procedure-voor-de-invoer-van-planten-en-plantaardige-producten.jpg>) · [gov.sr 3](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/LVV-IDB-Nieuwsbrief-uitgave-02.pdf>) · [dna.sr 4](<https://www.dna.sr/wetgeving/ontwerpwetten-bij-dna/in-behandeling/ontwerpwet-levensmiddelenwet-2026/>) · [gov.sr 5](<https://gov.sr/ministeries/ministerie-van-economische-zaken-ondernemerschap-technologische-innovatie/invoer-uitvoer-en-deviezen-controle-iud/>) · [faolex.fao.org 6](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sur129510.pdf>) · [gov.sr 7](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/staatsbesluit-van-2-februari-2006-no-20-houdende-wijziging-van-het-besluit-negatieve-lijst-2003-sb-2003-no-74.pdf>) · [ippc.int 8](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 9](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [dna.sr 10](<https://www.dna.sr/media/k4hdogeh/sb-2020-78.pdf>)

Supported by a combined statutory and commodity-classification inference for sealed manufactured plain Camellia tea. Plantenbeschermingswet 2020 article 1(1)(bb) includes manufactured products only when their nature/processing creates pest risk, and article 1(2) expressly gives IPPC/SPS term meanings and associated standards/guidelines priority. Current-linked ISPM 32 section 2 treats Category 1 commodities as requiring no phytosanitary measures; Appendix 2 expressly illustrates tea in Category 1. The Appendix is reference-only, not a freestanding binding exemption, but supplies the missing commodity evidence when applying the domestic definition. Articles 5–8/14 and the August 2026 notice control covered plants/products; neither specifically reclassifies manufactured tea. The separate trade Negative List omits tea and no enacted private-food certificate requirement was established.

**Personal post:** The interpretation concerns the product’s exclusion from regulated plant-product scope and is not limited to passenger baggage or gifts. It therefore applies to the stated 100 g paid private postal purchase, subject to ordinary customs declarations, inspection and clean manufactured retail packaging.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [dna.sr 1](<https://www.dna.sr/media/dc5fihzb/sb_2020___78.pdf>) · [gov.sr 2](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Procedure-voor-de-invoer-van-planten-en-plantaardige-producten.jpg>) · [gov.sr 3](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/LVV-IDB-Nieuwsbrief-uitgave-02.pdf>) · [dna.sr 4](<https://www.dna.sr/wetgeving/ontwerpwetten-bij-dna/in-behandeling/ontwerpwet-levensmiddelenwet-2026/>) · [gov.sr 5](<https://gov.sr/ministeries/ministerie-van-economische-zaken-ondernemerschap-technologische-innovatie/invoer-uitvoer-en-deviezen-controle-iud/>) · [faolex.fao.org 6](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sur129510.pdf>) · [gov.sr 7](<https://gov.sr/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/staatsbesluit-van-2-februari-2006-no-20-houdende-wijziging-van-het-besluit-negatieve-lijst-2003-sb-2003-no-74.pdf>) · [ippc.int 8](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [assets.ippc.int 9](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/publications/ru/1366800159_ISPM_32_2009_Ru_2012-12-10.pdf>) · [dna.sr 10](<https://www.dna.sr/media/k4hdogeh/sb-2020-78.pdf>)

### Sao Tome and Principe (ST)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The 2016 regulation covers dried plant parts for any purpose and expressly includes post; advance CIAT permission and phytosanitary documentation are required under articles 23–24,29,38. No automatic food-tea exception is established.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sao160888.pdf>)

The 2016 phytosanitary regulation expressly covers dried plant parts for any purpose. Articles 23–24 require advance CIAT permission, article 29 requires phytosanitary documentation, and article 38 covers postal consignments. A ministerial exemption power is not itself an issued exemption.

**Personal post:** Post is explicitly covered; the limited pharmaceutical/medicinal exception does not establish an exception for this food tea.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No document-free shipping route established. Do not obtain the documented approvals: the store will not use this route. A future published automatic exemption would require reassessment.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/sao160888.pdf>)

<a id="sv"></a>
### El Salvador (SV)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Current personal-post customs exemption expressly includes certificates and permits, resolving the earlier permit-versus-certificate uncertainty. The MAG processing condition covers risk reduction; applying it to steamed/dried retail sencha is a stated inference. The 2025 general tea-extract/powder tariff entries do not override this specific personal-purchase exemption.

**Document-rule sources:** [mag.gob.sv 1](<https://www.mag.gob.sv/guia-para-autorizaciones-de-importacion-de-productos-y-subproductos-de-origen-vegetal-en-el-centro-de-importaciones-y-exportaciones-de-el-salvador-ciex-el-salvador/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 2](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/download/anexo-1-del-boletin-informativo-no-dga-003-2025/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 3](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/ANEXO-1-BOLETIN-INF-No.-DGA-003-2025.pdf>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 4](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/BOLETIN-INFORMATIVO-No-014-2023-NO-EXIGENCIA-DE-PERMISOS-DE-IMPORTACION-DE-DNM-MINSAL-Y-MAG.pdf>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 5](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/Boleti%CC%81n-Informativo-14-2023.pdf>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 6](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/download/disposicion-administrativa-de-caracter-general-no-dga-028-2025/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 7](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/DISPOSICI%C3%93N-ADMINISTRATIVA-DE-CAR%C3%81CTER-GENERAL-No.-DGA-028-2025-2.pdf>)

Personal online postal purchases up to US$300 are exempt from non-tariff import requirements under current DGA-028-2025; its definition expressly includes permits and certificates. DGA-014-2023 separately confirms MINSAL/MAG relief for personal foods and processed plant products whose processing reduces phytosanitary risk. Applying that general processing rule to manufactured steamed/dried sencha is an inference. Use original unaltered sealed retail packaging from a producer under origin-country oversight, lawful free sale in Japan, and product/processor/batch identification; retain the ordinary purchase receipt and customs declaration.

**Personal post:** Current DGA-028-2025 expressly covers natural persons purchasing online for personal consumption through postal, courier and specified other routes; resale or quantities beyond personal need are excluded. DGA-014-2023 separately addresses processed personal foods. Recipient identity and ordinary purchase/postal documents remain necessary.

**Quantity conditions:** Aggregate purchase price on the invoice(s), excluding delivery charges, for a noncommercial personal online purchase under DGA-028-2025. This is not a tea weight allowance. Value threshold: 300 USD.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** None for the specified manufactured steamed/dried sencha if the packaging, lawful-origin-sale, personal-use and US$300 purchase conditions are met. The conclusion applies the published processing exemption; it is not a tea-specific permit ruling.

**Sources:** [mag.gob.sv 1](<https://www.mag.gob.sv/guia-para-autorizaciones-de-importacion-de-productos-y-subproductos-de-origen-vegetal-en-el-centro-de-importaciones-y-exportaciones-de-el-salvador-ciex-el-salvador/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 2](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/download/anexo-1-del-boletin-informativo-no-dga-003-2025/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 3](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/ANEXO-1-BOLETIN-INF-No.-DGA-003-2025.pdf>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 4](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/Boleti%CC%81n-Informativo-14-2023.pdf>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 5](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/download/disposicion-administrativa-de-caracter-general-no-dga-028-2025/>) · [sitio.aduana.gob.sv 6](<https://sitio.aduana.gob.sv/wp-content/uploads/download-manager-files/DISPOSICI%C3%93N-ADMINISTRATIVA-DE-CAR%C3%81CTER-GENERAL-No.-DGA-028-2025-2.pdf>)

<a id="sx"></a>
### Saint Maarten (SX)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Current legal basis and operative scope for LVV plant-produce controls, including any law/conditions adopted after the official 2022 policy explicitly said plant-protection legislation was being developed. The current LVV administrative page is broader than that historical legal account but has no manufactured-food definition. Public-health private-use scope is resolved.

**Document-rule sources:** [sintmaartengov.org 1](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Ministries/Departments/Pages/Agriculture-Livestock-and-Fisheries.aspx>) · [newgmc.com 2](<https://www.newgmc.com/index.php?download=466%3Aproduce-and-plant-produce-import-form&id=35%3Asops-a-ssops&option=com_phocadownload&view=category>) · [lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl 3](<https://lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl/CVDR475613>) · [sintmaartengov.org 4](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Documents/Official%20Publications/AB%202017%2C%20no.%2033%20Landsbesluit%20publieke%20gezondheid.pdf>) · [sintmaartengov.org 5](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Documents/Policies/Final%20Approved%20Agri%20Policy%202022.pdf>)

Sint Maarten LVV plant controls still do not classify manufactured sencha. Separately, current Landsbesluit publieke gezondheid (AB 2017 no.33), article 4(2), confines food import notification to commercial use. Article 4(3) confines origin health certificates to commercial imports of dairy, fish, poultry/meat and baby food; plain tea is not listed. Its official explanatory note distinguishes personal from commercial use by presentation, packaging and quantity, without a passenger-only limitation. This narrows the unresolved issue to the separate plant-health classification rather than imposing commercial food paperwork on a 100 g private parcel.

**Personal post:** The public-health notification/certificate duties are limited by intended commercial use, not by whether a seller was paid or by a traveler-only allowance. Treating a single 100 g parcel intended for the recipient’s own consumption as personal follows the enacted scope and explanatory note. Independent LVV plant-product scope remains unverified.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Current legal basis and operative scope for LVV plant-produce controls, including any law/conditions adopted after the official 2022 policy explicitly said plant-protection legislation was being developed. The current LVV administrative page is broader than that historical legal account but has no manufactured-food definition. Public-health private-use scope is resolved.

**Sources:** [sintmaartengov.org 1](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Ministries/Departments/Pages/Agriculture-Livestock-and-Fisheries.aspx>) · [newgmc.com 2](<https://www.newgmc.com/index.php?download=466%3Aproduce-and-plant-produce-import-form&id=35%3Asops-a-ssops&option=com_phocadownload&view=category>) · [lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl 3](<https://lokaleregelgeving.overheid.nl/CVDR475613>) · [sintmaartengov.org 4](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Documents/Official%20Publications/AB%202017%2C%20no.%2033%20Landsbesluit%20publieke%20gezondheid.pdf>) · [sintmaartengov.org 5](<https://www.sintmaartengov.org/Documents/Policies/Final%20Approved%20Agri%20Policy%202022.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed Sint Maarten LVV plant-import guidance and application material. No manufactured-tea or personal-mail entry identified. 2026-09-30: Retrieved the government’s complete AB 2017 no.33 Public Health Decree PDF and checked its current official register, which marks it effective 27 July 2018 to present. Read article 4(1)–(3), the import-notice form, and official explanatory note on PDF pages 39–41. Notification is limited to commercial consumption goods; health certification is additionally limited to listed animal foods/baby food. The note expressly distinguishes personal use based on presentation/quantity and repeats the commercial limit. A 2020 Inspectorate release reproduced by news outlets describes personal food up to 10 kg, but that secondary numerical threshold was not promoted into a primary-source quantity limit. Kept overall unresolved solely because independent LVV manufactured-tea classification remains missing. 2026-09-30 fresh official online recovery: Recovered the current official LVV page HTML and checked its links/media. The Import Export Conditions item is an image without linked commodity conditions; the available forms concern small animals. This yields no processed-tea import rule. The independently verified Public Health Decree personal-food scope remains intact; unresolved issue is the separate LVV plant-product classification. Saved sx-lvv.html. 30 September renewed official-source/general-definition review: Targeted official government/legal searches for plant imports, processed products and phytosanitary tea returned no new operative plant-product definition. The existing government plant-produce form remains an application instrument, not an exhaustive definition/list; examples of seeds, cuttings, fruits and plants neither affirmatively include nor exclude manufactured sencha. The independently recovered 2017 Public Health Decree commercial-use limitation remains favorable for this private order and is not displaced by older commercial exporter summaries. No fresh exemption or permit duty is inferred. 30 September operative-source recovery: Retrieved complete approved 2022 AgriculturePolicy from current www government host after the legacy www 1 link served a construction page. Section 5.2(p 16/17) says agriculture regulations/plant-protection laws were still being developed and identifies Commodities Act(former 1997 NAFoodLaw 334) as governing food/agricultural products. This is positive historical evidence about legislative status, not just an empty search. Current LVV page nevertheless describes import monitoring for plants/plant produce without publishing a manufacturing definition; targeted 2025/26 official-source checks yielded no operative successor. No 2026 exemption is inferred from a 2022 policy. Existing current 2017 public-health private-use conclusion preserved.

<a id="tc"></a>
### Turks and Caicos (TC)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Not supported on a conservative reading of the enacted unqualified plant-part category and mandatory permit duty, applying them to the actual physical manufactured sencha leaves. This is a legal-scope inference, not a tea-specific authority decision. Category 1 processing evidence alone does not automatically override the distinct national plant category; the stronger alternative reading remains documented.

**Document-rule sources:** [gov.tc 1](<https://gov.tc/trade/portal/imports>) · [agriculture.gov.tc 2](<https://www.agriculture.gov.tc/residents>) · [agc.gov.tc 3](<https://agc.gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/25-of-2024-plant-health-regulations-2024/fdocument?Itemid=9999>) · [gov.tc 4](<https://gov.tc/agc/2016-ordinances>) · [gov.tc 5](<https://gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/07-of-2016-food-and-drugs-ordinance-2016/viewdocument/725?Itemid=>) · [gov.tc 6](<https://gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/07-of-2016-food-and-drugs-ordinance-2016/fdocument?Itemid=9999>) · [gov.tc 7](<https://www.gov.tc/environmentalhealth/services/food-hygiene>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 8](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/tc3-fruits2014.pdf>) · [assets.ippc.int 9](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2016/11/30/Plant_Health_Ordinance_2012l_2.pdf>) · [ippc.int 10](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [borderforce.gov.tc 11](<https://borderforce.gov.tc/pets-food-plants>)

Conservative no-document exclusion by explicit domestic legal-scope inference. Plant Health Ordinance 2012 section 2 includes a part of a plant without a living/propagative qualifier; regulated article(a) includes any plant. The actual sencha consists of manufactured tea leaves, so this independent plant-part category is read as continuing to cover it even though its manufacture supports exclusion from the separate risk-qualified plant-product limb. Section 34(2)(a) requires prior permits for all regulated articles, not just a subsequently designated list; section 39(2) and 2024 regulations 25–27 expressly include mail/courier. Packaging is also named separately, but hypothetical pouch capability is not the basis of this exclusion. Domestic ISPM-congruence duties and the Category 1 manufacturing reading are the strongest contrary interpretation, preserved in definitionReview. No automatic provision recovered reconciles them by expressly excluding these nonliving leaves. Private-food controls supply no separate certificate barrier.

**Personal post:** Sections 34–35 apply to any regulated import; section 39(2) specifically covers postal arrivals without permits and 2024 regulations 25–27 govern mail/courier. Passenger section 42 and the current Border Force fruit/vegetable 25 lb carry-in allowance do not waive purchased personal postal leaf-tea requirements. The 2024 fee schedule expressly distinguishes home-consumption permits.

**Checkout:** 82 pouches / 8.2 kg tea; highest carrier band 10 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [gov.tc 1](<https://gov.tc/trade/portal/imports>) · [agriculture.gov.tc 2](<https://www.agriculture.gov.tc/residents>) · [agc.gov.tc 3](<https://agc.gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/25-of-2024-plant-health-regulations-2024/fdocument?Itemid=9999>) · [gov.tc 4](<https://gov.tc/agc/2016-ordinances>) · [gov.tc 5](<https://gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/07-of-2016-food-and-drugs-ordinance-2016/viewdocument/725?Itemid=>) · [gov.tc 6](<https://gov.tc/agc/component/edocman/07-of-2016-food-and-drugs-ordinance-2016/fdocument?Itemid=9999>) · [gov.tc 7](<https://www.gov.tc/environmentalhealth/services/food-hygiene>) · [ippc.int 8](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 9](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/tc3-fruits2014.pdf>) · [assets.ippc.int 10](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2016/11/30/Plant_Health_Ordinance_2012l_2.pdf>) · [borderforce.gov.tc 11](<https://borderforce.gov.tc/pets-food-plants>)

<a id="td"></a>
### Chad (TD)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The broad plant-material definition includes plant parts and objects manufactured from plants; articles 16 and 18 require an origin phytosanitary certificate for that material. Applying this to the scoped dried leaves is an explicit inference. The store cannot satisfy the certificate requirement; tea is not thereby described as absolutely prohibited.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/cha4040.pdf>)

Law 14/PR/95 article 2(b) defines plant material broadly to include plant parts and objects manufactured from plants; this is separate from article 2(c), which also covers products simply prepared by drying. Article 16 subjects all plant material to import control, and article 18 requires an origin phytosanitary certificate for all articles covered by article 16. Applying the broad plant-material wording to identifiable steamed/dried sencha leaves is a reasoned inference. The separate approval for regular professional importers in article 17 is not automatically imposed on private consumers.

**Personal post:** The consignment-certificate requirement is not limited to professional importers. No small private postal exception was found in the reviewed law.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/cha4040.pdf>)

<a id="tf"></a>
### French Southern Territories (TF)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

For the published postal route to the southern districts (Crozet, Kerguelen and Amsterdam), the guide prohibits dried/dehydrated vegetation. Applying that broad category to steamed/dried sencha is an inference; neither guide provides an industrial-processing or sealed-retail exception. The store’s ordinary-post-only route is therefore unsupported. This does not assert an identical legal rule for every other TAAF district.

**Document-rule sources:** [taaf.fr 1](<https://taaf.fr/content/uploads/2025/09/Guide-hivernant-2025-MAJ-290925.pdf>) · [taaf.fr 2](<https://taaf.fr/content/uploads/2023/09/Livret-Biosecurite.pdf>)

The 2025 TAAF guide, page 47, prohibits dried/dehydrated vegetation in postal consignments; artisanal herbal teas/spices are examples, not a stated limit on that category. The handbook, printed pages 9–10, also prohibits vegetation without express authorization. Treating factory-sealed sencha as dried vegetation is an explicitly identified inference.

**Personal post:** The guidance expressly concerns post to Crozet, Kerguelen and Amsterdam. Packaging and BIOSECURISE labelling instructions apply to permitted contents; they do not waive the preceding prohibition.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Only an applicable automatic manufactured-tea exception could support this southern-district postal route. Separate conditions for other TAAF districts were not established.

**Sources:** [taaf.fr 1](<https://taaf.fr/content/uploads/2025/09/Guide-hivernant-2025-MAJ-290925.pdf>) · [taaf.fr 2](<https://taaf.fr/content/uploads/2023/09/Livret-Biosecurite.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read postal and biosecurity guides, including food packaging/inventory instructions; did not treat the herbal-tea example as definitive for manufactured sencha. Downloaded and read the full 2023 biosecurity handbook pp. 9–10: express authorization for introduction of plants and a postal prohibition naming dried/dehydrated vegetation. Cross-checked the 2025 guide’s postal page 47 and its artisanal-tea examples; retained unresolved for manufactured sencha rather than inferring either universal permission or prohibition. 30 September: a second reviewer independently read the overarching dried/dehydrated category and the artisanal examples, concluding that exclusion of sencha is a defensible inference rather than inventing an unstated retail/manufacturing exception. Retained the geographic limitation of the postal guidance.

<a id="tg"></a>
### Togo (TG)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Whether the order’s prior declaration applies to personal postal food purchases, with a non-business procedure or exemption, remains unverified. Do not extend annual business-import authorization to private recipients solely because tea is plant food.

**Document-rule sources:** [togotrade.gouv.tg 1](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/media/LOI%20N%C2%B0%2096%20-%20007-%20PR%20relative%20%C3%A0%20la%20protection%20des%20v%C3%A9g%C3%A9taux.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/tg3-1998-099pqreg_fr.pdf>) · [agriculture.gouv.tg 3](<https://agriculture.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/ARRETE-INTERMINISTERIEL-PORTANT-TARIFICATION-DES-PRESTATIONS-PAYANTES-A-LA-DIRECTION-DE-LA-PROTECTION-DES-VEGETAUX.pdf>) · [agriculture.gouv.tg 4](<https://agriculture.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/FICHE-DE-DEMANDE-DU-PERMIS-DIMPORTATION-DES-VEGETAUX-ET-PRODUITS-VEGETAUX.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/tog238369.pdf>) · [jo.gouv.tg 6](<https://jo.gouv.tg/sites/default/files/JO/JOS_24_07_2020-65E%20ANNEE%2025TER.pdf>) · [commerce.gouv.tg 7](<https://commerce.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Rapport_pays_SC_version_validee.pdf>) · [togotrade.gouv.tg 8](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/menu/38>) · [togotrade.gouv.tg 9](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/media/ETAPES%20A%20SUIVRE%20POUR%20SE%20LANCER%20DANS%20LE%20COMMERCE%20ELECTRONIQUE%20TRANSFRONTALIER.pdf>)

Law 96-007 includes dried plant products, but articles 28–29 leave permits and accompanying documents subject to implementing prescriptions. Decree 98-099 articles 27–28 likewise defer the commodity conditions to ministerial orders. The operative Ministry-hosted fee order of 2 August 2011 expressly lists imported tea among foodstuffs subject to inspection, so processed tea cannot be assumed outside plant-health controls. That fee list does not itself establish which special document a 100 g private postal parcel needs. The official permit application accommodates postal entry, but a form alone does not prove that every inspected food needs a permit.

**Personal post:** The official permit form expressly accommodates postal imports; no general postal exemption is established. Whether that permit is required for the scoped tea has not been resolved by the published fee schedule or general law.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Downloaded official Gazette of 24 July 2020, Order 031/MCIDSPPCL/MEF/MAPAH of 17 July 2020. Annex includes foodstuffs of plant origin. Articles 2–7 create a prior declaration/approval process; article 3 asks for annual quantities, three-year imports, a valid company-creation card, economic-operator ID and tax clearance, and article 7 fixes an annual quantity authorization. This is a real special procedure, but its economic-operator context does not establish application to a one-off private postal buyer.

**Evidence access:** Actual Gazette downloaded and articles/annex read, printed pages 9–11. The later government trade-policy report link returned 403; it is a current-context locator only, not proof of full text inspected.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** The operative documentary prescription applicable to a one-off private postal tea parcel, and whether the prior-declaration/annual economic-operator procedure has a personal scope or exception.

**Sources:** [togotrade.gouv.tg 1](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/media/LOI%20N%C2%B0%2096%20-%20007-%20PR%20relative%20%C3%A0%20la%20protection%20des%20v%C3%A9g%C3%A9taux.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/tg3-1998-099pqreg_fr.pdf>) · [agriculture.gouv.tg 3](<https://agriculture.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/ARRETE-INTERMINISTERIEL-PORTANT-TARIFICATION-DES-PRESTATIONS-PAYANTES-A-LA-DIRECTION-DE-LA-PROTECTION-DES-VEGETAUX.pdf>) · [agriculture.gouv.tg 4](<https://agriculture.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/FICHE-DE-DEMANDE-DU-PERMIS-DIMPORTATION-DES-VEGETAUX-ET-PRODUITS-VEGETAUX.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/tog238369.pdf>) · [jo.gouv.tg 6](<https://jo.gouv.tg/sites/default/files/JO/JOS_24_07_2020-65E%20ANNEE%2025TER.pdf>) · [commerce.gouv.tg 7](<https://commerce.gouv.tg/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Rapport_pays_SC_version_validee.pdf>) · [togotrade.gouv.tg 8](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/menu/38>) · [togotrade.gouv.tg 9](<https://togotrade.gouv.tg/media/ETAPES%20A%20SUIVRE%20POUR%20SE%20LANCER%20DANS%20LE%20COMMERCE%20ELECTRONIQUE%20TRANSFRONTALIER.pdf>)

**Research trail:** Read articles 2,28,29 and searched tea/coffee import procedures and personal-use exemptions. Downloaded and read Decree 98-099, OCRed the 2011 fee order naming tea and the official postal-capable permit form. Read the 9 February 2024 pest-list order: it updates regulated organisms and interception measures, not a tea-document exemption or requirement. Generic ECOWAS inspection guidance and industrial-port procedures do not settle the private-post scope. 30 September independent food/conformity pass: Downloaded official Gazette of 24 July 2020, Order 031/MCIDSPPCL/MEF/MAPAH of 17 July 2020. Annex includes foodstuffs of plant origin. Articles 2–7 create a prior declaration/approval process; article 3 asks for annual quantities, three-year imports, a valid company-creation card, economic-operator ID and tax clearance, and article 7 fixes an annual quantity authorization. This is a real special procedure, but its economic-operator context does not establish application to a one-off private postal buyer. Actual Gazette downloaded and articles/annex read, printed pages 9–11. The later government trade-policy report link returned 403; it is a current-context locator only, not proof of full text inspected. 30 September fresh official-online recovery: Recovered newly published official cross-border e-commerce guide linked from Togo Trade Portal. It addresses establishing an online business, NIF/company registration, logistics and compliance with destination rules. It creates neither a retail-customer permit duty nor a private parcel exemption. The portal commodity procedures remain business workflows; no tea procedure was located. 30 September common-definition review: Law 96-007 expressly includes dried material; Decree 98-099 Articles 27–28 leave documentary prescriptions to ministerial measures. The 2011 fee order actually names imported tea for inspection, so ISPM 32 cannot be treated as displacing a contrary domestic processing scope. Examined international references do not establish general ISPM definition priority. The inspection fee itself is not a certificate requirement.

<a id="th"></a>
### Thailand (TH)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The evidenced default route requires a permit, certificate or special regulatory clearance. Do not ship under the no-special-documents constraint unless an applicable exemption is affirmatively established.

**Document-rule sources:** [bangkokherbarium.doa.go.th 1](<https://bangkokherbarium.doa.go.th/lawTh/EN2550.pdf>) · [ippc.int 2](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/thailand/reportingobligation/2017/03/notification-of-ministry-of-agriculture-and-cooperatives-plants-and-carriers-from-certain-sources-as-restricted-articles-3/>) · [logistics.fda.moph.go.th 3](<https://logistics.fda.moph.go.th/media.php?id=678157204414668800&name=personal+use-en.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 4](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

[MAFF-POSTAL](#maff-postal): **Q**. Thai 2007 notification lists dried Camellia sinensis leaves as restricted articles. Its preserved-food exception is not treated as an established waiver for this postal sencha product.

**Personal post:** Exact Japanese postal manufactured-green-tea row; FDA personal-food tea quantity remains 1 kg and does not waive quarantine.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 1 kg product weight. Tea for personal import. FDA personal-use tea quantity; does not itself waive agricultural documentation.

**Checkout:** 10 pouches / 1 kg tea; highest carrier band 1.28 kg gross. Current maximum is within this numeric threshold; this does not settle permits, value limits, frequency, packaging or genuine personal use.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Arrange Japanese phytosanitary certification before posting unless Thai DOA provides an explicit processing-based exemption for this product.

**Sources:** [bangkokherbarium.doa.go.th 1](<https://bangkokherbarium.doa.go.th/lawTh/EN2550.pdf>) · [ippc.int 2](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/thailand/reportingobligation/2017/03/notification-of-ministry-of-agriculture-and-cooperatives-plants-and-carriers-from-certain-sources-as-restricted-articles-3/>) · [logistics.fda.moph.go.th 3](<https://logistics.fda.moph.go.th/media.php?id=678157204414668800&name=personal+use-en.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 4](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/e_hayami_yubin.pdf?d=1702>)

<a id="tj"></a>
### Tajikistan (TJ)

**Phytosanitary certificate route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The current official regulated-material list expressly includes finished tea under HS 0902, and Article 13(1) requires a phytosanitary certificate on import. The store cannot satisfy that certificate requirement.

**Document-rule sources:** [ncz.tj 1](<https://ncz.tj/node/1341>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/eu/tajikistan/index.html>) · [tajtrade.tj 3](<https://tajtrade.tj/media/%E2%84%96375%20%D0%9F%D0%9F%D0%A0%D0%A2%20%D0%9F%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%8C%20%D0%BF%D0%BE%D0%B4%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B9%20%D0%BC%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%B8%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B2.pdf>)

Government Resolution 375 of 23 June 2020, Annex 1 item 37, expressly lists tea with or without flavouring (HS 0902) as regulated material, without a retail-packaging exclusion. Law 1567 of 2019 as amended in June 2024, Article 13(1), requires the exporting authority’s phytosanitary certificate for imported regulated material. The earlier uncertainty about manufactured tea classification is resolved by that commodity list.

**Personal post:** Article 13 applies to importation of regulated material, not only commercial imports. The HS 0902 listing has no small-package or personal-use exclusion. A personal postal shipment is therefore within the published certificate rule; no postal exemption was found.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**Sources:** [ncz.tj 1](<https://ncz.tj/node/1341>) · [maff.go.jp 2](<https://www.maff.go.jp/pps/j/search/ekuni/eu/tajikistan/index.html>) · [tajtrade.tj 3](<https://tajtrade.tj/media/%E2%84%96375%20%D0%9F%D0%9F%D0%A0%D0%A2%20%D0%9F%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%87%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%8C%20%D0%BF%D0%BE%D0%B4%D0%BA%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B9%20%D0%BC%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%B8%D0%B0%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B2.pdf>)

<a id="tn"></a>
### Tunisia (TN)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The current NPPO-filed Order of 19 February 2016 Article 5(6) automatically exempts small amounts of non-seed plant products for personal consumption from phytosanitary certification and inspection. It is not restricted to accompanied baggage. This supports small personal postal purchases; it does not establish any specific large-order allowance.

**Document-rule sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2025/02/16/exigences-phytosanitaire-a-limportation-fr.pdf>)

Order of 19 February 2016 article 5(6) exempts small quantities of plant products for personal consumption, excluding seeds, from phytosanitary certification and inspection. The provision is not expressly limited to passenger baggage.

**Personal post:** The statutory exemption supports a small personal postal consignment, but the reviewed rule does not define a numeric 'small quantity' ceiling.

**Quantity conditions:** Small quantity for personal consumption; no numerical limit found.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**To resolve:** The term small quantity has no verified numerical definition. The 24.9 kg checkout maximum must not be represented as a verified personal-use allowance.

**Sources:** [assets.ippc.int 1](<https://assets.ippc.int/static/media/files/reportingobligation/2025/02/16/exigences-phytosanitaire-a-limportation-fr.pdf>)

<a id="to"></a>
### Tonga (TO)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Conservative exclusion under the current parent Act’s affirmative permit duty: section 25(1), read with the unqualified section 2 plant-material definition, covers processed tea by legal-scope inference. Regulation 26’s risk-qualified requirement is acknowledged, but it does not expressly say manufactured goods outside that assessment may enter without permit, unlike the express no-permit concessions in regulation 23. No applicable automatic tea/general-manufactured-food permit waiver was found. This is a special import-permit barrier under the store constraint, not a finding that tea is prohibited or an unsupported demand for a foreign certificate.

**Document-rule sources:** [maff.gov.to 1](<https://maff.gov.to/qqmd/>) · [mpi.govt.nz 2](<https://www.mpi.govt.nz/dmsdocument/697/direct>) · [micor.agriculture.gov.au 3](<https://micor.agriculture.gov.au/plants/tonga_to/herb-and-species---dried>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ton80201.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ton49222.pdf>) · [maff.gov.to 6](<https://maff.gov.to/legislation/>) · [ago.gov.to 7](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/2020/2020-0073/FoodAct2020_1.pdf>) · [maff.gov.to 8](<https://maff.gov.to/division/food/>) · [wto.org 9](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s409_e.pdf>) · [ago.gov.to 10](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1981/1981-0024/PlantQuarantineAct_3.pdf>) · [ago.gov.to 11](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/legislation/current-revised-edition/by-title.html?view=acts_alpha>) · [gov.si 12](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>)

Current official Plant Quarantine Act (2020 Revised Edition), section 2, defines plant material as all materials of plant origin; manufactured items are excluded only from the separate definition of plant. Section 25(1) makes importing any plant, plant material or other regulated article unlawful until a written permit is issued under prescribed regulations. Applying this unqualified plant-material scope to finished steamed/dried sencha is a legal-scope inference. Section 17 was repealed in 1995 and is not a current permit provision. Regulation 26 specifically requires permits with conditions for manufactured material assessed by the Director as a potential serious exotic-pest carrier, but contains no express general permit waiver for other manufactured material; regulations 23–25 contain narrower preserved fruit/vegetable and dried-flower provisions. ISPM 32’s Category 1 tea example is technical evidence, not an automatic domestic statutory waiver. Food Act 2020 import definitions concern commercial purposes and do not independently establish a private-food certificate duty.

**Personal post:** The Act defines import by any conveyance, applies to any person, authorizes examination/opening of mail in sections 11–12, and expressly requires Customs/Post Office holding and notification for regulated incoming mail in section 29. There is no personal-consumption or 100 g exception in the reviewed permit provision.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No additional source is needed for the conservative no-permit store exclusion under section 25(1). A published automatic permit waiver for manufactured retail tea, or authoritative legal construction that regulation 26 exhaustively displaces section 25(1) for such material, would change it. The reviewed text contains neither; the interpretive caveat is preserved rather than presenting a tea-specific ruling.

**Sources:** [maff.gov.to 1](<https://maff.gov.to/qqmd/>) · [mpi.govt.nz 2](<https://www.mpi.govt.nz/dmsdocument/697/direct>) · [micor.agriculture.gov.au 3](<https://micor.agriculture.gov.au/plants/tonga_to/herb-and-species---dried>) · [faolex.fao.org 4](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ton80201.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/ton49222.pdf>) · [maff.gov.to 6](<https://maff.gov.to/legislation/>) · [ago.gov.to 7](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/2020/2020-0073/FoodAct2020_1.pdf>) · [maff.gov.to 8](<https://maff.gov.to/division/food/>) · [wto.org 9](<https://www.wto.org/english/tratop_e/tpr_e/s409_e.pdf>) · [ago.gov.to 10](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/images/LEGISLATION/PRINCIPAL/1981/1981-0024/PlantQuarantineAct_3.pdf>) · [ago.gov.to 11](<https://ago.gov.to/cms/legislation/current-revised-edition/by-title.html?view=acts_alpha>) · [gov.si 12](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastline/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>)

<a id="tt"></a>
### Trinidad and Tobago (TT)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Exclude under the no-certificate constraint: Food and Drugs Act section 32(2) and regulation 10 independently require an origin-law/free-sale certificate for imported food. Application to personal mailed sencha is an explicit inference from the unqualified duty; no automatic personal exception was established. This is a food certificate, not a phytosanitary certificate.

**Document-rule sources:** [agriculture.gov.tt 1](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/media-releases/what-you-need-to-know-about-a-plant-import-permit/>) · [ttbizlink.gov.tt 2](<https://www.ttbizlink.gov.tt/trade/tnt/cmn/pdf/Plant%20Protection%20Subsidiary%20Regulations-63.56.pdf>) · [micor.agriculture.gov.au 3](<https://micor.agriculture.gov.au/plants/trinidad_and_tobago_tt/other>) · [agriculture.gov.tt 4](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/publications/legal-notice-no-333-2020-the-plant-protection-exemption-from-import-permit-order-2020/>) · [ippc.int 5](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/trinidad-and-tobago/reportingobligation/2020/10/legal-notice-333-the-plant-protection-exemption-from-import-permit-order-2020-1/>) · [faolex.fao.org 6](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/tri123063.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.tt 7](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Food-and-Drugs-Act-30-01.pdf>) · [laws.gov.tt 8](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/106042?type=act>) · [laws.gov.tt 9](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/list?currentid=182&offset=180&q=>) · [laws.gov.tt 10](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/124738?type=amendment>) · [laws.gov.tt 11](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/124781?type=amendment>)

Food and Drugs Act Cap.30:01 section 32(2) prohibits importing any food without a certificate confirming origin-country legal conformity and lawful sale. Food and Drugs Regulations rule 10 requires an English-language certificate from the competent origin-country official body, or a Minister-acceptable issuer where no such body exists. The government-hosted 2016 consolidation retains this unqualified duty, and no automatic personal-food exception was established. Applying it to sencha is an explicit legal-scope inference. The separate question whether tea falls within the 2020 processed-herbs plant exemption is not resolved or needed for this food-document exclusion.

**Personal post:** The food certificate duty applies to any imported food without an express resale, minimum quantity or transport-mode qualifier. Applying it to a private postal purchase is a stated inference. No automatic personal-post exception was established; the plant ministry separately confirms mail/courier coverage for regulated plants.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** No ordinary-declaration-only route supported under Food and Drugs Act section 32(2) and regulation 10. A specific operative personal-post food-certificate exemption could change the result, but none was established. Whether processed Camellia tea is included in the plant-order herbs/spices exemption remains a separate non-decisive classification question.

**Sources:** [agriculture.gov.tt 1](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/media-releases/what-you-need-to-know-about-a-plant-import-permit/>) · [ttbizlink.gov.tt 2](<https://www.ttbizlink.gov.tt/trade/tnt/cmn/pdf/Plant%20Protection%20Subsidiary%20Regulations-63.56.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.tt 3](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/publications/legal-notice-no-333-2020-the-plant-protection-exemption-from-import-permit-order-2020/>) · [ippc.int 4](<https://www.ippc.int/en/countries/trinidad-and-tobago/reportingobligation/2020/10/legal-notice-333-the-plant-protection-exemption-from-import-permit-order-2020-1/>) · [faolex.fao.org 5](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/tri123063.pdf>) · [agriculture.gov.tt 6](<https://agriculture.gov.tt/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Food-and-Drugs-Act-30-01.pdf>) · [laws.gov.tt 7](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/106042?type=act>) · [laws.gov.tt 8](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/list?currentid=182&offset=180&q=>) · [laws.gov.tt 9](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/124738?type=amendment>) · [laws.gov.tt 10](<https://laws.gov.tt/ttdll-web/revision/download/124781?type=amendment>)

**Research trail:** Reviewed ministry notice expressly covering online/mail plant purchases and Plant Protection subsidiary regulations. Preserved-fruit/vegetable exceptions do not expressly resolve manufactured tea. 2026-09-30: Found Australia Micor reproduction of Trinidad and Tobago NPPO processed-product exemptions (2019 notification), including sterilised dried herbs/spices. Tea is not named; ordinary sencha steaming is not proof of sterilisation. Did not assume that exemption covers loose manufactured tea. Read full Legal Notice 333/2020, retained in current Ministry legislative framework and 2022 consolidated Act. Paragraph 2 exempts multi-method processed/sterilized/cooked herbs and spices, roasted coffee and 16 other classes; Camellia tea is not explicitly listed. NPPO IPPC notice confirms listed exemptions cover phytosanitary certificates as well as permits. Whether the herbs category includes manufactured Camellia tea remains unestablished; ordinary sencha steaming is not assumed to satisfy a sterilization condition. 2026-09-30: Retrieved full government-hosted 2007 and 2016 consolidations of Food and Drugs Act/Regulations; read section 2 food/importer definitions, section 32(2), regulation 10 and published exemption clauses. No automatic personal-food certificate exemption found. Checked current official law index and downloaded the 2026 notices: LN 423 approves new drugs, LN 409 adds Indian Pharmacopoeia, LN 34/35 correct drug approvals; none changes food-import certification. Targeted current health/customs personal-food searches found no tea waiver. The separate LN 333/2020 herbs-category question is preserved rather than falsely declaring phytosanitary requirements settled. 2026-09-30: Screened the current official index’s 56 notices from 2017–2026: 49 readable titles/operative notices concern drug approvals, drug withdrawal, pharmaceutical schedules or corrigenda; read full 2017/2019 regulatory amendments and OCR-read scanned LN 283/2020. Seven older linked downloads were truncated/unreadable and were not treated as verified evidence. Saved the title/access log. This cross-check found no personal-food certificate exemption and does not claim every post-2016 notice was readable.

<a id="tv"></a>
### Tuvalu (TV)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The cited agricultural rule supports this finished retail tea without an import permit, phytosanitary certificate or special agricultural approval; ordinary postal/customs declarations and the stated product/quantity conditions remain.

**Personal post:** The exemption is for importation of goods, without a baggage-only limitation.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

### Taiwan (TW)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The cited agricultural rule supports this finished retail tea without an import permit, phytosanitary certificate or special agricultural approval; ordinary postal/customs declarations and the stated product/quantity conditions remain.

Taiwan agriculture guidance identifies green, black and roasted tea as non-quarantine processed plant products. The international-post guidance expressly lists these teas as exempt commodities.

**Personal post:** Applies to international postal packages; current APHIA guidance likewise exempts completely processed/roasted tea.

**Quantity conditions:** Up to 6 kg product weight. Per customs declaration line item for personal food imports. Separate TFDA personal food inspection exemption; tea origin-document exception also uses 6 kg. Value threshold: 1000 USD.

<a id="tz"></a>
### Tanzania (United Rep.) (TZ)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

Mainland Tanzania: TBS’s current PVoC list explicitly includes retail green tea HS 0902.10.00 and 0902.10.90 (March 2025 list, page 23). The amended 2025 regulation 17 and programme guide require conformity certification for listed consignments. The published personal-goods and under-US$5,000 concessions require an application and approval per shipment; neither is automatic. This independently excludes the store’s no-special-documents route without claiming that a phytosanitary certificate is required. Zanzibar has a separate ZFDA food-import registration/permit route expressly including personal-use importers in its current-linked 2018 guidance. The agricultural pest-risk classification remains unverified but cannot remove these separate requirements.

**Document-rule sources:** [tbs.go.tz 1](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/PVoC%20HS%20Codes%2029%20March%202025_1785233045.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 2](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/PVoC%20Exemption%202025.FINAL_1786358956.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 3](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/GUIDELINE%20FOR%20PVOC%20PROGRAMME%20-%202025_1786358704.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 4](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/documents/sw-1786362319-Batch%20Amendment%20Regulation.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 5](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/documents/sw-1786362149-The%20Standards%20(%20Imports%20Registration%20And%20Batch%20Certification)%20Regulations,%202021.pdf>) · [trade.tanzania.go.tz 6](<https://trade.tanzania.go.tz/media/GUIDELINES%20FOR%20FOOD%20IMPORTATION%20AND%20EXPORTATION.pdf>) · [zfda.go.tz 7](<https://zfda.go.tz/download/guidelines-for-food-importation-and-exportation/>)

The 2023 Plant Health Regulations require permits for regulated plant products and explicitly cover courier/postal consignments in regulation 72. Manufactured-product coverage depends on pest risk; no authoritative finished-tea classification was found.

**Personal post:** The mainland conformity regime defines importation to include goods brought in for use, not only sale; the programme covers each listed consignment. Its personal-goods and low-value exemptions require a special application, and no automatic postal exemption is published in the reviewed guide. Zanzibar guidance explicitly includes personal food importers. No traveler-only allowance is used.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Separate agricultural question: does TPHPA classify steamed/dried retail tea as a regulated pest-risk-bearing product? No answer is needed to establish the independent conformity/food-document incompatibility with this store’s constraint.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/tan231675.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 2](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/PVoC%20HS%20Codes%2029%20March%202025_1785233045.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 3](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/PVoC%20Exemption%202025.FINAL_1786358956.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 4](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/GUIDELINE%20FOR%20PVOC%20PROGRAMME%20-%202025_1786358704.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 5](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/documents/sw-1786362319-Batch%20Amendment%20Regulation.pdf>) · [tbs.go.tz 6](<https://www.tbs.go.tz/uploads/documents/sw-1786362149-The%20Standards%20(%20Imports%20Registration%20And%20Batch%20Certification)%20Regulations,%202021.pdf>) · [trade.tanzania.go.tz 7](<https://trade.tanzania.go.tz/media/GUIDELINES%20FOR%20FOOD%20IMPORTATION%20AND%20EXPORTATION.pdf>) · [zfda.go.tz 8](<https://zfda.go.tz/download/guidelines-for-food-importation-and-exportation/>)

**Research trail:** Read regulations 56 and 72 and the manufactured-product definition; searched personal tea imports without equating business Tea Board licensing with consumer obligations. 30 September: recovered TBS’s migrated current official law and import-control downloads, rather than relying on dead pre-migration URLs. Read GN 681/2021 definitions and business-limited registration rule, GN 127/2025 substituted regulation 17, the full 2025 PVoC guide and exemption criteria, and exact retail-green-tea entries on page 23 of the March 2025 list. Independent review confirmed no automatic private-post exception and distinguished business registration from the separate conformity regime. Downloaded and OCR-read ZFDA’s current-linked 2018 guidance, PDF pages 9–12 (printed 4–7), confirming personal importers and the registration/permit route. Did not apply obsolete mainland TFDA 2011 guidance as current TBS law.

<a id="ug"></a>
### Uganda (UG)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Conditional support combines the domestic risk-qualified manufactured-product definition applied to clean industrial sencha with an affirmative food-conformity arrival route. Full SI 112/2022 regulation 7(1), notwithstanding regulation 5, provides destination inspection for Part 1 food arriving without a pre-export CoC. Regulation 8(1) makes the application an ordinary customs declaration through the electronic single window prior to OR upon arrival, with usual shipping information; 8(2)(i) requires an exemption letter only where applicable. This assessed route does not seek a regulation 5(5) exemption. Regulation 7(3)/10(6) and the current integrated portal provide authority-issued clearance after successful inspection, which is allowed under the stated constraint. The 2026 news says importers can create accounts, not that every private postal recipient must separately register. The substantial contrary reading applies the exemption-letter requirement automatically to low-value Part 2 food even when using regulation 7; the preferred reading gives effect to regulation 7's notwithstanding clause and preserves a non-exemption arrival route. No hypothetical unpublished registration duty is imposed. Actual standards compliance, accurate declaration, possible testing, surcharge and other lawful fees remain conditions.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/uga158615.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/uga238074.pdf>) · [ulii.org 3](<https://ulii.org/en/akn/ug/act/si/2020/4/eng%402020-01-24>) · [mtic.go.ug 4](<https://www.mtic.go.ug/export-and-import-procedures/permits-and-licences/>) · [agriculture.go.ug 5](<https://www.agriculture.go.ug/online-certification/>) · [webstore.unbs.go.ug 6](<https://webstore.unbs.go.ug/store.php?preview=&src=4027>) · [unbs.go.ug 7](<https://www.unbs.go.ug/attachments/menus/57/THE%20UNBS%20%28INSPECTION%20%26%20CLEARANCE%29%20REGULATION%202022.pdf>) · [unbs.go.ug 8](<https://www.unbs.go.ug/attachments/menus/14/Portal.pdf>) · [verigates.bureauveritas.com 9](<https://verigates.bureauveritas.com/sites/verigates/files/2023-07/UGANDA%20%28PVoC%29%20Datasheet%20Rev%2011.1.pdf>) · [ulii.org 10](<https://ulii.org/en/akn/ug/act/2016/6/eng%402023-12-31>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 11](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/ug_2016-6cons2023_ppact_en.pdf>) · [unbs.go.ug 12](<https://unbs.go.ug/news-highlights.php?news=339&read=>) · [media.ulii.org 13](<https://media.ulii.org/media/legislation/107267/source_file/0360de7f6a9a8b96/uganda-national-bureau-of-standards-inspection-and-clearance-of-import-regulations-2022.pdf>) · [media.ulii.org 14](<https://media.ulii.org/media/legislation/107109/source_file/d505729b34f2d023/uganda-national-bureau-of-standards-certification-regulations-2021.pdf>) · [ugandatrades.go.ug 15](<https://ugandatrades.go.ug/media/UNBS%20Certification%20Regulations,%202022%20-%20SI%20No%20108%20of%202022.pdf>)

Explicit processing-scope inference: current consolidated Plant Protection and Health Act Chapter 39 section 1 covers manufactured products only where their nature/processing may create plant-pest or disease risk. Applying actual industrial steaming, rolling and drying with current-linked ISPM 32 tea Category 1 as technical evidence supports excluding clean manufactured sencha from that definition, without claiming direct national incorporation of ISPM 32. SI 4/2020 regulates defined plant goods and risk-capable articles. Clean non-plant packaging and freedom from pests remain conditions; statutory arrival inspection is preserved. The independent UNBS import-clearance process remains unresolved under the no-special-registration constraint.

**Personal post:** Regulation 6 expressly includes personal use. The usual low-value exemption procedure calls for an authority letter, but whether the alternative regulation 7 destination-inspection route operates for private post using only routine customs documents is unresolved. Returning-resident personal effects are not this online purchase.

**Separate food or conformity rule:** Full SI 112/2022 applies compulsory standards to trade AND personal use (regulations 2/6). Food is Part 1 Schedule 1; regulation 7 expressly notwithstanding regulation 5 allows arrival destination inspection without pre-export CoC. Regulation 8(1) requires ordinary electronic customs declaration upon arrival; applicable shipping information remains. Regulation 8(2)(i) exemption letter belongs to a regulation 5(5) exemption route, and is not treated as universally required under the separate regulation 7 route. Inspection and authority-issued ICC are routine arrival outcomes permitted by the audit constraint. SI 112 regulation 13(3) digital-mark proof is limited to Part II Schedule 3 of the Certification Regulations 2021, whose cosmetics/electricals/construction categories do not include tea. Actual SI 108/2022 amendment section 13 changes only the per-unit fee, not the categories;23 A(1) is substantive standards compliance, and 23 A(2) digital-mark rule concerns listed local manufacture. Schedule 1 Part 3 low-value destination-inspection exception EXPRESSLY excludes food, so inspection remains; Schedule 2 paragraph 2 minimum fee has its own belowUS$1300 value exception, not a food-inspection waiver. Possible 15% surcharge, laboratory fees and other lawful charges are not waived by this conclusion.

**Evidence access:** Complete actual Gazette SI 112/2022 now recovered through ULII media, replacing the prior full-PDF access gap. Full SI 68/2021 certification regulations and actual Gazette SI 108/2022 amendment recovered too, the latter from the official Trade Ministry portal. All operative routes, digital-mark provisions and schedules independently read. Original UNBS host still returns 403; no claim it supplied the full download. Current compulsory green-tea specification and 2026 integrated portal news remain corroborative.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/uga158615.pdf>) · [faolex.fao.org 2](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/uga238074.pdf>) · [ulii.org 3](<https://ulii.org/en/akn/ug/act/si/2020/4/eng%402020-01-24>) · [mtic.go.ug 4](<https://www.mtic.go.ug/export-and-import-procedures/permits-and-licences/>) · [agriculture.go.ug 5](<https://www.agriculture.go.ug/online-certification/>) · [webstore.unbs.go.ug 6](<https://webstore.unbs.go.ug/store.php?preview=&src=4027>) · [unbs.go.ug 7](<https://www.unbs.go.ug/attachments/menus/57/THE%20UNBS%20%28INSPECTION%20%26%20CLEARANCE%29%20REGULATION%202022.pdf>) · [unbs.go.ug 8](<https://www.unbs.go.ug/attachments/menus/14/Portal.pdf>) · [verigates.bureauveritas.com 9](<https://verigates.bureauveritas.com/sites/verigates/files/2023-07/UGANDA%20%28PVoC%29%20Datasheet%20Rev%2011.1.pdf>) · [ulii.org 10](<https://ulii.org/en/akn/ug/act/2016/6/eng%402023-12-31>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 11](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/ug_2016-6cons2023_ppact_en.pdf>) · [unbs.go.ug 12](<https://unbs.go.ug/news-highlights.php?news=339&read=>) · [media.ulii.org 13](<https://media.ulii.org/media/legislation/107267/source_file/0360de7f6a9a8b96/uganda-national-bureau-of-standards-inspection-and-clearance-of-import-regulations-2022.pdf>) · [media.ulii.org 14](<https://media.ulii.org/media/legislation/107109/source_file/d505729b34f2d023/uganda-national-bureau-of-standards-certification-regulations-2021.pdf>) · [ugandatrades.go.ug 15](<https://ugandatrades.go.ug/media/UNBS%20Certification%20Regulations,%202022%20-%20SI%20No%20108%20of%202022.pdf>)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**
