**Document-rule sources:** [postevaticane.va 1](<https://www.postevaticane.va/script-php/poste/PHPposte-invioPacchi/index.php>) · [guypost.gy 2](<https://guypost.gy/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/ListOfProhibitedArticles-updated.pdf>) · [vaticanstate.va 3](<https://www.vaticanstate.va/phocadownload/laws-decrees/Supplementoleggiedisposizioni.pdf>) · [vatican.va 4](<https://www.vatican.va/roman_curia/secretariat_state/archivio/documents/rc_seg-st_19290211_patti-lateranensi_it.html>) · [postevaticane.va 5](<https://www.postevaticane.va/it/posta-raccomandata>) · [vaticanstate.va 6](<https://www.vaticanstate.va/it/novita/1540-firmato-il-protocollo-di-collaborazione-doganale-tra-governatorato-e-agenzia-delle-dogane-e-dei-monopoli-della-repubblica-italiana.html>) · [def.giustiziatributaria.gov.it 7](<https://def.giustiziatributaria.gov.it/DocTribFrontend/getAttoNormativoDetail.do?ACTION=getArticolo&articolo=Articolo+1&codiceOrdinamento=200000100000000&id=%7B442FDF84-CF15-4F73-9BA4-4CA474515FCD%7D>) · [ulsa.va 8](<https://www.ulsa.va/it/pubblicazioni/bollettini/bollettino-n--16---2008/atti-di-sua-santita-benedetto-xvi/leggi-sulle-fonti-del-diritto-1-ottobre-2008-n-lxxi.html>) · [vaticanstate.va 9](<https://www.vaticanstate.va/en/state-and-government/legislation-and-regulations/general-regulations.html?download=271:n-dclix>) · [adm.gov.it 10](<https://www.adm.gov.it/portale/documents/20182/6907210/Accordo+Italia+Vaticano.pdf/7809007b-38be-4201-85fb-43852a3e9664>) · [adm.gov.it 11](<https://www.adm.gov.it/portale/documents/20182/219876727/27022025++Protocollo+collaborazione+tecnico+amministrativa+ADM+e+il+Governatorato+SCV.pdf/4230441b-bbe0-f5f2-131b-cf90e5b1fdc7?t=1747911929091>) · [adm.gov.it 12](<https://www.adm.gov.it/portale/normativa-internazionale>) · [adm.gov.it 13](<https://www.adm.gov.it/portale/accordi-internazionali>)

Vatican Law LXXI of 1 October 2008 article 3 makes Italian law subsidiary and subject to reception by the competent Vatican authority. Article 12 receives particular subjects, including post and public health, but does not clearly establish the applicable agricultural plant-health rule for inbound Japanese tea. Postal service, duty relief and transit sources therefore do not establish a finished-tea exemption. Actual 2007 and 2025 customs protocols were subsequently recovered: neither establishes an Italian/EU plant-health reception rule; the 2025 protocol expressly preserves the separate legislation and disclaims legally binding international duties.

**Personal post:** Postal documentation confirms the service, but the material found did not address inbound personal tea consignments or identify the agricultural clearance rule.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** The applicable Vatican plant-health rule, or an actual instrument receiving the relevant Italian agricultural rules, remains unestablished. The 2008 sources-of-law statute does not itself establish automatic application of the whole Italian/EU phytosanitary regime.

**Research trail:** Checked Vatican postal customs guidance and the destination’s UPU country return. Searched for applicable Vatican plant-health legislation or an agreement applying Italy’s dried-tea exemption; no authoritative link was established. Rechecked current Vatican postal service terms, the state’s foundational personal-import/customs provisions and Lateran Treaty article 20 on foreign-goods transit. These address service, taxation and transit rather than a tea agricultural exemption; no equivalent SPS agreement or inbound retail-tea rule was located. 30 September final source review: read the official 2025 Vatican–Italy customs cooperation announcement and the 1930 customs convention, including Articles 1, 2, 4 and 8. Duty relief and customs transit including post do not establish an agricultural exemption or application of Italy/EU plant-health rules to direct Vatican personal imports. No applicable public tea classification was found. 30 September bounded remaining-scope review: Recovered official Vatican Law LXXI of 1 October 2008. Article 3 makes Italian law subsidiary and subject to reception by the competent Vatican authority; article 12 lists particular subjects received, including post and public health. Those provisions do not clearly establish which Italian agricultural plant-health law applies to an inbound Japanese tea parcel. Existing postal/customs transit evidence does not fill that gap. 30 September fresh current-regulations recovery: Read complete official Decree DCLIX of 8 May 2024 via the current Governorate portal. Its three articles reorganize customs-service control, budget and staff; it contains no substantive import or Italian plant-law reception provision. This does not close the independent agricultural scope. 30 September additional official primary recovery: downloaded the signed 15 February 2007 Italy–Vatican protocol and attached service instructions from ADM, and the full signed 27 February 2025 protocol with annexes. Visually read the scanned operative pages. The 2007 protocol concerns customs routing, controls, data and implementing instructions; service-instruction Art.1 places advance notices on the Governorate, for shipments above EUR 1,000 or sensitive goods of any value, rather than an identified tea-exporter phytosanitary duty. The 2025 protocol Art.7(1) expressly denies international legally binding effect, Art.7(2) preserves the separate national laws and Italy's EU duties, and its Art.6 annexes concern customs training and cross-border cash declarations. No provision receives the Italian/EU plant-health regime or determines personal tea requirements. Complete sources preserved in french-va-primary-recovery/.

<a id="vc"></a>
### Saint Vincent (VC)

**Conditional personal route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

Plant Protection Act 16 of 2005 section 2 limits plants to living material and manufactured plant products to products whose nature or processing may create pest-introduction/spread risk. Sections 10(2) and 11 attach permits/certificates to specified covered articles rather than all plant-derived food. The manufactured-tea facts and ISPM 32 Category 1 example supply a reasoned factual basis for applying the statutory boundary without a bespoke tea ruling. The regulated-article definition concerns material capable of carrying/spreading pests and deemed to require measures; clean new retail/postal packaging alone is not evidence of such a designation. The 2019 ministry notice applies to covered plant parts/products and is not read to erase section 2. The newly recovered official two-page commodity guidance names planting materials and specific fruits, roots, flowers and vegetables, not tea; it is undated and not treated as an exhaustive current waiver. Section 15 postal reporting and section 12 inspection remain ordinary controls and do not expand the defined commodity class. The actual product is pure Camellia sinensis sencha manufactured by steaming to arrest enzyme activity, repeated rolling/pressing with hot air, and drying, then supplied sealed for infusion. MAFF describes this manufacture. ISPM 32 section 2 and Appendix 2 identify manufactured tea as Category 1, supporting the factual conclusion that this differs from merely dried raw botanical material. The international standard is used as technical evidence when applying the local definition, not as an automatically binding local exemption. Ordinary inspection, food safety and pest freedom remain conditions; this is not a route for raw/sun-dried leaves, seeds, blends or contaminated goods.

**Document-rule sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/stv160536.pdf>) · [importlicensing.wto.org 2](<https://importlicensing.wto.org/content/plant-and-plant-products-2>) · [agriculture.gov.vc 3](<https://agriculture.gov.vc/agriculture/images/stories/AgricInfo/Agrinfo_September_2019.pdf>) · [foreign.gov.vc 4](<https://foreign.gov.vc/foreign/index.php/import-export-guide-content/612-licenses-permits>) · [nationalparks.gov.vc 5](<https://nationalparks.gov.vc/nationalparks/images/documents/EnvironmentalHealthServicesAct1991.pdf>) · [gov.si 6](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastlin/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 7](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>) · [agriculture.gov.vc 8](<https://agriculture.gov.vc/agriculture/images/stories/Agriculture/pdf_documents/1678-importation%20of%20plants%20and%20plants%20products.pdf>)

Plant Protection Act 16 of 2005 section 2 limits plants to living material and manufactured plant products to products whose nature or processing may create pest-introduction/spread risk. Sections 10(2) and 11 attach permits/certificates to specified covered articles rather than all plant-derived food. The manufactured-tea facts and ISPM 32 Category 1 example supply a reasoned factual basis for applying the statutory boundary without a bespoke tea ruling. The regulated-article definition concerns material capable of carrying/spreading pests and deemed to require measures; clean new retail/postal packaging alone is not evidence of such a designation. The 2019 ministry notice applies to covered plant parts/products and is not read to erase section 2. The newly recovered official two-page commodity guidance names planting materials and specific fruits, roots, flowers and vegetables, not tea; it is undated and not treated as an exhaustive current waiver. Section 15 postal reporting and section 12 inspection remain ordinary controls and do not expand the defined commodity class.

**Personal post:** This conclusion follows commodity scope in the applicable local law, not a baggage, gift or noncommercial-seller exception. It applies to the stated private direct postal purchase and clean new packaging.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Use only the stated personal route after verifying all its conditions.

**Sources:** [faolex.fao.org 1](<https://faolex.fao.org/docs/pdf/stv160536.pdf>) · [importlicensing.wto.org 2](<https://importlicensing.wto.org/content/plant-and-plant-products-2>) · [agriculture.gov.vc 3](<https://agriculture.gov.vc/agriculture/images/stories/AgricInfo/Agrinfo_September_2019.pdf>) · [foreign.gov.vc 4](<https://foreign.gov.vc/foreign/index.php/import-export-guide-content/612-licenses-permits>) · [nationalparks.gov.vc 5](<https://nationalparks.gov.vc/nationalparks/images/documents/EnvironmentalHealthServicesAct1991.pdf>) · [foreign.gov.vc 6](<https://foreign.gov.vc/foreign/index.php/import-export-guide-content/618-agencies-involved-in-import-procedures>) · [ippc.int 7](<https://www.ippc.int/en/publications/587/>) · [gov.si 8](<https://www.gov.si/assets/organi-v-sestavi/UVHVVR/Zdravje-rastlin/IZVOZ-rastlin/mednarodni-standardi-ISPM/ISPM_32_2009_En_2015-12-22_PostCPM10_InkAmReformatted.pdf>) · [maff.go.jp 9](<https://www.maff.go.jp/j/pr/aff/2204/spe1_02.html>) · [agriculture.gov.vc 10](<https://agriculture.gov.vc/agriculture/images/stories/Agriculture/pdf_documents/1678-importation%20of%20plants%20and%20plants%20products.pdf>)

Excluded the draft Food Security and Sustainability Act from operative-law evidence. 2026-09-30: Read and saved the official 2026 consolidated legislative index, pages 120 and 184; checked all five revised-law collection pages and targeted Cap. 93/1996-regulation searches. Confirmed the 2022 Act remains uncommenced, rather than treating assent as commencement. The exact older operative schedules are indexed but not supplied by the current online revised-law collection; no publicly verified tea classification found. 2026-09-30: Final targeted searches for Cap.93 Plant Protection Ordinance, the 1991 subsidiary regulations and 2002 amendments found no new public operative commodity schedule beyond the already saved January 2026 official index. The missing evidence is the actual current schedule text or a finished-tea NPPO classification; current nursery guidance cannot resolve it. No authority contact made. 2026-09-30 fresh official online recovery: Recovered the current official Fumigation of Plants Act, revised 1 January 2023. Section 2 expressly includes parcel post in import, but plant scope concerns living material and fruit/vegetables intended for propagation rather than food; section 4 allows fumigation. This instrument supplies no general private finished-tea certificate duty and cannot replace the still-missing operative Cap.93 commodity schedules. Saved vg-fumigation.pdf and extracted full text. 30 September renewed official-source/general-definition review: A bounded exact-title search for the operative Plant Protection Ordinance and 1991/1996/2002 subsidiary instruments produced the already-known legislative index and the uncommenced 2022 Food Security legislation, rather than the missing operative schedules. No further requests to the previously exhausted revised-law pages were made. Current portal states digitization is a work in progress. Also checked exact 1941 Cap 93/1991 subsidiary leads through official national/UKarchive search; recovered only official policy summaries, not operative text. This confirms access gap, not absence of legislation. January 2026 index remains affirmative evidence the Act/regulations exist; no new commodity conclusion.

<a id="vi"></a>
### US Virgin Islands (VI)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

No advance plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate requirement identified for the finished-tea route; comply with ordinary postal/customs declaration, any published personal quantity limit, and arrival inspection. APHIS expressly authorizes dried Camellia sinensis tea without plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate; all consignments remain subject to arrival inspection. Current 7 CFR 319.7 expressly includes Puerto Rico and the US Virgin Islands within the United States for these import provisions. FDA Q&A 10.2 expressly confirms no FDA prior notice for imports into the US Virgin Islands (different from Puerto Rico).

APHIS expressly authorizes dried Camellia sinensis tea without plant import permit or phytosanitary certificate; all consignments remain subject to arrival inspection. Current 7 CFR 319.7 expressly includes Puerto Rico and the US Virgin Islands within the United States for these import provisions.

### Wallis and Futuna (WF)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Sencha’s processing and ISPM 32 support the factual manufactured/nonliving classification. However, the explicit loose/bulk tea permit-only provision makes WF different from a jurisdiction with only a generic risk definition. A sealed retail pouch does not prove loose leaves are outside the tea-in-bulk expression. Traveller allowances do not settle mail. Current binding certificate scope and relation between the specific tea-in-bulk provision and unmanufactured-product definition; full current territorial plant-health consolidation or a published general manufactured-food postal rule. No contrary certificate exemption was established.

**Research trail:** Read SIVAP import/declaration guidance and the referenced local phytosanitary regime. Read the official traveller sheet and distinguished its exemptions from postal imports. Searched for tea-specific postal and processed-food conditions; no applicable affirmative rule found. Read indexed official 2026 AIP GEN 1.4-4, section 1.4.2, which explicitly covers goods and mail. Its tea-in-bulk exception removes only the permit, not the certificate; finished retail tea’s classification remains ambiguous. Checked the phytosanitary arrival-form update effective January 2026; it is expressly handed to airline passengers and provides no postal certificate exemption. Searched for current local Order 93-275 amendments or a retail-tea postal clarification; no applicable published clarification was located. 30 September bounded legal-scope peer review: The current accessible AIP section includes mail. Its French wording covers living plants/parts and non-manufactured plant products, then expressly exempts tea in bulk from the import permit alone. It does not waive the phytosanitary certificate for that category. A sealed retail pouch does not by itself prove that loose tea is outside the tea-in-bulk expression; conversely, the specific tea exception makes it unsafe to infer that every manufactured tea is entirely outside scope. The retail/manufactured distinction remains unresolved in this text. Traveller sheets use an arrival declaration and cannot independently supply a postal waiver. 30 September independent definition review recovered SPREP 2022 full comparative report; existing-law column reproduces 95-245 Arts 2–3 and default prior-permit waiver but no certificate waiver. Proposed new biosafety sections were not treated as enacted. Official Gazette 714, May 2025 indexedtext calls further import/export sections future proposals. Actual sencha manufacture and ISPM 32 are favorable factual evidence; specific tea-in-bulk permit-only entry still prevents clean postal certificate exclusion. See wf-pm-definition-review/review-notes.json.

### Samoa (WS)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

<a id="xk"></a>
### Kosovo (XK)

**Allowed under the stated conditions.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route supported, subject to conditions.

The current complete certificate annex expressly limits tea controls to fresh leaves, excluding steamed/dried sencha. The general non-animal-food control regulation provides risk-based checks and document checks, not a blanket certificate for every processed food. No tea-specific special approval requirement was identified for this personal-use parcel; ordinary postal/customs declaration remains required.

**Document-rule sources:** [gzk.rks-gov.net 1](<https://gzk.rks-gov.net/ActDetail.aspx?ActID=106863>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=1961&menuid=100&reporeid=396>) · [gzk.rks-gov.net 3](<https://gzk.rks-gov.net/ActDocumentDetail.aspx?ActID=10544>)

Legal classification inference: Law 04/L-120 Annex V Part B, as amended by Administrative Instruction 05/2025, regulates ex 090210/ex 090220 only as fresh, whole, uncut, unfermented tea leaves. Manufactured steamed/dried retail sencha does not meet that description and therefore is outside the certificate-controlled tea entry.

**Personal post:** This is a commodity-based import rule, applying to postal consignments as well as cargo. No veterinary or traveller allowance is relied on.

**Checkout:** 166 pouches / 16.6 kg tea; highest carrier band 20 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Agricultural route supported for the scoped product; comply with the stated packaging, declaration and personal-use conditions.

**Sources:** [gzk.rks-gov.net 1](<https://gzk.rks-gov.net/ActDetail.aspx?ActID=106863>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?downloadid=1961&menuid=100&reporeid=396>) · [gzk.rks-gov.net 3](<https://gzk.rks-gov.net/ActDocumentDetail.aspx?ActID=10544>)

### Mayotte (YT)

**Unresolved — no verified import route.**

**Store decision:** No-special-documents route unresolved.

Existing 2025 schedule is genuinely favorable to dried tea, but the binding 2019 postal restriction remains separate and the current 2026-DAAF-236 amendment/consolidation has not been retrieved. A general processed-product postal exclusion could settle the category; a bespoke sencha ruling is not intrinsically required. No repeated retrieval of exhausted YT routes was attempted in this peer review. Remaining gap: Current 2026 amended annex and exact reconciliation of the 2019 postal text with the DAAF unprocessed-product description.

**Document-rule sources:** [daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr 1](<https://daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/ap-2025-daaf-0230_importation_vegetaux_signe.pdf>) · [mayotte.gouv.fr 2](<https://www.mayotte.gouv.fr/Actualites/Communiques-de-presse/Communiques-de-presse-2026/Quoi-mettre-dans-nos-valises-cet-ete>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 3](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/yt_2019-1092_post-gepaeck_fr.pdf>) · [daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr 4](<https://daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr/importation-des-vegetaux-aout-2025-a733.html>) · [daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr 5](<https://daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/arrete_no2025-daaf-535_modifiant_l_arrete_2025-daaf-0230.pdf>) · [daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr 6](<https://daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr/importation-des-vegetaux-2026-a775.html>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 7](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/index.php?menuid=28&downloadid=3229&reporeid=140>) · [daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr 8](<https://daaf.mayotte.agriculture.gouv.fr/IMG/pdf/presentation_copsav_-_v_finale.pdf>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 9](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-340>) · [info.agriculture.gouv.fr 10](<https://info.agriculture.gouv.fr/boagri/instruction-2026-340/telechargement>) · [mayotte.gouv.fr 11](<https://www.mayotte.gouv.fr/Actualites/Communiques-de-presse/Communiques-de-presse-2025/Introduction-de-vegetaux-suite-au-passage-du-cyclone-CHIDO>)

Mayotte Order 2025-DAAF-0230 excludes dried tea from the Camellia species prohibition; its chapter 09 documentary schedule names unroasted coffee and fresh pepper, not ordinary tea. That does not establish postal admissibility. The separate 2019-1092 postal order describes fresh plant material, including refrigerated or dry material and plant parts; current public guidance does not expressly resolve manufactured tea. The latest 2026 consolidation also needs verification.

**Personal post:** The available sources do not establish whether manufactured steamed/dried food tea falls outside the separate postal restriction. A general current processed-product exclusion could settle this without a bespoke official ruling; the old general dried-tea schedule alone does not establish the postal route.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Not cleared for dispatch under the store no-permits/no-certificates constraint; review remains open.

**To resolve:** Current 2026 amended annex and exact reconciliation of the 2019 postal text with the DAAF unprocessed-product description.

Re-read the primary 2019 postal order from the German NPPO mirror: Article 1 says fresh material including refrigerated or dry material and plant parts, Article 3 research derogation is not a private-consumer waiver. Checked current DAAF June 2026 index and May 2026 amendment index; attempted both official pages and amendment retrieval repeatedly, without success. 30 September: located DAAF’s 2025 COPSAV presentation, page 11, describing the 2019 postal ban as applying to unprocessed products. This narrows the issue but is not a manufactured-tea ruling and does not replace the unavailable 2026 amended schedule. Both direct official-page and PDF retrieval timed out; the slide was read through its indexed text. 30 September final source-recovery attempt: current DAAF index still lists amendment 2026-DAAF-236 and the consolidated order. Canonical download, HTTP and SPIP article routes timed out; the alternative official prefecture April–May 2026 RAA archive also closed connections. Indexed issue titles and the JKI link to older rules do not disclose the current amended annex or settle the tea/postal interaction. 30 September bounded legal-scope peer review: Existing 2025 schedule is genuinely favorable to dried tea, but the binding 2019 postal restriction remains separate and the current 2026-DAAF-236 amendment/consolidation has not been retrieved. A general processed-product postal exclusion could settle the category; a bespoke sencha ruling is not intrinsically required. No repeated retrieval of exhausted YT routes was attempted in this peer review. 30 September fresh current-source recovery: official indexed DGAL/SDPRS/2026-340 record confirms an in-force 2026 inspection instruction, and its indexed PDF cites Mayotte amendments 2025-0288 and 2026-236. Full instruction download timed out. A new data.gouv locator supplied 161 exact official Mayotte 2026 Gazette URLs; direct May issues 080/090/094 failed with HTTP/2 errors, with an HTTP/1.1 follow-up returning an empty reply. This materially different official-document path did not recover the annex. <a id="za"></a>
### South Africa (ZA)

**Permit / documented clearance route.**

**Store decision:** Incompatible with the no-special-documents constraint.

The published Agricultural Pests Act section 3 permit requirement covers dead plant parts and derivatives. The complete R 1013 statutory exemptions contain no Camellia/Thea food-tea exemption. Kenya’s official tea export market guidance independently lists a South African import permit for tea. A separately issued dispensation would itself be special approval, which the store excludes.

**Document-rule sources:** [nda.gov.za 1](<https://www.nda.gov.za/images/Services/Agricultural%20Pests%20Act.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/za_1989-1013cons2022-2855_einfuhr_de.pdf>) · [infotradekenya.go.ke 3](<https://infotradekenya.go.ke/menu/899>)

Legal inference from the current Agricultural Pests Act: its definition of plant includes dead parts and derivatives, and section 3 requires an import permit unless an exemption applies. The complete R 1013 exemption table, consolidated through 2022, lists food herbs by genus but contains no Camellia/Thea exemption. Treat manufactured sencha as requiring a plant import permit; obtain the consignment’s certificate and inspection conditions through that permit.

**Personal post:** The Act applies to imports by any person and does not create a general small personal-post exemption. A permit route is not evidence that the recipient already holds a permit or that a particular application will be approved.

**Checkout:** 249 pouches / 24.9 kg tea; highest carrier band 30 kg gross. No numeric agricultural/personal threshold was verified; the carrier maximum is not a personal-use allowance.

**Dispatch:** Do not dispatch under the store constraint. The published route requires the additional regulatory step explained below.

**To resolve:** Confirm with the permit office whether it has a separate processing-specific dispensation for commercially steamed/dried retail Camellia sinensis tea; none was found in the published statutory exemption table. The issued permit must establish the required Japanese phytosanitary documentation.

**Sources:** [nda.gov.za 1](<https://www.nda.gov.za/images/Services/Agricultural%20Pests%20Act.pdf>) · [pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de 2](<https://pflanzengesundheit.julius-kuehn.de/dokumente/upload/za_1989-1013cons2022-2855_einfuhr_de.pdf>) · [nda.gov.za 3](<https://www.nda.gov.za/component/content/article/299-import-authorisation?Itemid=437&catid=19>) · [gov.za 4](<https://www.gov.za/documents/acts/plant-health-phytosanitary-act-35-2024-20-dec-2024>)
